# wechart tasks

Inputs: [`docs/spec.md`](./spec.md) (180 acceptance criteria, F1–F21), [`docs/research/`](./research/), [`docs/design/direction.md`](./design/direction.md).

185 tasks. Every AC is covered by at least one task, and every task cites at least one AC; the coverage table at the end lists all 180. Tasks are ordered by dependency first, then by user value. `[P]` means the task depends on no unfinished task at the point it appears, so it can run in parallel with its neighbours.

**Format.** Each task line follows the shape CLAUDE.md defines, with `Files`, `Failing test` and `Depends on` sub-bullets. `[P]` marks a task whose dependencies are already met where it appears.

**Order.** Task numbers group the work by feature; the real order is the `Depends on` graph, which is acyclic. Thirteen tasks depend on a higher-numbered task (for example T023 on T071, T088 on T113) — always take the next unchecked task whose dependencies are met, never the next number.

**Working rules.** One task, one observable behaviour, one commit, message `T00N: <title> (AC-…)`. Write the failing test first, confirm it fails for the right reason, then the code. Tick an AC in `docs/spec.md` only when a passing test demonstrates it.

**`--passWithNoTests` is temporary.** The scaffold gives each package a `test` script before it has tests, so the flag keeps `pnpm test` green. It also turns a mistyped include glob into a silent pass, so drop it from a package's `test` script in the same commit as that package's first test. Scaffold tasks cite the ACs they first make testable.

**Package layout** (created by T001):

| Package                | Contents                                                                                   |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `packages/core`        | chart spec, marking model, filter model, canonical Cube query builder — no DOM, no React   |
| `packages/api`         | Fastify service: Cube proxy, metadata persistence, jobs, connection tests                  |
| `packages/web`         | Vite + React app: shell, Explore, chart editor, dashboards, model editor, admin workspaces |
| `packages/charts`      | ECharts renderer for the chart spec, plus the marking/hit-test layer                       |
| `packages/ui`          | in-house primitives and design tokens                                                      |
| `packages/recommender` | legality rules and weighted scoring                                                        |
| `e2e`                  | Playwright acceptance tests                                                                |

---

Every package declares `test` and `typecheck`; the TypeScript configs exclude `.claude/`.

A local run covers Chrome, Firefox and Safari. Root `test` runs the package suites only; `pnpm e2e` runs the acceptance suite, so the Stop hook is not gated on browsers that may be absent.

One versioned `documents` table rather than five per-kind repositories: AC-014 names five kinds but asks only that each is stored and returns unchanged, and AC-015 asks that a stale save is refused. Neither needs per-kind columns, and inventing a relational shape for behaviour no AC describes would be guesswork. Tests run against a stub Cube over real HTTP; the real Cube joins docker-compose at T010/T024, where metadata correctness is the point rather than what wechart sends.

AC-002 ticked: 12 cases, each of which navigates, **reloads**, and asserts the surface and selected object came back — reload is the criterion, so none asserts only that a link works. Workspace routes are generated from `WORKSPACES`, so a workspace cannot exist in the switcher and not the router.

## F2. wechart service: Cube access, metadata and persistence

- [x] T010 (AC-009) Serve metadata from the new model within 60 seconds of a `schemaVersion` change
  - Files: `packages/api/src/cube/meta-cache.ts`, `packages/api/src/cube/meta-cache.test.ts`, `packages/api/src/server.ts`
  - Failing test: `meta-cache.test.ts`: with a fake clock, a changed `schemaVersion` causes the next metadata request after at most 60 s to return the new model's members, with no process restart (AC-009).
  - Depends on: T004
  - Done 2026-09-19. AC-009 ticked. Research correction: `schemaVersion` is a Cube **config hook** (`fn(ctx) => string`, called per request, recompiling when the string changes — `research/cube/configuration-deployment.md` §3), not something Cube pushes to clients. So the 60-second bound is wechart's own: a cached entry older than that is refetched before being served.
  - Caching is what makes the bound necessary. Without it metadata is never stale, but a ten-tile dashboard becomes ten `/v1/meta` requests against a queue whose default concurrency is 2. Concurrent callers share one in-flight fetch for the same reason.
  - `invalidate()` skips the bound for wechart's own publishes (T142/T143): waiting out 60 seconds is the wrong answer to "did my change take effect?". Failures are never cached.
- [x] T011 (AC-010) Hold the request open while Cube replies "Continue wait"
  - Files: `packages/web/src/query/{loadTile,useTileData}.ts`, `packages/web/src/query/{loadTile,useTileData}.test.ts|tsx`
  - Failing test: `loadTile.test.ts`: two "Continue wait" responses followed by a result leave the tile in its loading state throughout and then render the result, with no error state (AC-010).
  - Depends on: T004
  - Done 2026-09-19. AC-010 ticked. Cube answers a still-running query with HTTP **200** and a body of `{"error":"Continue wait"}` — an error-shaped body that is not an error. Trusting the status code renders an empty tile; trusting the word "error" shows a red tile for a query that is merely slow. Both are the failure this criterion names, so the sentinel is matched explicitly and a test asserts the tile never passes through an error state on the way.
  - The request id is minted once and carried across the whole poll, so Cube sees one query rather than a series — and so T012 has something to name when it cancels.
  - The poll is bounded (`maxPolls`), because a tile that hangs forever is its own failure. Retries are off in the hook: a Cube error is something the tile shows (AC-012), and backoff would hide it and spend AC-175's budget.
- [x] T012 (AC-011) Cancel a superseded query against Cube before rendering the newer result
  - Files: `packages/web/src/query/loadTile.ts`, `packages/web/src/query/cancel.test.ts`, `packages/api/src/cube/client.ts`, `packages/api/src/server.ts`, `packages/api/src/server.test.ts`
  - Failing test: `cancel.test.ts`: changing a tile's filters aborts the in-flight fetch and issues `DELETE /v1/running-query/{requestId}` for it, and the older result never replaces the newer one (AC-011).
  - Depends on: T011
  - Done 2026-09-19. AC-011 ticked. **Dropping the response is not cancelling** — the criterion says "against Cube". An abandoned query keeps running and holds one of the queue's two default slots until its orphan timeout, so a user dragging a filter slider can starve their own dashboard. The abort listener fires `DELETE /api/running-query/{id}`, which the service forwards to Cube.
  - A test asserts the cancellation names the id the load actually used: one naming a fresh id stops nothing, and would pass a weaker assertion.
  - The route always answers 204, including when Cube says the query is unknown. The tile that issued it is already gone and a failed cancellation is not something anyone can act on; the warning goes to the log.
- [x] T013 (AC-012) Show a tile error state carrying Cube's message and the query JSON
  - Files: `packages/web/src/tiles/TileError.tsx`, `packages/web/src/tiles/TileError.test.tsx`
  - Failing test: `TileError.test.tsx`: a Cube error renders the message and a control that reveals the exact query JSON sent, instead of an empty chart (AC-012).
  - Depends on: T011
  - Done 2026-09-19. AC-012 ticked. Cube's message is shown **verbatim** rather than translated into something friendlier: "Can't find join path between Orders and Suppliers" tells an author what to change, where "Something went wrong" tells them to file a ticket. A test asserts the original message survives.
  - The query JSON is one control away — the other half of diagnosing a failure, but noise while the message is enough. The state pairs an icon with a label, never colour alone.
- [x] T014 (AC-013) Mark a tile accelerated with a data age, or live
  - Files: `packages/web/src/tiles/AccelerationBadge.tsx`, `packages/web/src/tiles/AccelerationBadge.test.tsx`
  - Failing test: `AccelerationBadge.test.tsx`: a response with `usedPreAggregations` renders the accelerated badge and the data age; a source-database response renders the live badge (AC-013).
  - Depends on: T011
  - Done 2026-09-19. AC-013 ticked. Both directions are asserted, because both mislead: labelling everything "live" hides that a number is an hour old, and labelling everything "accelerated" claims a freshness guarantee that is not there (requirements §4 — volumes vary widely, so the UI has to say which queries are accelerated).
  - Cube sends `usedPreAggregations` **with no entries** for a source-database query, so its presence proves nothing; only a non-empty set means accelerated. A test pins that case, since checking for the key alone would label every tile accelerated.
  - **Put on the real tiles (2026-09-21).** The badge was ticked from its component test, but nothing rendered it: no tile imported `AccelerationBadge`. Found while building T154, whose data-as-of badge sits in the same head. Files:
    - Web:
      - `query/answerMeta.ts` (`answerMetaOf`) and its test. What Cube says of an answer besides its rows is `lastRefreshTime` and `usedPreAggregations`. It is carried as one `answered` record wherever the answer goes, replacing T154's `asOf`: the page's load, a refresh, a filtered answer, and a save with drafts.
      - `ChartTile.tsx` (`served`) draws the badge in the head, before the data-as-of time.
      - The record's path through the dashboard: `DashboardPage.tsx`, `DashboardView.tsx` (its filtered overlay included), `filters/FilteredCharts.tsx`, and `edit/chartDrafts.ts` with its test. `dashboard/refreshTile.test.tsx` checks that a filtered tile's badge is its filtered answer's.
      - `AccelerationBadge.tsx` names the pre-aggregation by the model's `preAggregationId`, not its Cube Store table.
      - `shell/styles.ts`: the prototype's `.badge`, in the success colour when accelerated.
    - The Cube stack:
      - `docker-compose.yml`: a `cube-refresh-worker` service with the same image and configuration as the API instance, plus `CUBEJS_REFRESH_WORKER=true`.
      - `infra/cube/cube.js`: `scheduledRefreshContexts`, covering the mounted model and each trusted store's.
      - A seed cube, `infra/cube/model/cubes/monthly_orders.yml`. It has a rollup of count and total by status and month, partitioned by year, with an index on status. It is a cube of its own with no joins, so nothing else the sample model answers changes. It gives F16 a rollup to list, rebuild and index.
    - **Why the worker.** Observed locally: in production mode, a Cube API instance with no refresh worker answers HTTP 400 to any query a rollup would serve until the rollup is built ("this API instance wasn't set up to build pre-aggregations"). With the worker, the rollup was built 24–30 s after start. Recorded in `research/cube/pre-aggregations.md`, "Observed locally".
    - **Review fixes (2026-09-21, verdict PASS):**
      - Written out, the two badges left a half-width tile's title one letter wide at 1280 px, and pushed "Table view" under the corner controls. They are now shown as the prototype's tile badge is (`✓ 3m` in `prototype/dashboard.js`): "⚡ 16m" and "as of 11:01:13 PM". The whole sentences are the badges' text for a screen reader, and their tooltips; the tooltip also names the rollup. `e2e/acceleration.spec.ts` now checks the title keeps at least 96 px and "Table view" ends before ↻.
      - A test that a filtered tile's badge comes from its filtered answer (above).
      - Stale notes corrected (T137's note about the seed model, and the badge's comment on an absent `usedPreAggregations`).
      - Left as found: the relative age is worked out when the tile draws, so "16m" doesn't count up on an open dashboard. The absolute time beside it stays true. The Jobs log names a rollup by its table, where the tile uses the model's name.
    - AC-013 stays ticked, now on the real surface: `e2e/acceleration.spec.ts`, against the real Cube in all three browsers. A `monthly_orders` tile reads "Accelerated · built … ago", with its icon and the rollup's model name. An `orders` tile reads "Live" and not "Accelerated". The test first waits for the worker to have built the rollup.
- [x] T015 (AC-016) Record every query wechart issues and list the records in the Jobs workspace
  - Files: `packages/api/src/repos/query-log.ts`, `packages/api/src/repos/query-log.test.ts`, `packages/api/src/db/migrations/1758240001000_query-log.cjs`, `packages/api/src/db/schema.ts`, `packages/api/src/server.ts`, `packages/api/src/server.test.ts`, `packages/web/src/workspaces/jobs/QueryLog.tsx`, `packages/web/src/workspaces/jobs/QueryLog.test.tsx`
  - Failing test: `query-log.test.ts`: rendering a chart produces one record holding the Cube request, the duration and the acceleration flag, and the Jobs endpoint returns it (AC-016).
  - Depends on: T005, T011
  - Done 2026-09-19. AC-016 ticked. **Failed queries are recorded too.** A query that ran for four seconds and then errored is exactly what someone opens the Jobs workspace to find; recording only successes would make it a log of what worked rather than of what the system did.
  - Recording never fails a chart that loaded. A test asserts a load still returns 200 when the log write throws — observability that can break the thing it observes is worse than none.
  - Its own table, not `documents`: this is append-only and read by recency, so the only index is `created_at DESC` (`research/db-access.md`).
  - Still to wire: the Jobs **route** renders `QueryLog` against `/api/jobs/queries`. The component and the endpoint both exist and are tested; joining them belongs with the Jobs surface work at T155–T158.
  - **Wired 2026-09-21, found while building T137:** AC-016 had been ticked on the component's and the endpoint's tests alone. The Jobs workspace rendered nothing, which is exactly the rule in `ac-ticks-need-the-real-surface`.
    - `workspaces/jobs/JobsPage.tsx` fetches `/api/jobs/queries`, refetching when the workspace opens, and renders `QueryLog`. It is mounted as the Jobs workspace's content.
    - `e2e/jobs.spec.ts` (1 in each browser): open the seeded dashboard, then `/jobs` lists an `orders.count` query with its duration and source. It failed first, with no Jobs heading, then passed.

## F3. Explore: folders, catalog and search

- [x] T016 (AC-017) Show the folder tree and the selected folder's contents
  - Files: `packages/web/src/workspaces/explore/{FolderTree,FolderContents}.tsx`, `packages/api/src/routes/folders.ts`, `packages/web/src/workspaces/explore/FolderContents.test.tsx`
  - Failing test: `FolderContents.test.tsx`: every dashboard and library chart in the folder is listed with its type, owner and last-updated time, and no column is blank for an item that has that value (AC-017).
  - Depends on: T003, T005
  - Done 2026-09-19. **AC-017 and AC-003 both ticked.** The scroll half of AC-003, deferred from T007, is now in `e2e/explore.spec.ts` against a seeded list of 34 items; it asserts the page really scrolled before asserting the offset survives, so it cannot pass on a page that never moved.
  - **AC-003 genuinely failed the first time.** Not because the theme switch lost the scroll — nothing unmounted, the document height and row count were identical — but because the top bar scrolled away, so clicking the theme control made the browser scroll it back into view. The fix is a sticky bar, which a workbench wants regardless: the switcher, search and theme stay reachable however far a surface has scrolled.
  - Columns for the listing are **generated** from `body` in Postgres rather than written beside it, so a rename cannot leave the listing stale; a test covers exactly that.
  - Two infrastructure changes this task forced: the API now builds with Vite and runs as its own process in `playwright.config.ts` (Node's `--experimental-strip-types` does no module resolution, so it wants an explicit `.ts` on every relative import); and **the tests now use their own database**. Sharing one with development let test fixtures into the catalog the app listed, and the dev seed then saw folders nobody had created and skipped itself.
- [x] T017 (AC-019) Search dashboards and library charts by title, excluding dashboard-local charts
  - **Reopened 2026-09-24, and AC-019 unticked.** The criterion's trigger is "the user types in **the Explore search box**", and Explore has no search box — the user found it by comparing the running app with the prototype, which has one ("Search dashboards and charts", beside the folder title) *as well as* the global ⌘K search in the top bar. The assignment therefore lives in `useDashboardColours` and is carried across renders. `colours.test.ts` asserts that the naive version *is* wrong, so the fix cannot be quietly reverted.
  - Rejected on the way: an `onTileSpec` callback on `DashboardView` so a test could observe what each tile was handed. That is a prop that exists only for a test. Extracting the hook instead made the across-renders behaviour — the part a pure function cannot express — directly testable.
  - The e2e reads the two tiles' **legends** and maps member → fill by geometry, because zrender emits a flat SVG with no group per legend entry. Comparing two lists of fills by position would pass whatever the colours meant, which is the bug itself.
  - **The first version of that e2e proved nothing, and the note here claimed otherwise.** Both seeded charts were grouped by a plain dimension, so Cube returned their statuses in the same order — and colour falls back to first-appearance order when a tile chooses for itself, so rank-based colouring painted them identically. The test asserted a tautology. The claim that the two charts "list their statuses in opposite order" was read off DOM traversal order, not the data, and was false. The cold review caught it and checked the queries against the running Cube.
  - Fixed by making the pair genuinely differ: one chart groups by category, the other runs over time, and the sample orders cycle through the statuses day by day, so the two reach them in different sequences. The test now **asserts that premise** — if the two legends ever agree on order, it fails there rather than going quietly hollow — and the wiring is mutation-verified: passing the raw specs instead of the assignment makes a member paint `#1baf7a` in one tile and `#eb6834` in the other.
  - Worth remembering: the mutation appeared to pass at first because `playwright.config.ts` reuses an already-running preview server, which was serving an older build. A mutation test against a stale bundle proves nothing. Kill the servers, or let Playwright build.
  - AC-054's e2e resolves the painted period against the measured canvas: the browser keeps it as `calc(8.33333% + 1.33333px)`, since a percentage cannot resolve before layout, and that comes to 79.0 px against the 932 px canvas — the column pitch exactly.
  - Two things the seed had to gain: a dashboard with a real structure (two page tabs, a nested section, two charts) and the two charts themselves. Both split by `orders.status`, so the same members appear in both tiles and AC-039 has something to be true about. The first attempt wrote the charts' wells as encoding channels (`x`/`y`/`color`); a chart spec's wells are `measures`/`dimensions`/`timeDimensions`, and the charts rendered as missing until that was fixed — caught only by the e2e.
  - A failed chart and an absent chart are kept apart. The first cut caught everything into one `null` and drew the missing-chart state for both; a query Cube refused then looked like a chart someone had deleted. Now a load failure carries its message to `TileError` (AC-012) and only a genuinely missing chart says so. Both have a test.
  - `DashboardView` is keyed by the dashboard id, so moving between dashboards starts a new edit session instead of carrying the previous one's history into a document that is no longer on screen.
  - **Save does nothing yet** (AC-065, T071), and edit mode has no way out. That is coherent for now because nothing is editable until T061 onwards, so there is nothing for Save to lose. T071 must check it: see its entry.
  - Deferred deliberately, each named so the next task does not read it as settled: `TileBody`'s `divider` and the image/link/filter branch have no test and no seeded tile (they belong to AC-063, AC-064 and F8); `TabGroupView` renders outside any `Canvas` and ignores the group's `w`, so a tab group occupies no columns (AC-058, AC-059); the stored dashboard body is cast rather than parsed, where a chart body goes through strict `readChartDocument` (close it at T071, when the write path exists); and all tiles load under one query, so the slowest gates the dashboard and no `AbortSignal` reaches `loadTile`, which puts AC-011's cancellation out of reach from a dashboard — the per-tile structure AC-010/011/012/042 assume has to be built for dashboard filters anyway (F8).
  - Also left alone: `GET /api/documents/:kind/:id` answers 400 when persistence is unconfigured, where 503 is the right class. It matches how every other route in `server.ts` reports the same condition, so it is fixed there or not at all.
  - **Also ticks AC-039.** The colour mechanism is built and tested at T046, but the criterion is about "every tile of a dashboard" and this is the first place several tiles are rendered together. Share one assignment across them and assert a member matches in two tiles at once.
  - **Also ticks AC-054.** The grid and the snapping are built and unit-tested at T060, but "show a 12-column grid on the canvas" is painted in CSS and nothing renders the canvas until now. The e2e must read `getComputedStyle` on the canvas in edit mode, the way `e2e/theme.spec.ts` and `e2e/a11y-tokens.spec.ts` check painted values — a `data-` attribute is the switch, not the painting.
  - **Also brought forward, ahead of its recorded dependency on T071.** That dependency was for a dashboard to open; T071 is the *write* path and the read path already exists, so the seed supplies one. AC-018 is about opening a saved dashboard, not about saving it, and the round trip is re-verified at T071. Doing it here is what lets F7's criteria be verified as they land instead of all at the end — the failure mode that left AC-012's `TileError` written, tested and never rendered until T049.
  - **Also ticks AC-053.** The edit bar and sidebar are built and unit-tested at T059, but nothing renders them until a dashboard exists. Render `EditChrome` around the dashboard here and add an e2e that opens a saved dashboard, activates Edit, and finds Undo, Redo, Discard, Save, Add, Outline and Properties. `EditChrome` must be mounted with the loaded dashboard, or keyed by its id, so a route change from one dashboard to another re-seeds the session.

  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-025 and AC-007 both ticked.** AC-007 waited from T004 until the browser genuinely needed metadata: the test asserts the page *did* fetch `/api/meta`, then that nothing went to the Cube host and no response carried the secret. Without the first assertion the other two pass on a page that fetches nothing.
  - The real stack is now in `docker-compose.yml`: a seeded sample warehouse, Cube Store, and Cube 1.7.42 in **production mode** (no Playground, authentication enforced), so local development matches how it is deployed. The JWT the `CubeClient` mints authenticates against real Cube, which is AC-008 confirmed against the thing rather than a stub.
  - Two findings. **Cube talks to Cube Store on HTTP 3030, not the MySQL port 3306** — pointing it at 3306 fails with "Parse Error: Expected HTTP/" and a permanently unhealthy deployment. And Cube Store's image is per-architecture: `v1.7.42` is amd64-only, Apple Silicon needs `-arm64v8`, so the tag is an env var CI overrides.
  - **Time dimensions are not a separate array** in Cube's metadata — they are dimensions whose `type` is `time`. Listing them with the rest would bury the field most charts are built around, so they are their own group.
  - Also fixed here: the intermittent browser-suite failures seen since T017 were **browser context launch exceeding the 30s test timeout** on cold runs, not assertion failures — the app was never at fault. The per-test timeout is now 60s, verified over a forced cold run and a warm one.

Refusing is implemented by *not* calling `preventDefault` on `dragover`, which is what makes a target decline in HTML drag and drop.

Rule names are ported from `docs/research/recommender-algorithms.md` §2; the four caps are recorded there as product decisions.

  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-030 and AC-031 stay unchecked**: both are stated over what is *offered* ("never recommend", "offered as the top pick or a runner-up"), and nothing recommends yet. The rules provably reject, and T028 — which produces the recommendations — asserts no offered candidate violates them. That is the criterion's actual shape.
  - Every cap is tested **at the boundary in both directions**: accepted at the cap, rejected one past it. A rule written with the wrong comparison passes a test that only tries a wildly-over case.
  - The heatmap rule counts **cells**, not either axis: 15×15 is over the cap though neither side looks large.
  - The four caps live in one `CAPS` object with the provenance stated beside them — the research found no published maximum for any of them, so they are product decisions rather than literature.
  - `checkLegality` returns every rule's verdict, passed or failed, because AC-029's trace has to show the rules that *passed* too.
- [x] T028 (AC-028, AC-030, AC-031) Recommend a chart type from the field signature, with at least one runner-up and a reason
  - Files: `packages/recommender/src/{recommend,legality,index}.ts`, `packages/recommender/src/{recommend,legality}.test.ts`
  - Failing test: `recommend.test.ts`: a field change produces a top pick plus at least one runner-up, each with a reason of at most 80 characters (AC-028).
  - Depends on: T027
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-028, AC-030 and AC-031 all ticked.** The two carried from T027 are demonstrated the way they are written — over what is *offered* — by running eleven signatures through the recommender and asserting nothing it returns has two value axes or exceeds a channel cap.
  - Enumerate, filter, rank, the shape the research recommends. The **weights are ours**: Draco's shipped weights are hand-tuned rather than learned and its design space has no pie, KPI or table, so they could not be ported wholesale (research §7). What is ported is the *shape* — a cost summed from named soft rules, lower being better — plus the channel ordering from Draco-APT and CompassQL.
  - Runners-up are a different chart type from the winner, not the same chart again: an alternative should be another way of seeing the data, which is what Voyager's grouping achieves (research §3).
  - **A decision the tests forced into the open**: a field whose cardinality nobody knows is treated as *over* any cap, not under it. The guesses are not symmetric — assuming small offers a pie that then draws five hundred slices, while assuming large offers a bar for a category that turns out to have four values: a worse chart, not a broken one. `legality.ts` and `recommend.ts` had silently disagreed about this, and a test caught it.

  - **A failed refetch cannot unmount a loaded editor.** `editorViewOf` shows the editor whenever there is a chart, and the error only when there is none — TanStack Query keeps the last good data and sets the error beside it, so checking the error first would discard the user's edits on a network blip. The query also uses `staleTime: Infinity` with `gcTime: 0`: fresh on every opening, never refetched while open.

Assertions unchanged; each one's meaning verified unchanged by the cold review. A freshly created chart needs no special case: `UPGRADES[0]` turns the stub into a well-formed empty chart before the browser sees it.
  - **Outside the original Files: the server now says why it refuses a newer format, on reads only.** A `ChartDocumentError` raised while upgrading a chart on read fell through to the generic handler as a 500 "Internal error", so AC-181's "saying so" never reached any browser. The GET route now answers 422 with the message. **Scoped to the GET route deliberately**: the repo also reads a document back *after* writing it, so mapped globally the same error answered a committed save or rename with a 4xx meaning "nothing was written" — the cold review caught that in my first version. Pinned by a test that a write never answers with a refusal.

A write must merge the edited state onto the *loaded document*, never rebuild one from `EditorState` — or it drops them, the same shape as the `dashboardOf` data loss at T075.

Mutation-verified both ways — removing the recommendation leaves the tile undrawn, and a constant `bar` fails with "Expected: line, Received: bar".

  - **Query first, then recommend**, because cardinality is counted in the rows returned: three statuses recommend `bar`, an empty result `hbar` (unknown cardinality counts as unbounded). Deciding before the query — the order the old code used — could disagree with the editor, which always has its rows.
  - **"No chart type yet" is kept, and is now true where it appears.** It is reached by a chart the recommender can place nowhere, such as one with only a time dimension: every candidate needs a measure except a table, and a table with no value and no category has nothing to encode. **I first recorded this branch as unreachable**, generalising from the recommender's one documented empty case, and left it untested on that basis; the cold review found the counter-example and it now has a test. Such a chart is queried although its rows cannot change the answer — as the editor queries it — which keeps one path for every typeless chart.
  - **An empty chart is not queried** and says "This chart has no fields yet.", imported from `templates.ts` (`NOTHING_CHOSEN`) so it matches an unbound tile (AC-068) and the two cannot drift.
  - **A throw while drawing fails that tile only.** Every tile's chart loads in one `Promise.all`, so the reading, recommending and compiling after the query sit inside the same `try` as the query; otherwise one bad value would have made every tile on the dashboard read "no longer available".
  - AC-012 pinned by identity: the tile receives the very `TileLoadError` thrown, query and all. The earlier test used `toEqual`, which compares errors by name and message and would have passed a rebuilt error that had lost its query.

  - **Added 2026-09-21, found by T193's cold review.** AC-046 was ticked at T052 on the editor alone. Dashboard tiles render axes, labels, tooltips and tables — every surface the criterion names — but `specFor` never passed the model's formats to `compile`, so a currency measure read `$76,900.00` in the editor and `76,900` on every dashboard. The criterion's failing state was observable on the surface most people read. Invisible because both of the main dashboard's charts count orders, which have no format.
  - Done 2026-09-21. **AC-046 unticked**, not ticked: it is false in the editor too, for trellis, heatmap and scatter charts (T195). T052 only ever tested a vertical bar.
  - **Formats are applied as tiles are drawn, not as they load.** `compile` only carries formats onto the spec — it computes nothing from them — so the dashboard reads the model through the editor's own query (`META_QUERY`) and applies the formats at render. Each tile paints when its own data arrives and picks up its formats when the model does. **My first version awaited the model inside every chart's load** and called that "off the critical path": the fetch was concurrent, but every tile's first paint still waited for it, and since nothing bounds a `/v1/meta` call, one stuck request would have held every dashboard on its loading state. Dashboards had never called `/api/meta` before. The same redesign removed three smaller defects the review found in that version: a malformed model threw and failed every tile; a brief model failure was cached as good data under the charts' key for five minutes; and it used `fetchQuery`, which is deprecated in the installed TanStack Query and rejects on a failed refetch where `useQuery` keeps the last good data.
  - **`formatsFrom` never throws.** A `cubes` that is not a list, or holds a null, made `formatsOf` throw; now it yields no formats.
  - **Horizontal bars wrote their categories as money.** `axis.ts` always hung the measure's formatter on `yAxis`, which for a horizontal bar is the category axis — so the values went unformatted and order ids read `$1,234.00`. Found in the editor's shared code, and fixed here rather than in T195 because this task is what would have carried it onto dashboards, where the recommender turns any bar over fifty categories horizontal. The formatter now goes to whichever axis is the value axis, found by its type.
  - **One definition of the model query.** `FieldList` registered its own function under the same key, so whichever ran first decided what the other saw.
  - **Proven both ways in the browser.** A currency measure reads as money on a tile's axis and in its table (found by column name, since Cube orders its columns); a count on the same dashboard stays a plain number. Mutations: supplying no formats fails the first; writing every measure as money fails the second ("Expected: 0, Received: 5").

The tile draws unformatted, which is AC-046's failing state shown silently, and for a `percent` measure changes how large the number looks (0.25 against 25%), not only its style.   - **Built 2026-09-21.**
    - **Research, observed first.** A throwaway Cube v1.7.42 with one probe member per format showed what `/v1/meta` reports, recorded in `research/cube/data-modeling.md` ("What `/v1/meta` reports for each format"):
      - every number member's `formatDescription.specifier`, the d3 string Cube means, including `,.2~f` when nothing is declared;
      - a currency code beside a `$` specifier;
      - a time pattern as `{type: 'custom-time', value}`.
    - `d3-format@3.1.2` was already researched (`research/supporting-utilities.md`). `d3-time-format@4.1.0` was researched for this task (`research/d3-time-format.md`) as the library Cube's own client uses for time patterns. Both are pinned in `@wechart/core`.
    - **Core (`format.ts`):**
      - A member's format is `{specifier, currency?, time?}` as meta reports it, or a name or specifier written by hand.
      - Numbers are written with d3-format in an en-US locale, with the hyphen-minus wechart has always used rather than d3's U+2212. A currency code (EUR, GBP…) swaps in its own symbol, named by Intl. An unreadable specifier falls back to Cube's plain number.
      - A time member's declared pattern wins over its granularity (AC-188's "unless the member's model format says otherwise"). It is written with a private en-US d3-time-format locale in UTC, and the pattern also applies under the `member.<granularity>` column. A pattern with no directive (a time dimension's `format: link` reaches meta as the pattern "link") keeps to the granularity's form.
    - **Web:**
      - `formatsOf` now reads what meta reports: the specifier and currency for a member that declares a format, and the pattern for a time member.
      - A number dimension that declares no format, such as an id, is still left as it came (T195).
    - **Charts:**
      - The helper that wrote only time members (`modelWriter`, formerly `time.ts`) now also writes any member with a declared format. So a dimension's format shows on a category axis, a tooltip heading, a legend and a trellis panel title, not only in tables (T195 finding 7).

  - **direction.md decides the shape, so this is not an open choice.** `direction.md` §2 Layout: "A tile's ✎ pencil opens the chart editor as a **full-viewport overlay** with '← Back to _dashboard_'. From view mode, the pencil switches the dashboard into edit mode first." And: "an Actual size / Fit preview at the tile's own size, and Esc = Back (keeps the changes)." `EditChrome`'s `overlay` prop is the existing mechanism — it is what `SettingsPage` uses, and it keeps the session mounted.
  - **Measure the width, do not compute it.** The document stores columns, not pixels, and sections and tab groups each have their own nested canvas, so a nested tile's column track is narrower than the page's. Computing width from `w` and the page width would be wrong for exactly the tiles inside sections. Measure the tile's element at the moment the editor opens — possible only because an overlay leaves it in the DOM.
  - The tile id must survive the call: `DashboardView.tsx` currently resolves the tile to its chart id and drops the tile id before calling `onEditChart`, so the overlay could not know which tile to size against. The prop's parameter is also misnamed `tileId` while carrying a chart id.

  - Re-verify **AC-057** ("open its chart editor with E"). Its e2e test asserted the URL became `/explore/charts/…/edit`; under the overlay the dashboard stays mounted and the URL does not change.   - **Boundary with T192 moved, 2026-09-21, because AC-066 forced it.** "Return with the tile updated" needs the edit to be *somewhere* the tile reads. Writing the chart document on Back would do it — and would break **AC-066, which is ticked**: a chart written during the session survives a Discard. So this task holds the edit **in the session** as a draft the tile renders, which the session's whole-state history then reverts for free. T192 keeps what AC-183 actually adds: writing the chart document *only* on dashboard save, and proving nothing is written before.
  - **Esc and Back keep the changes** (direction.md §2: "Esc = Back (keeps the changes)"). The opposite of the settings page, whose Back goes through a discard guard (AC-073). Do not copy that guard across.
  - **Done as T193 (2026-09-21), recorded here because this is where it was found.** A recommendation-following chart could not reach a dashboard, and AC-034 needs it to. `specFor` in `DashboardPage.tsx` refused any chart with no saved `chartType` ("This chart has no chart type yet"), while the document's own rule is that an absent type means "the recommender's pick is followed" — which is what the editor does. So a chart built following its recommendation draws in the editor and errors on the tile: AC-034's failing state, "the dashboard tile does not reflect the applied changes". Invisible so far because every seeded chart saves an explicit `bar`. No task or criterion chose the refusal, and the comment directly above `specFor` states the principle it breaks: "a chart has to look the same on a dashboard as it did where it was built".
    - **The fix is to recommend on the dashboard as the editor does, not to store the effective type.** Storing it would turn every recommendation-following chart into a manual one — presence of a type *means* the user chose it — so its type would stop adapting to field changes, which nobody asked for.
    - The inputs are already there. The editor's signature is built from the spec's wells and the query's rows alone (`ChartEditor.tsx`, `signature`), not from Cube metadata, and `specFor` has both. Extract that into one shared function so the two cannot drift; query first, then type = saved ?? recommended; keep "no chart type yet" only where nothing can be recommended, which makes the message true. Do not query an empty chart — the editor's own guard is `enabled: !isEmpty(spec)`.
  - **Built 2026-09-21.** The ✎ pencil (view and edit mode) and E open the editor as an overlay through `EditChrome`'s `overlay`; `session.edit()` enters edit mode first and is a no-op when already editing, so it cannot re-base a session's changes. Back and Esc hold the edit as a draft on the session's document (`chartDrafts.ts`): drawn by `tileChartOf`, the function every tile uses, so an emptied chart reads "no fields yet"; undone, redone and discarded with everything else, which is what demonstrates **AC-066's chart-spec clause**, open since T072 ("Discard takes the chart back as well"). A chart already drafted reopens on its draft. The editor's state is written back by merging onto the loaded document (`chartDocumentWith`), which round-trips every field.

    - **Save silently lost a chart edit and the tile reverted** — AC-034's own failing state, reached through Save. The reason names Undo, not Discard, which throws away every change in the session. T192 narrows the block to shared charts.
    - **The preview's chart height came from the saved dashboard**, so a tile resized in the session was previewed with its chart at the old height. The height now comes from the caller that holds the session.

    - A drafted tile lost its currency format (formats were applied before drafts replaced the saved spec); the view now formats after merging.

The Actual-size test compares the chart inside the frame, not only the frame the tile's box was handed.

  - **Decided, not fixed:**
    - **"The tile's own size" is its size on screen when the editor is opened** (review F8). Editing adds a 300 px sidebar, so the same tile is narrower opened with E in edit mode than with the pencil in view mode. A reader sees the view-mode size, so that is arguably the truer one — but getting it from edit mode means modelling every nested canvas's width, which "measure, do not compute" was adopted to avoid. The edit-mode size is also the conservative one: a chart that fits there fits for readers. Revisit if users find it misleading.

Every tab group came back on its first tab — a tile edited from a later tab was not on screen on return, and focus had nowhere to go — and both panes came back at the top, AC-067's failing state. The settings page had always done this. `EditChrome` now keeps the dashboard at the same place in the tree under an overlay, hidden with visibility (no layout change, so scroll positions stay valid), inert and `aria-hidden`; the overlay is an absolutely placed cover.   - **Narrowed 2026-09-21 when T032 took the draft-holding half.** T032 holds a tile's chart edit in the session (`chartDrafts.ts`), which is what made "return with the tile updated" possible without breaking AC-066 — and it demonstrates AC-066's last clause in the browser ("Discard takes the chart back as well"). What remains here is exactly AC-183's own claim: write the chart document on dashboard save, and never before. Until this lands, **Save is held back** while any chart edit made from a tile is waiting, with the reason beside it (T032's `saveBlocked`) — so nothing is lost, but a dashboard with such an edit cannot be saved without undoing back past it. This task lifts that for dashboard-local charts. The overlay's "Save dashboard" button (direction.md §2: "applies the chart and saves the dashboard") belongs here too.
  - **Added 2026-09-21 with the user's approval, split out of T032.** No chart document is written anywhere in `packages/web` today — charts are only ever read or deleted.   - Why drafts rather than "write now, revert on Discard" — recorded here because this task's first plan held both halves: the latter would make Discard a server write, racing anyone else editing a shared chart. AC-066's last clause is now demonstrated by T032, not here.
  - **Library charts wait on T134 (AC-128).** Writing a shared chart edited from a tile changes every dashboard that uses it, and AC-128 requires the editor to say so, with the count, and offer a local copy *before* changes spread. Until T134 lands this task writes local charts only and keeps Save held back for a shared chart's draft (T032's `saveBlocked`, narrowed from "any draft" to "any shared-chart draft"). Also needs the chart's **version**: `loadChart` keeps only the body, so the draft must carry the version it was loaded at, or a concurrent edit to the chart would be overwritten in silence where the dashboard's own save refuses one (409). If a chart write fails, do not save the dashboard: keep the session and its drafts, and say why — nothing may be lost to a partial save.
  - `EditChrome` is generic over the document type, so drafts either widen the session's state to `{ dashboard, chartDrafts }` or ride on a wrapper `DashboardView` passes in. Whichever, the tile must render the *draft* spec while the session holds one, or the user applies a change and sees the old chart.
  - A shared library chart edited from a tile affects every dashboard that uses it. direction.md rule 41 requires the editor to say so and offer "Make a local copy"; `GET /api/charts/:id/dashboards` already returns the dashboards. Out of this task's scope unless AC-183's test needs it, but do not write a shared chart silently.
  - **Merge onto the loaded document; never rebuild one from `EditorState`.** (Handed over from T191, which found it.) The editor's state has no slot for the document's `encoding`, title, owner, `folderId` or `localToDashboardId`, so `editorStateOf` drops them. Building the written document with `chartDocumentOf(state)` would drop them again on save — a chart silently leaving its folder or its dashboard, the exact shape of the `dashboardOf` data loss at T075. Keep the loaded `ChartDocument` beside the draft and write `{ ...loaded, chart: { ...loaded.chart, <edited fields> } }`. Test it with a chart that carries every one of those fields.
  - **Writing the type: `chartType` only if the user chose one.** Omit it for a chart following the recommendation. Writing the effective type would pin it as manual on the next open (T191 loads a present type as manual, per the document's rule). Safe since T193, which made the dashboard recommend for a typeless chart exactly as the editor does.
  - **Encoding is not previewed.** The editor's preview ignores stored `encoding` (`ChartEditor.tsx`, `previewDocument`), so once anything writes encoding, the preview and the tile draw differently. Nothing writes it yet; note it if this task starts to.
  - **Built 2026-09-21.**
    - **One transaction, on the server.** A dashboard save that carries chart edits goes to `PUT /api/dashboards/:id` with `{ dashboard: {body, version}, charts: [{id, body, version}] }`; a save with none still uses `PUT /api/documents/dashboard/:id`, unchanged. The repository's `updateTogether` writes every document in one READ COMMITTED transaction, each checked against the version it was read at, in (kind, id) order so two saves touching the same documents take their locks in the same order; any failure rolls back all of them and rethrows the same error (`docs/research/db-access.md`, "Query-time transactions"). Proven against the real Postgres: a non-transactional version failed the rollback test, and the transactional one passes. The race test passes without the transaction too — it proves the version check, not atomicity, and is recorded as such.
    - **The dashboard-save route refuses what the client holds back**, by the same rule (`refusalOf` in the client): a library chart → 400, a chart local to another dashboard → 400, and a body that would move a chart to another dashboard → 400. Locality is judged only at the version the client read — a mismatch is a 409 at once — so a client cannot have its write judged by a locality nobody checked. A stored chart this build cannot read → 422, as the document GET answers it. **This is not every route that writes a chart:** `PUT /api/documents/:kind/:id` writes any chart, library or not — it is the library chart's own save — so "no client can spread a change by calling the API" would be false, and the first version of this record and of the route's comment said it; both corrected at review (F2). A stale version inside the transaction → 409 naming which document (`document: {kind, id}`), which `saveDashboard` turns into a `StaleSaveError` saying whether it was the dashboard or a chart; any other refusal reaches the user with the server's own reason, not "Could not save: 400". A deadlock (`40P01`) → 409, the same "changed somewhere else" as a race. **That is a default, not a decision:** `docs/research/db-access.md` lists retry-versus-conflict as open for the user, as are statement and lock timeouts (left at Postgres's defaults). The stakes are low — every `updateTogether` locks in (kind, id) order, and a single-document write holds one lock, so no two writers here can form a cycle — but the first version of this record said "decided here", which it was not mine to say (review F12).
    - **Version carried from the first read.** `loadChart` returns the version; `ChartDraft.version` keeps it through every reopening of a drafted chart, so a second edit is still checked against the version the first was read at. A draft with no version holds Save back (`whyUnsaveable`) and `chartWritesOf` throws for one, so the two guards cannot silently disagree into a blind overwrite.
    - **Save held back only for a chart that cannot be written with this dashboard**: a library chart, a chart local to another dashboard, a versionless draft. Library charts wait on T134 (AC-128), client (`whyUnsaveable`) and server (400) alike; T134's entry records what it inherits. Only drafts of charts a tile still shows count, and only those are written: a tile deleted after its chart was edited no longer holds Save back, and its chart is not written for nothing on screen.
    - **The tile keeps the edit through the save.** Saving leaves edit mode at once, before the server answers, and the drafts go with the session — so the tile fell back to the chart as loaded until the charts were fetched again. The save writes the drafts into the charts' cached query first (`onMutate`), and a failed save re-fetches them. The browser test records every chart type the tile shows from Save on; without the cache write it sees `bar` twice.
    - **The editor's "Save dashboard"** (direction.md §2: "applies the chart and saves the dashboard"; AC-183 quotes it). `EditSession.save(document)` saves a document handed to it, through the same refusal as the bar's Save — the "other route" `EditChrome`'s own refusal was written for, which no route reached until now; a refused document is kept as a change, not dropped. The overlay's Back and Save dashboard leave by one path (`leave`), so what is saved is exactly what Back would apply. It is held back, with its reason beside it and tied to it, only by a *change* to a chart that cannot be written — `whyUnsaveableWith` asks with the pending edit before it is applied — so opening a shared chart to look at it does not stop the dashboard's other changes being saved.

The transaction guarantees a failed save writes *nothing*, so nothing is half-saved — but nothing keeps the work for another try either. The client now applies the server's rule (`refusalOf`), which needs the dashboard's id — `DashboardView` takes `dashboardId`, required, so seventeen test renders now pass one — and a refusal reaches the user with the server's reason.

    - **`EditChrome`** takes an `onSave` that may return a promise: edit mode is left when it resolves, and on a rejection the session stays exactly as it was.     - **`DashboardPage`** hands back `save.mutateAsync(...)`. `onSuccess` awaits the dashboard's refetch, so the saved dashboard is in the cache when the session leaves (the page picks it up a moment later — see F8); it does **not** await the charts' refetch — that re-runs every tile's Cube query, and would hold the user at "Saving…" long after the save is stored, while the tiles already show the saved charts.     - `saveDashboard` arms its own `AbortController` and `setTimeout`, passes the signal to `fetch`, and clears the timer in a `finally`. Not `AbortSignal.timeout`: MDN says it "does not provide a way to cancel its timeout" and recommends exactly this pairing where the timeout must be cancellable, and its clock is the platform's, which a test's fake timers cannot drive (`docs/research/abort-and-timers.md`, written for this task).
    - **The wording follows the user's own caution.** The request may land after the browser stops waiting, so the reason says the work is still here, that the save may still have gone through, and to look in another tab before saving again — rather than sending someone to save over their own write, or to reload on the false promise that only their copy exists.
    - Told apart from an unreachable server by `signal.aborted`, since both arrive as a rejected fetch.
    - **Tests:** `dashboard/edit/save.test.ts` on fake timers — nothing at 59 seconds, failed at 61 with the reason, the request actually abandoned, and a save that answers in time leaving no timer behind; `e2e/chart-save.spec.ts` holds a save at the network for the real minute and finds the session still open with its edit still drawn (AC-184).

The reason must not send the user into a worse loss: a save that did land moves the version on, so the next Save would be refused as a conflict with the user's *own* save, and "reloading discards yours" would then be untrue — the reload would show their work. Holding the page open removes that race rather than catching it, so the test now asserts the new guarantee: no Edit is offered while the save is out, and the session begun afterwards starts from what the save stored, so its own save is accepted rather than refused. The test-integrity hook flagged the replaced matcher; it is reported to the user rather than worked around.

- [x] T033 (AC-035) Undo, redo and discard every change made since the editor opened
  - Files: `packages/web/src/editor/history.ts`, `packages/web/src/editor/history.test.ts`, `packages/web/src/editor/ChartEditor.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`, `e2e/chart-editor.spec.ts`

  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-035 ticked. The history stores **states, not actions**. "Every change" is the criterion, and storing states means a new kind of change is undoable the day it is added, with no inverse operation to remember to write. The editor's undoable state is therefore one value — cube, spec and chart type together — rather than three pieces each of which would have to record itself.
  - A drag in progress is deliberately **outside** the history: it is a pointer position, not a change to the chart, and undo should not step back through one.
  - **Discard is itself undoable.** It lands on the opening state with everything before it still in the past, so discarding by mistake is recoverable. The criterion asks that Discard restores the original, not that it destroys the history.
  - The browser test walks back through all four kinds of change — chart type, dimension, measure, and the choice of cube — until Undo is disabled, rather than testing one step.

  - Files: `packages/core/src/chart-spec.ts`, `packages/web/src/editor/ResultsDrawer.tsx`, `packages/web/src/editor/ChartEditor.tsx`, `packages/web/src/editor/ResultsDrawer.test.tsx`, `e2e/chart-editor.spec.ts`

  - Depends on: T025
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-036 ticked. The result rows are **the same data the chart rendered**, passed in rather than re-fetched — re-fetching would let the drawer and the chart disagree, which is exactly the failure the criterion names. A test changes the query and asserts both move together.
  - Underlying rows come from the chart's own query with `ungrouped: true` and a cap, which is what direction.md §7a already decided. Derived from the same spec as the chart's query, so the two cannot name different members, and the measures come back unaggregated — the drawer shows the amounts that were summed, not just the keys.
  - They are fetched only while that view is open. The rows beneath an aggregate can be a large query and nobody has asked for it yet.
  - **Rejected at cold review, then rebuilt.** The first attempt selected the cube's primary key alongside the dimensions, and claimed a cube without a public primary key "gives no way to ask". Both were wrong. Cube hides primary keys by default (`research/cube/data-modeling.md`), so the view was inert on `customers` and `products` — two of the three sample cubes — which is AC-036's own "any of the three views is missing". And the repo's research names `ungrouped` and `drill_members` as other routes, so the claim contradicted its own sources.

A time member is selected in `dimensions`, not `timeDimensions`: without a granularity Cube filters without grouping, so the bare `timeDimensions` form returned no time column at all.
  - **Truncation is disclosed.** Both tables cap at 100 rows and say so. A table that silently stops at 100 while the chart reports 240 reads as the two disagreeing, which is the thing the criterion exists to prevent.
  - **A real bug the test caught:** the drawer was rendered inside the result's success branch, so any re-query unmounted it and it reopened closed — changing a field would silently shut a drawer the user had opened. The fix is twofold: it sits outside the loading and error branches, **and** its open/view state lives in the editor, because the drawer is still unmounted when the chart has no fields and state held inside it would be lost when a well is emptied or the cube changed.

## F5. Chart rendering

- [x] T035 (AC-037) Render the bar family from a chart spec
  - Files: `packages/core/src/render-spec.ts`, `packages/core/src/render-spec.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/theme.ts`, `packages/charts/src/series/bar.ts`, `packages/charts/src/series/bar.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/testing/renderToSvg.ts`
  - Failing test: `bar.test.ts`: bar, horizontal bar, grouped bar and stacked bar each render from a valid spec (AC-037).
  - Depends on: T001
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-037 stays unchecked**: it names 18 chart types and this task delivers 4. It is ticked at T044, when the last of them renders. T036–T044 carry the rest.
  - **The tests render, they do not inspect an option.** ECharts draws headlessly through its SSR mode (`init(null, …, { ssr: true, renderer: 'svg' })`), so each test produces a real SVG and reads the marks back out of it. AC-037 fails "when a listed type cannot be produced from a valid spec", and an option object is not a chart: a test asserting `series[0].type === 'bar'` passes just as happily when the option is unrenderable.
  - Orientation is read from where the category labels land — spread along the bottom for a bar, down the side for a horizontal bar — rather than from the option, so "horizontal" means the drawn result and not the flag we set.
  - Grouped and stacked are told apart by geometry: grouped marks occupy disjoint horizontal extents on a shared baseline, stacked marks share one column with the upper mark's foot meeting the lower one's head. Mutating the layout table so `hbar` is not horizontal and `stackedBar` is not stacked fails 4 tests, so the assertions discriminate.
  - **Rejected at cold review on the mark rules, then fixed.** The first version claimed direction.md §6 was "implemented and demonstrated" and neither half held. The gap was drawn as a 1 px border on each of two touching marks, on the assumption that two 1 px borders make 2 px — but an SVG stroke straddles the boundary the two marks share, so both strokes land in the same 1 px band. Each mark carries the **full** width. And the only assertion guarding it looked for `stroke="<surface>"` in the output, which survives a border width of 0: zrender emits `stroke` whatever the width, and omits `stroke-width` entirely when it is 1 — precisely the value the bug produced.
  - Rounding was asserted as "the outline contains an arc", which is true of a pill, of a half-pixel radius, and of a bar rounded at the baseline — the rule inverted. The helper now records each corner's radius and position, and the tests assert both. A **negative** bar grows downwards, so its data-end is its bottom; the radii follow the sign of each value, which also means they are per datum and not per series. Restoring either bug fails a test.
  - "Outermost segment" is per drawn segment, not per series: `seriesOf` emits `null` gaps, so a stack whose top series is missing in one category would otherwise get a flat top there.
  - `bar` and `groupedBar` share one layout, which the review rightly asked about. It is a decision: a grouped bar *is* a bar chart with more than one series, so grouping is a property of the data. Making `bar` refuse a second series would turn "add another measure" into an error for something the user plainly meant. A test pins the two together so they cannot quietly diverge, and so the grouped block is not read as proving something about the type that it only proves about the data.
  - The test palette is deliberately **not** the default ramp. With `DEFAULT_PALETTE[0]` and `[1]` every assertion passed even if `barOption` ignored `spec.palette` — and the palette being handed in rather than looked up is the hinge AC-039 hangs on.
  - **The chart-type vocabulary moved to `@wechart/core`**, which the recommender now re-exports. It is what a saved chart is written in, and the renderer has to agree with it; two lists would drift, and a recommendation for a type the renderer never heard of fails at draw time. The 18 names are unchanged, and `ChartSpec.chartType` is now typed as `ChartType` rather than `string` — without that the stated reason was aspirational, since a saved chart was not actually written in the vocabulary.
  - The path reader throws on any command it does not handle. Skipping one silently returns a wrong bounding box, which would make every geometry assertion in this file meaningless rather than failing.
  - A gap is `null`, never `0`: zero is a measurement and a missing row is the absence of one. Cube returns measures as JSON strings, so `seriesOf` converts them — a renderer trusting the raw value would sort and scale them as text.
- [x] T036 (AC-037) Render the line and area family
  - Files: `packages/charts/src/series/line.ts`, `packages/charts/src/series/line.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/testing/renderToSvg.ts`
  - Failing test: `line.test.ts`: line, multi-series line, area and stacked area each render from a valid spec (AC-037).
  - Depends on: T035
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-037 stays unchecked** — 8 of its 18 types now render. It is ticked at T044.
  - A line is an unfilled area, so all four are one `line` series set differing only in whether the region beneath is filled and whether the series stack. Mutating that table so `area` does not fill and `stackedArea` does not stack fails 3 tests.
  - **Finding marks needed a second route.** A line is a stroke with `fill="none"`, invisible to the fill-based `marksOf`; an area draws both a filled polygon and the line on top. `strokesOf` keys on `fill="none"`, which is also what separates a line from its own markers — a marker is a stroked path in the series colour too, but a filled circle carrying a scale transform. Setting `symbolSize` made those markers appear and the first version of the helper counted them as lines, reporting four where one was expected.
  - Stacking is proved by geometry rather than by the `stack` option: the upper series' line sits above the lower one at every category. The multi-series test asserts the unstacked pair **crosses** (count is below total in jan and above it in feb), so a chart that merely claims to stack fails.
  - **A gap breaks the line.** `connectNulls` stays at ECharts' default of false, and the test asserts one break for a null month and none without. A missing month is not a month with no orders; joining across it would draw a trend through data that was never measured. This is the rendered consequence of `seriesOf` emitting `null` rather than `0`.
  - direction.md §6 is demonstrated: lines at 2 px, and a marker per point of at least 8 px. Marker size is read from the transform matrix, because ECharts scales a unit circle — the drawn diameter is nowhere in the path data.
  - Carries T035's review lessons: the test palette is not the default ramp, so the palette hand-in is falsifiable here too.
- [x] T037 (AC-037) Render scatter
  - Files: `packages/charts/src/series/scatter.ts`, `packages/charts/src/series/scatter.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/testing/renderToSvg.ts`
  - Failing test: `scatter.test.ts`: a scatter spec renders its points with both value axes bound (AC-037).
  - Depends on: T035, T188
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-037 stays unchecked** — 9 of its 18 types now render.
  - **The first type whose x axis is a measure**, which is why it does not go through `seriesOf`: that shapes rows into categories and series, and a scatter has neither. Each row is one point. It is also the type the old `{category, series, values}` encoding could not express at all, and the reason the channel model landed first.
  - **"Both value axes bound" is tested as spacing, not as axis type.** A chart that draws points against two axes could still have one on a categorical scale, and nothing about the points' existence would show it. With x values 10, 20 and 40, a value axis puts the second gap at twice the first and a category axis spaces all three evenly. Mutating `xAxis` to `category` moves the ratio from 2.0 to 1.0 and fails the test.
  - A separate test pairs each point with its own row: left to right the points go low, high, middle, so a renderer that sorted rows by either measure produces a monotonic run and fails.
  - `pointsOf` is a third way of finding marks. A scatter point is the same scaled unit circle a line marker is, but filled with the series colour and carrying no stroke, so the stroke-keyed `markersOf` cannot see it. The position lives in the transform matrix, not in the path data.
  - A row missing either measure is dropped rather than drawn. There is no baseline to fall back to as a bar has, and a point at zero would assert a measurement that was never made.

  - Files: `packages/charts/src/series/pie.ts`, `packages/charts/src/series/pie.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/testing/renderToSvg.ts`
  - Failing test: `pie.test.ts`: pie and donut each render from a valid spec (AC-037).
  - Depends on: T035, T188
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-037 stays unchecked** — 11 of its 18 types now render.
  - **Counting slices does not test a pie.** Its whole claim is that each slice's *angle* is its share of the total, and a renderer that gave every category an equal slice would pass a count. The angles are read back out of the drawn geometry: with 50/25/25 the slices must be 180°, 90° and 90°, where an even split would be three 120° slices and fail all three.
  - **The hole is read from the geometry, not from the option that asked for it.** ECharts writes a pie slice with its edge running to the centre and a donut's stopping at the inner radius, so "is a ring" is a fact about the path. Setting the donut's inner radius to zero fails the test.
  - A category with no measurement is left out rather than drawn as a zero-width sliver. A sliver in the legend invites the reader to look for a slice that is not there.
  - Slices carry the same surface-coloured gap as the bar family, so a pie's slices and a stacked bar's segments read the same way across a dashboard.

  - Files: `packages/web/src/tiles/DataTable.tsx`, `packages/web/src/tiles/DataTable.test.tsx`, `packages/web/src/tiles/ChartTile.tsx`, `e2e/table-view.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `DataTable.test.tsx`: a table spec renders with real `<table>` semantics and virtualised rows (AC-037); `e2e/table-view.spec.ts`: every chart type offers a table view reachable from its tile (AC-178).

  - **Two requirements pull against each other.** Real `<table>` semantics are what a screen reader needs, and virtualisation is what stops ten thousand rows being ten thousand DOM nodes — and the usual way to virtualise is absolute positioning, which destroys table layout. TanStack's own guide suggests `display: grid`, "breaking semantic HTML". `research/table.md` settles it the other way: fixed row heights plus **spacer rows** standing in for what is not drawn, so the native layout survives, and `aria-rowcount`/`aria-rowindex`, which the APG table pattern requires exactly when rows "are hidden or not present in the DOM".

It earns its place at the pivot (T042) and sorting (T051).
  - The virtualiser's rect is supplied rather than observed. The tile decides the height, so there is nothing to discover by measuring — and measuring is what fails without a layout engine: the default watcher reports a zero-height rect, the window comes out empty and nothing is drawn.
  - **AC-178 is tested against the vocabulary, not a sample.** All eighteen types are exercised, and the fixture fills every well so none is left unable to draw. Skipping the ones that fell short would have been the easy green and would have excused exactly the types most likely to lack a view — the test-integrity hook blocked a `test.skip` that would have done it.
  - Known, accepted: React Compiler declines to memoise `DataTable` because TanStack Virtual returns functions it cannot memoise safely (`react-hooks/incompatible-library`). It is a warning about optimisation, not correctness.
