  - Failing test: `chart-document.test.ts`: a document carrying source, wells, chart type, encoding overrides and options round-trips through JSON unchanged, and a body missing `schemaVersion` is refused rather than guessed at (AC-014).
  - Depends on: T035
  - **Persist the authoring shape only.** All three reference products agree and none stores a render shape: Grafana's resolved `DataFrame[]` is built by `PanelQueryRunner.getData()` and never saved, Metabase hands the renderer `{card, data}`, Superset builds `ChartProps` at render. The one exception anywhere is a Grafana snapshot, which is a separate mode with its own metadata block.
  - **No cached derived query.** Superset caches one in `slices.query_context` and it is the source of its worst documented maintenance problem: the `form_data` → `query_context` derivation lived only in the frontend, so keeping the cache correct forced ~50 TypeScript functions to be re-implemented in Python. `toCubeQuery(spec)` stays a pure function computed on demand.
  - **Roles are named in the data, not recovered by aliasing.** Superset's flat `{controlName: value}` map costs it `extract_query_fields`' alias table plus three hand-maintained key lists in `standardizedFormData.ts`, purely so switching chart type keeps the user's fields.
  - **Encoding and options are sparse overrides over a computed baseline**, which is Metabase's model and already wechart's own for `chartTypeState`: absent means "follow the recommendation", present means the user pinned it. It is also why a permissive options map is tolerable — an unknown key is inert, not corrupt.
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-014 was already ticked at T003 and stays ticked**; this task gives it a chart definition to carry rather than the catalog metadata it held before. AC-037 waits for T188 and T044.
  - The catalog fields stay at the document's top level because the `documents` table generates its `title`, `folder_id` and `owner` columns out of them — nesting them would empty the folder tree and search. A test asserts their position, not just their presence.
  - **Absent is not empty.** Reading a chart with no pinned encoding back as `encoding: {}` would turn every recommendation into a pin, which is the distinction AC-032 rests on. Mutating the reader to default it fails the test.
  - **The reader refuses what it cannot place** rather than recovering: no version, a newer version, no cube, or a well holding something that is not a member name. Metabase's reader recovers to an empty query on any error, which is how instances ended up with cards that exist but have no query — a condition a later schema upgrade was written to repair and, by its own comment, failed to. An unreadable chart is worth an error; a chart that reads as something else is not.
  - An older version is refused here too, and upgraded by T187 before the reader sees it. That keeps the reader single-format and the migration in one place.
- [x] T187 (AC-181) Upgrade a chart saved in an earlier format, and refuse one from a newer format
  - Files: `packages/core/src/chart-migrations.ts`, `packages/core/src/chart-migrations.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/chart-document.ts`, `packages/api/src/repos/documents.ts`, `packages/api/src/repos/documents.test.ts`
  - Failing test: `chart-migrations.test.ts`: a fixture document at every past version upgrades to the current one and produces the same chart type, members and query; a document from a newer version is refused with a message naming the version (AC-181).
  - Depends on: T186
  - Forward-only, upgrade-on-read, one step per version, with a fixture per version that must keep passing. Both Grafana (`DashboardMigrator`, `if (oldVersion < N && finalTargetVersion >= N)`) and Metabase (`upgrade-card-schema-to`) work this way.
  - **The chain is append-only.** Metabase has two upgrade steps it can never delete — 21 "caused performance problems", 23 "This fix was a failure … now it's just a no-op" — because older rows still have to traverse them. A step is therefore written once and neutered rather than removed.
  - **A newer format is refused, not guessed at.** Metabase's reader recovers to `{}` on any deserialization error, which is how instances ended up with cards that exist but have no query — the condition a later schema upgrade was written to repair and failed to.
  - Superset's approach is explicitly rejected: it has no version on the payload, rewrites every row in place, stores the whole pre-migration document inside the document as `form_data_bak` permanently, and swallows per-row failures so a partial migration surfaces nothing.
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-181 ticked.** The upgrade runs in `toDocument`, the single place a stored row becomes a document — Metabase's `after-select` position. Removing that one call fails the repo test, so the wiring is load-bearing: a migration nothing calls is not a migration.
  - **Reading never writes.** The upgrade is re-applied on every read until the user saves, which is what Grafana's panel migrations do. A test asserts the row's version does not move across repeated reads; an upgrade that saved itself would bump the version on every read and race with a concurrent edit (AC-015).
  - **A fixture per past version, frozen.** A test asserts there is one for every version below the current, so adding a version without a fixture fails. These are fixtures of *stored data*: if one stops passing, the migration is wrong, not the fixture.
  - **A design error the tests caught.** The reader refused an empty cube, but "no cube chosen yet" is exactly the state a newly opened editor is in, and it is what the pre-definition bodies upgrade to. A *missing* cube is malformed; an *empty* one is legitimate. Conflating them made those bodies unopenable.
  - **AC-014 and AC-181 interact, and two existing tests were measuring the wrong thing.** "Stores a chart unchanged" used an arbitrary body, which is now a version-0 chart and so is upgraded on read — it was exercising AC-181 while claiming to test storage fidelity. Its fixture is now a current-format chart; the assertions are untouched. A chart in the current format is unchanged; only an older one is upgraded, which is what AC-181 asks for.
  - **2026-09-21, found at T191: "saying so" did not reach the browser.** `ChartDocumentError` was raised by the repo while upgrading on read and fell through the server's generic handler as a 500 "Internal error", so the sentence explaining the refusal was produced and thrown away. This task's tests call the core function directly and could not see that. **Fixed at T191 for reads:** the GET route now answers 422 with the message, and the chart editor shows it.
  - **Still open on two surfaces**, recorded rather than left to memory. AC-181 stays ticked because its `Fails when:` — a newer chart *silently rendering something other than what it describes* — is not observed on either; but the SHALL's "saying so" is met only in the editor.
    - **The dashboard tile** turns every read failure, the new 422 included, into "This chart is no longer available." (`specFor` in `DashboardPage.tsx`) — true of a deleted chart, false of one saved by a newer version. It should pass the server's reason on, as `loadEditorState` does.
    - **Writes.** The repo reads a document back after writing it, so a save or rename of a newer-format chart commits and then fails with the same error — still a 500 after the write went through. It should not claim failure; it probably should not read back through the upgrade at all. T191 pins only that it never answers with a 4xx.
- [x] T188 (AC-037) Compile a saved chart document into a RenderSpec
  - Files: `packages/core/src/compile.ts`, `packages/core/src/compile.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/render-spec.ts`, `packages/core/src/chart-document.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`
  - Failing test: `compile.test.ts`: a saved document plus result rows compiles to the `RenderSpec` the renderers already consume, and the same document with an encoding override compiles to the overridden channels (AC-037).
  - Depends on: T186
  - **This is the bridge AC-037 is written against.** Until it exists, "render … from a chart spec" cannot be demonstrated end-to-end however many chart types render.
  - **`Encoding` becomes a channel model** — x, y, colour, size, facet, row, column — replacing `{category, series, values[]}`, which was fitted to the bar and line families and cannot express scatter (two value axes), heatmap (two categories plus colour), treemap (hierarchy plus size), pivot (rows × columns × measures) or trellis (a facet). The channel vocabulary and its renderer-neutrality are already documented in `docs/research/vega-lite.md`: the fieldDef model "names data roles and visual channels", and rendering happens only after compiling.
  - **Channels bind to result columns by name.** Metabase's `graph.dimensions`/`graph.metrics` are plain result-column names, arrived at after abandoning ref-keyed settings over issue #11829 — open from 2019 to 2025 — and both keying schemes still coexist in their data. Cube member names are already stable names, so this costs nothing and avoids the whole class.
  - **Per-series and per-member settings need addressing, panel-wide ones do not** (Grafana's `options` vs `fieldConfig.defaults` vs matcher-selected `overrides`). The mechanical test: a setting that must apply to some series and not others has to be addressable. AC-040 (axes) and AC-046 (per-member format) are both in that category; the legend (AC-038) is not.
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-037 still waits for T044**, which needs all 18 types; the bridge it was missing now exists.
  - **The bar and line renderers pass unchanged.** That is the evidence the channel model is a faithful replacement for `{category, series, values}` rather than a rewrite: only `seriesOf` read the encoding's internals, and it now reads x, colour and y.
  - **Every channel holds a list**, even though most hold at most one member. The value axis genuinely takes several — a grouped bar of two measures — and Metabase's `graph.dimensions`/`graph.metrics` are both arrays for the same reason. One shape beats a union of singular and plural channels that every caller would have to know about.
  - **Three states, not two.** A channel the user never mentioned takes the computed default; one they filled wins; one they set to an **empty list** is them clearing it, and that also wins. Collapsing empty into absent would make "remove this field" impossible to express.
  - Overrides apply **per channel**. Mutating `compile` to swap in the override map wholesale fails a test — pinning a colour would otherwise blank the axes.
  - `defaultEncoding` prefers a time dimension on x and moves the plain dimension to colour. Time is the axis a reader expects to run left to right, and a second categorical member is more use as the series than fighting it for x.
  - **The e2e flake is solved, and it was never what it looked like.** The failure reports as `renders no text below 12 px at /explore` but the artifact says `Test timeout of 60000ms exceeded while setting up "context"` — the browser did not hand out a context in time, with nothing in the app involved. An earlier pass at this chased font tokens for a sub-12px value that does not exist. Playwright defaults to half the cores (6 here) and each worker launches a real browser at once, on a machine also running the docker stack and other services; local workers are now capped at 4 and CI keeps the default. Three consecutive full runs clean.

- [x] T189 (AC-037) Draw the chart in the app, from a compiled spec
  - Files: `packages/charts/src/echarts.ts`, `packages/charts/src/optionFor.ts`, `packages/charts/src/optionFor.test.ts`, `packages/web/src/tiles/ChartCanvas.tsx`, `packages/web/src/editor/ChartEditor.tsx`, `packages/web/vite.config.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, `e2e/chart-editor.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `e2e/chart-editor.spec.ts`: adding fields in the editor draws a chart with marks in it, and changing the fields redraws it (AC-037).
  - Depends on: T188
  - **User-approved plan change, 2026-09-20.** Eleven chart types rendered headlessly and nothing in the app drew one: `packages/web` imported neither `@wechart/charts` nor `echarts`, and no task created the component that would. The editor showed a row count and a JSON preview.
  - The headless SSR path proves an *option is renderable*. It says nothing about the browser lifecycle — `init` on a real node, `ResizeObserver`, `dispose` under React StrictMode, or switching theme — which is where integration failures actually live. Doing this with four renderer modules to adapt rather than eight is the point of doing it now.
  - `@wechart/charts` stays framework-free: it builds options, and `packages/web` owns the DOM and the React lifecycle, the same split `@wechart/ui` already has with tokens and components.
  - Imports go through `echarts/core` with an explicit registration set. `docs/research/echarts.md` is blunt about the alternative: `sideEffects` lists `index.js` and `lib/chart/*.js`, so one stray root import adds about 368 KB gzipped.
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-037 still waits for T044.** The editor now draws, with a browser test that asserts *marks*, not merely an `<svg>` — an empty chart frame would satisfy the latter — and that adding a second measure changes what is drawn.
  - **A type with no renderer says so rather than falling back.** `optionFor` throws and the canvas shows it. A fallback to bar would put a chart on screen the spec never asked for, which is worse than admitting the gap; the editor already explains unbuildable charts under AC-033.
  - The preview goes spec → document → `RenderSpec`, the same path a saved chart will take, so the preview and the saved chart cannot drift apart.
  - **Three real integration problems the headless tests could not have found**, which is the argument for doing this at four renderers rather than eight:
    1. **StrictMode double-invokes the effect** and ECharts throws on a second `init` for one element, so the instance is looked up with `getInstanceByDom` before creating one.
    2. **The browser suite was serving the dev server.** Once the app pulled ECharts through Vite's dev transform on every page load the suite went from about 40 seconds to two minutes and WebKit began timing out. It now serves the production build, as the API webServer already did — which is also the artefact users get.
    3. **`server.proxy` does not apply to `vite preview`.** Vite keeps `preview.proxy` separate, so serving the build without it would have 404'd every `/api` call.
  - Two test assumptions of mine were wrong, not the code: removing the dimension leaves one measure, which the recommender calls a KPI — a type with no renderer, so the app correctly declines to draw rather than redrawing; and the remove control is addressed by its label, not a test id.
  - The timeout is now 120s. It exists to catch a hung app and a pass takes about a second, so the ceiling costs no signal — it stops context-setup starvation on a loaded machine being reported as a product defect.

A test removes the first member and asserts the second keeps its colour.

- [x] T048 (AC-041) Aggregate the remainder into a labelled "Other (n)"
  - Files: `packages/core/src/topN.ts`, `packages/core/src/topN.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/compile.ts`, `packages/charts/src/colours.ts`
  - Failing test: `topN.test.ts`: past the top-N setting the chart renders N categories plus one "Other (n)" labelled with the number of members it covers (AC-041).
  - Depends on: T035
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-041 ticked.**
  - **The count in the label is what stops "Other" being a lie.** A slice labelled only "Other" could cover two members or two hundred, and a reader has no way to tell how much of the chart they are not seeing. The criterion asks for it explicitly and a test asserts it for two different tail sizes.
  - Folded in `compile`, not per renderer, so no chart type can be quietly exempt from the cap. It is a transformation of the rows, which is what that step is for.
  - **Ranked by value, not by arrival.** Otherwise the tail is whichever members Cube happened to return last, and the chart hides large members while showing small ones. A test puts the largest member second in the rows and expects it kept.
  - **The tail takes the neutral, not a palette slot** (direction.md §5). "Other" is not a member, and giving it a member's colour would put it in the same visual class as the things it hides.
  - No item is added when the category count is exactly the setting: an "Other (0)" would be an empty category taking a colour and a legend entry for nothing.
  - Ranking uses the *first* measure. Summing across measures of different units would be adding pounds to counts. The same additivity caveat as the pivot applies: a total of averages is not an average, and a non-additive measure needs its "Other" from Cube.

- [x] T051 (AC-044) Sort categories by value or label and keep the order across a refresh
  - Files: `packages/core/src/sort.ts`, `packages/core/src/sort.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/compile.ts`, `packages/web/src/editor/ChartEditor.tsx`, `packages/web/src/tiles/ChartTile.tsx`, `e2e/chart-editor.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `sort.test.ts`: ascending, descending and label orders are applied, and a refresh with unchanged data preserves the order (AC-044).
  - Depends on: T035
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-044 ticked.**
  - **"Keeps the order after a refresh" is what shaped it.** The order has to be a property of the chart, not of the rows that happened to arrive, so the same setting over the same data gives the same answer — ties included. Tied categories keep arrival order, which is what stops a refresh reshuffling a chart that did not change.
  - Sorting runs after the top-N fold in `compile`: the cap *selects* which categories are shown and the sort *arranges* what survives, so the tail is chosen on merit rather than on whatever the sort happened to put last.
  - A category with no measurement sorts last in **either** direction. Treated as zero it would lead an ascending chart and claim to be the smallest measurement, which it is not. "Other" is pinned last for the same reason: it is not a member, and sorting it by its total would imply it were one.
  - "Cube's order" is the default and a real choice rather than the absence of one — Cube was asked for an ordering and returned it, and leaving it alone is different from having none.
  - **A real bug the browser test found**, and the same class as the results drawer at T034: `ChartTile` kept "showing the table" in its own state, and the editor unmounts the tile on every re-query — so adding a field silently flipped a reader back from the table to the chart. The state now belongs to whatever outlives the tile, and the tile takes it as an optional controlled prop.
  - **A fixture that would have passed for the wrong reason:** the first version sorted by `orders.count`, and the sample data has exactly 80 orders in each status, so every category ties and ascending and descending are identical. Switched to a measure that varies.
- [x] T052 (AC-046) Format every value with its member's format in axes, labels, tooltips and tables
  - Files: `packages/core/src/format.ts`, `packages/core/src/format.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/tooltip.ts`, `packages/charts/src/format-places.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/axis.ts`, `packages/web/src/editor/fields.ts`, `packages/web/src/tiles/{DataTable,Kpi,PivotTable}.tsx`
  - Failing test: `format.test.ts`: currency and percentage members render with their declared format (AC-046); `format-places.test.ts` and `DataTable.test.tsx`: they do so in the axis, the tooltip and the table.
  - Depends on: T041, T047
  - Done 2026-09-20. **AC-046 ticked.**
  - **"From the Cube model" is the load-bearing phrase.** A format is not a per-tile setting: it is declared once on the measure and follows it everywhere, so the same number never appears as `1234` on an axis and `$1,234.00` in the table of the same chart. Verified against the running Cube — `/v1/meta` reports `format: "currency"` on `orders.total_amount`.
  - **The criterion names four places for a reason, so the places are tested, not just the formatter.** A formatter nothing calls formats nothing. Separate tests cover the axis, the tooltip and the table.
  - **A tooltip had to be built before it could carry a format.** No renderer set one. direction.md asks for a crosshair on line and area and a per-mark tooltip elsewhere, and that is the split. Every interpolated value is escaped: ECharts' tooltip formatter returns raw HTML, and `research/echarts.md` records that 6.1.0 fixed an XSS in exactly that path.
  - The member's format is the **default** and AC-040's axis setting the **exception**, not the other way round — a currency measure should read as money without anyone asking, and an explicit `numberFormat` still wins.
  - Cube's format vocabulary is wider than a chart can honour: `imageUrl`, `link`, `id` and d3 specifiers are all legal. Those fall back to a plain grouped number, because refusing to draw would be worse than drawing a number.
  - A gap formats as blank, never `0` or `NaN`: nothing was measured, and both alternatives assert something. Text passes through, because formatting a dimension's label as currency would be worse than leaving it.

The criterion's letter ("the edit sidebar with Add, Outline and Properties") is met by the tab, but a tab labelled Outline that shows nothing is not an outline, and the user's standing rule is that a tick needs the real surface. It comes back when the Outline shows the dashboard's tree (page tabs, then items, sections and tab groups nested, each with its kind) and a row selects and scrolls to its element, as the prototype does.

  - Depends on: T005

Wiring the route now would ship an edit bar whose Undo, Redo and Discard are permanently idle (nothing is editable until T060) over a Save that persists nothing (T071); that is a worse product state than the placeholder and a false basis for a tick. Same reasoning as AC-039 at T046. **AC-053 ticks at T023**, where an e2e can activate Edit on a real dashboard.

The chrome is generic over the document type because it never reads one — the document's shape belongs to the grid and the element grammar (T060 onwards) — and it fixes an architecture (`EditSession<T>` with `editing`, `document`, `changes` and `change`) that T060's canvas, T064's Add panel and T071's save have to fit.
  - It reuses `packages/web/src/editor/history.ts` rather than growing a second undo stack: it is already generic over the state it holds, `ChartEditor` uses it the same way, and storing whole states is what AC-060's "restore the element and its contents" will need. T066's file list is amended accordingly.
  - The change count travels **inside** the history snapshot. Discard is itself undoable, so it leaves an extra entry on the past, and a count taken from the stack's depth reads "3 changes" where the user has made one. There is a test for exactly that sequence.
  - Two deferrals worth naming so the next task does not read them as bugs: **Save does not leave edit mode** (AC-065 owns that, T071), so there is currently no way out of edit mode; and ⌘S and ⌘Z are in direction.md but trace to no AC, so they are absent.

The first cut seeded the history once at mount, which would have quietly ignored a dashboard that arrived from the server afterwards — the same class of bug as the T034 drawer and `ChartTile`'s table-view state. Found on re-reading, fixed, and covered by a test that fails against the old code. The cold review flagged it independently.

The first cut declared `role="toolbar"` without the pattern, which the cold review caught.

It is decisive only near the snap boundary, which is the only place "nearest column" is defined at all — so the test asserts that the boundary sits halfway between one tile width and the next. Verified by removing the term and watching that test, and only that test, fail.
- [x] T061 (AC-055) Resize a tile by edge or corner with a live readout, and offer no numeric size fields
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/grid/{resize.ts,resize.test.ts,Canvas.tsx}`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/model/{document.ts,document.test.ts}`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`, `e2e/{no-size-fields.spec.ts,dashboard.spec.ts}`
  - Failing test: `resize.test.ts`: dragging an edge or corner resizes the tile and shows columns, percentage and pixels (AC-055); `e2e/no-size-fields.spec.ts`: no width or height input exists anywhere in the editor; `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`: a real drag widens the tile and shows the live readout.
  - Depends on: T060
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-055 ticked.
  - The size is a function of **where the pointer started and where it is now**, never of accumulated deltas. Accumulating rounds at every intermediate move, so a slow drag and a quick one over the same distance would end on different sizes and dragging out and back would not return to where it began — which is what one of the tests asserts.
  - An edge is one axis. A pointer never travels in a straight line, so an edge that took both would resize the other axis on every drag; there is a test that drags diagonally from each edge.
  - The readout is rendered as `<output>` and reads exactly as direction.md section 2 writes it — `6 / 12 cols · 50% · 320 px` — with a test pinning that literal string. It is the only feedback a resize gives, and rule 36 names its three parts.
  - A resize goes through the edit session rather than into local state, so it is already undoable and already something Discard reverts (AC-060, AC-066). `resizeTile` rebuilds only the branch down to the tile and shares the rest, because the session compares states by identity.
  - **`MIN_COLUMNS` and `MIN_ROWS` are not in direction.md.** They come from the prototype in `docs/design/prototype`, which is the only place a minimum was ever decided, and the source says so plainly rather than implying the design document settled it. The first cut used 8 row units, which was simply invented — the prototype's `minH` is 10 (and 5 for a link tile, an exception that belongs with AC-064's tile, not here). Corrected before review.
  - The no-size-fields sweep is a DOM sweep rather than a component assertion, because rule 36 is a property of the whole surface: a size field *anywhere* breaks it. Verified by planting an `<input aria-label="Width (px)">` in the sidebar and watching both sweep tests fail and name it. It fills the chart editor's wells first and opens the results drawer — an empty editor renders almost none of its controls, so sweeping it would have been sweeping nothing.
  - The sweep reads every label source **together** rather than the first one present. A field named `tileWidth` whose visible label says "Width (px)" would otherwise be judged on the name alone, and `width` does not match inside camelCase under a word boundary.
  - Honest about its own reach: the three sidebar panels are empty today, because nothing passes `panels` yet, so those two sweeps currently assert over nothing and stand as a guard for when the Properties inspector arrives (T064 onwards). The model editor and any future dialog are not visited. direction.md rule 36 scopes the prohibition to dashboard edit mode, which the sweep does cover.
  - The e2e compares the readout's column count against the tile's `grid-column` span, not against a count recomputed from measured pixels. The pixel version passed in Chrome and failed in Firefox on sub-pixel rounding, which has nothing to do with the criterion.
  - `resizeTile` hands back untouched branches **by identity**, including the whole document when nothing matched, so an edit that changed one tile does not look to the history like an edit that changed every page. The first cut rebuilt every page and every section while claiming in its own docstring to do otherwise, and the test named "shares the branches it did not change" asserted only non-identities — it would have passed either way. Both corrected, and the test now asserts `toBe` on the branches that should be shared.
  - A tab group carries a width of its own, so it can be the target of a resize; a section cannot, being sized by what it holds. Resizing a tab group used to return successfully having done nothing, which would have been a trap at T062 and T064.
  - `MAX_ROWS` comes from the same prototype line as the minimum (`Math.min(120, …)`). Citing half a source and inventing the other half is exactly how `MIN_ROWS` went wrong, so the ceiling was taken along with the floor.
  - A canvas that has not been laid out measures zero, and the column pitch with it — the first pixel of a drag would have flung the tile to one end of the grid. With no width to count against, the width now stays where it was.
  - `data-resizing` is painted, not merely set: direction.md asks for "a stronger [grid] during a drag or resize", and the e2e asserts the canvas's background actually changes mid-drag. It was a hook with nothing on the other end until the review pointed that out.
  - Three gesture bugs found by writing the component test rather than by the browser, which happily hid all of them: `setPointerCapture` is not implemented in jsdom and threw, taking the whole `pointerdown` handler with it; a press and release with no movement reported a resize, putting a change on the edit history that changed nothing so Undo lit up for a click; and `pointercancel` committed whatever size was under the pointer when the browser took it away, which is not a size anyone chose.
  - **Pointer only for now.** The handles are `aria-hidden` and there is no keyboard path to a resize until Shift+arrows at T063 (AC-057). direction.md rule 39 asks that every edit-mode action work without a mouse, so until T063 lands that rule is not met on this surface.
- [x] T062 (AC-056) Drag a tile by its grip with a drop marker, including onto page-tab and tab-group labels
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/grid/{drag.ts,dropTarget.ts,dropTarget.test.ts,Canvas.tsx}`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/edit/MoveToMenu.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/model/{document.ts,document.test.ts}`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`, `packages/api/src/seed.ts`, `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `dropTarget.test.ts` decides where a drop lands; `document.test.ts` proves the move; `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts` drags for real onto a tile, a page tab and a tab-group label (AC-056).
  - Depends on: T060
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-056 ticked.
  - **A structural change came first.** `Elements` used to group runs of tiles into separate canvases and render sections between them. That looks identical and cannot support this criterion: a drop has to name the container it landed in, and one page produced several canvases all claiming to be that page. Now there is one canvas per container, with sections and tab groups as genuine grid items — which is also what makes "drop a tile before a section" a position that exists. The `grid-tile` test id became `grid-item`, since that is what it now identifies.
  - The decision is taken against **rectangles, not the DOM**. The prototype asks `elementFromPoint` and walks up with `closest`, which is untestable without a browser — and the rule that a tab label beats the container beneath it is exactly what has to be pinned, because getting it the wrong way round produces the criterion's own failing state.
  - **A test that passed for the wrong reason.** Mutating the label-beats-container rule did not fail anything: the innermost-container search was not filtering by kind, so a tab label — the smallest box of all — was being returned *as* a container, with an insertion index it has no use for. Both the bug and the blind test are fixed, and the mutation now fails.
  - **The drop index was horizontal only**, which the browser caught: dragging a tile onto its neighbour put it at the end of the container. A full-width section on the next row has its midpoint near the left edge, so a pointer on the right of the row above looks past it. Now compared in reading order — row first, x only within a row — with a unit test for the wrapped grid.
  - The index counts positions **without** the tile being moved, which is what `dropTargetAt` produces and what `moveTile` expects. Both ends are pinned from both sides in `document.test.ts`, because disagreeing about that convention is the classic off-by-one of every drag reorder.
  - **The ⋯ "Move to" menu is a condition, not a nicety.** `docs/research/dashboard-grid.md` makes it one: "whichever option wins, SC 2.5.7 forbids drag-only paths; the ⋯ 'Move to' menu must stay." A grip as the only route would fail WCAG 2.2 at AA. It offers Move to and nothing else — direction.md also puts Duplicate and Delete here, but those are AC-062 and AC-060, and a menu item that did nothing would be worse than an absent one. It closes on Escape and gives focus back, and closes on a press outside.
  - The seeded dashboard gained a tab group on its Appendix page. The criterion names two kinds of label — a page tab and a tab-group label — and they are different code paths reaching different containers; without one seeded, the second was untestable and the tick would have rested on half the criterion.
  - **A data-loss bug, found by the cold review.** The restructure made sections grid items, so a section got a grip; its own body is a drop container; and the move is one pass that inserts where the key matches and removes where it does not. Dropping a section into itself therefore rebuilt the subtree and then discarded the branch that had just been rebuilt — the section and everything in it vanished, silently, from a plausible gesture. Fixed twice over: `moveTile` refuses any move into the element itself or anything nested inside it, and **only tiles get grips**, since AC-056 is about dragging a tile and a grip on a container was scope no criterion describes. Moving containers belongs with the Outline.
  - The reading order was wrong for a row of unequal heights. Grid items carry explicit heights, so two in one row share a top but not a bottom, and a pointer below a short tile was inside no item's band at all — the tile was skipped and the drop landed one place too far along. Rows are now identified by where items *start*. Reachable since T061 shipped resize; the seeded fixture has only equal-height rows, so only a unit test can see it.
  - The commit used to happen inside a `setDragging` updater. Updaters must be pure: StrictMode invokes them twice, `onMove` builds a fresh document each time, and `push` dedupes only by identity — so one drop put **two** entries on the undo stack and the first Undo would have appeared to do nothing. The live drag now sits in a ref and the commit is a plain statement.
  - The ⋯ menu was being clipped by the tile's own `overflow: hidden`, which an inner `overflow: auto` cannot rescue; on a short tile most of the destination list was simply invisible. The tile stops clipping while its menu is open.
  - **The research doc's condition is specific and was only half met.** It asks for `aria-roledescription` on the grip, persistent instructions referenced by `aria-describedby`, and polite live-region announcements on pick-up, move, drop and cancel. Only the ⋯ menu half had shipped. All of it is now in, announced with readable names rather than ids.
  - Tab groups have no resize handles, because they are given no height of their own; T061's note said `resizeTile` handles a tab group's width "because it can be the target of a resize", and that path is currently unreachable from the UI. The model keeps it for the Outline and Properties work.
  - `DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX` is 5, matching direction.md rule 47's marking gesture. The research doc parks this as a question for the user (rule 47 is about marking, not tile drags); 5 px is taken as the working answer and is one constant to change if the user wants otherwise.

  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/grid/{keyboard.ts,keyboard.test.ts,Canvas.tsx}`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/model/{document.ts,document.test.ts}`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/{DashboardView.tsx,DashboardPage.tsx}`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`, `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`

  - Shift+arrows clamp to exactly the limits a drag clamps to, with a test asserting both routes stop in the same place. The document records what size a tile *is*, never how it came to be that size, so a limit that applied to one route and not the other would let a drag produce a tile the keyboard could never make, or the reverse.
  - The keys follow the prototype in `docs/design/prototype`, with one departure: the prototype opens Properties with Enter, and the criterion names **E** for the chart editor.
  - A key acts only when the **tile itself** has focus, never when one merely bubbled up from inside it. Without that guard, Delete pressed on the tile's own ⋯ button destroyed the tile — verified by removing the guard and watching the test fail — and an arrow key in an open menu moved it. It also protects the text fields that arrive with AC-058's markdown and header tiles.

  - Every tile is a tab stop in edit mode, with `role="group"` and a name that lists only the keys that answer **on that item** — a section answers none of them and a markdown tile has no chart for E to open, so promising otherwise would be a promise the tile does not keep.
  - **A note here previously claimed a lint suppression that does not exist.** It said `jsx-a11y/no-noninteractive-tabindex` was suppressed at that line; there is no `eslint-disable` in the file. The rules pass only because their `allowExpressionValues` option exempts a value that is an expression rather than a literal, so a clean lint was never evidence that either rule had been weighed. The reasoning is now written where the attributes are, saying exactly that. Caught by the cold review, and the third time in this feature that a comment of mine claimed more than the code did.
  - **Worth revisiting:** a thirty-tile dashboard is thirty tab stops, and edit mode now adds a grip and a ⋯ button to each, so it is closer to three per tile. The APG composite pattern with a roving tabindex would collapse all of them — and this repo already implements that pattern in `edit/EditBar.tsx`. One tab stop per tile is what the prototype and dnd-kit both do, so it is sourced rather than invented, but it is not obviously the right end state.
  - A key that changes nothing records nothing. `moveBy` hands back the same document at either end of a container and `resizeTile` does at the size limits, so holding an arrow key against the edge used to pile up undo entries that each undid nothing — the very failure the pointer path already guards against for a click on a resize handle.
  - The keys refuse a container, exactly as the drag does. Delete is the most destructive of the four and AC-057 says "a tile"; taking a section and everything in it with no prompt would also pre-empt AC-061's "ask whether to delete its contents or keep them" in the more destructive direction.

  - Depends on: T064
  - **Built 2026-09-21.** Files as planned, except that the routes are in `api/src/server.ts` beside the others rather than in `routes/uploads.ts`. Plus:
    - core: `images.ts` and its test (the rule);
    - api: migration `1758240003000_images.cjs`, `db/schema.ts`, `repos/images.ts` and its test, `images.test.ts`, `main.ts`;
    - web: `dashboard/model/document.ts` (`imageId`, `alt`, `fit` on a tile, and `changeTile`), `dashboard/edit/TileProperties.tsx`, `dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`, `shell/styles.ts`;
    - `e2e/tiles.spec.ts`;
    - a route-level `bodyLimit` note in `docs/research/api-framework.md`, fetched from Fastify's docs.
    - **One rule, in core** (`imageProblem`), used by the browser before sending and by the server before storing:
      - PNG, JPEG, WebP, GIF or SVG, judged by the file's first bytes as well as its declared type, so a renamed file is refused;
      - at most 2 MB, read as 2,000,000 bytes: the stricter of the decimal and binary readings, so nothing over 2 MB by either is accepted.
    - **Stored with the dashboard** (direction.md §2) means its own table in the metadata database, with each row naming its dashboard. The image isn't inlined in the dashboard document, which it would weigh down on every read and every saved version. It is sent as base64 in JSON, the body the API already parses, on a route whose `bodyLimit` makes room for it. The server's own size check gives the message, because Fastify documents no status for an over-limit body.
    - **Served** as the type it was accepted as, with `nosniff` and `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`. An SVG can carry script: an `<img>` never runs it, but the image's own address can be opened directly.
    - **The Properties panel** (the edit sidebar's third tab, empty until now) shows the selected tile's settings. For an image: its file, its alternative text, and Fit inside or Fill and crop. A refused file is named there (`role="alert"`) and never sent. Each keystroke in the alternative text is an undoable change.
    - **The warning** "⚠ No alternative text" sits on the tile in edit mode until alternative text is set; icon plus label (direction.md rule 3). In view mode there is no warning, and an image with no alternative text is presentational (`alt=""`).
  - **Tests, red first:**
    - core `images.test.ts` (4): each type accepted when it is what it says, including SVG after a byte-order mark; other types refused; renamed files refused; 2,000,000 accepted and 2,000,001 refused.
    - api `images.test.ts` (5): a PNG stored and answered with its id; another type, a renamed file and an oversized one refused with 422 and nothing stored; a malformed body refused; an SVG served as `image/svg+xml` with `nosniff` and the sandboxing policy; an unknown id 404.
    - `repos/images.test.ts` (2), against the real database: bytes read back exactly.
    - web `ImageTile.test.tsx` (5): the warning in edit mode, none with alt text or in view mode, the image fitted; through the dashboard, a PDF and a 2,000,001-byte PNG refused with nothing sent, and an accepted PNG added, its alternative text removing the warning, both saved.
    - `e2e/tiles.spec.ts` (2), on the real app: added from the Add panel; a text file and an oversized PNG refused; a real 1×1 PNG uploaded and decoded (`naturalWidth` 1); warned until given alternative text; after save and reload, shown as an image named by it. The server refuses a renamed file sent straight to it, and serves an accepted PNG back byte for byte.
  - **Known:** direction.md says an image tile "has no tile chrome" in view mode. The tile still has its border and padding, which no criterion checks. Images of a deleted tile stay in the table.
  - **Done 2026-09-21. AC-063 ticked** — `e2e/tiles.spec.ts`, with `ImageTile.test.tsx` and both packages' rule tests.
  - **Cold review: PASS**, with no security defect in upload or serving. Fixed in a follow-up, with tests that were red first:
    - **bug: an upload that finished late undid what was changed meanwhile.** Its answer was applied to the dashboard from the render in which the file was chosen, so alternative text typed during the upload was lost when it landed. The edit session now has `update`, a change applied to the document as it is when the change lands, and the Properties panel uses it. Test: alt text typed while the upload is held survives it and is saved with the image.
    - **low: a body past the upload route's limit gave a 500 "Internal error".** Fastify's `FST_ERR_CTP_BODY_TOO_LARGE` now gets a 413 "Images can be at most 2 MB." Test: a 3 MB image sent straight to the API.
    - **low: one image tile's refusal stayed on screen when another was selected.** The panel is now keyed by tile.
    - **Recorded, not changed:**
      - **Unsourced claims.** The security reasoning (`<img>` never runs an SVG's script; what `sandbox` and `nosniff` do) and the file signatures have no source in `docs/research/`.
      - **"2 MB" is read as 2,000,000 bytes**, which the user has not confirmed. A 2,040,000-byte file reads as about 1.95 MB in binary units but is refused with "this one is 2.0 MB".
      - **Some valid SVGs are refused:** one starting with a comment or DOCTYPE without an XML declaration, or with `<svg` beyond the first 512 bytes.
      - **Images outlive their tile:** no cleanup when a tile or dashboard is deleted, and the test reset does not clear them.
      - **Saving during an upload** saves without the image, and the late answer is then dropped.

The e2e edits, saves, reloads from the server and compares — and a no-op save fails it, which was verified by making Save do nothing and watching it break.

(2) A stored dashboard body is cast (`as Partial<DashboardDocument>`), not parsed, where a chart body goes through strict `readChartDocument`; a body whose `pages` is not an array throws inside render with no error boundary, so close that asymmetry once the write path exists.   - **"Unsaved changes" is judged by content, not by keystrokes.** The page rebuilds its draft on every keystroke, so an identity check would call it dirty the moment anyone touched a field and stay that way after they put it back. A guard that asks about a dashboard that is exactly as it was is one people learn to click through, and then it protects nothing. Both directions are mutation-verified: skipping the guard fails six tests, always asking fails two.

Escape inside the dialog means Keep editing. When Esc-as-exit is built, note that the dialog's window-level listener will intercept first, which is the right precedence.

I had "verified" the token with a grep whose scope included the line I had just written. (2) The guard was only proven for the *name* field; replacing it with `document.title !== draft.title` left all 449 tests green while a mode change was discarded without asking, which is the criterion's failing state exactly. (3) The dialog focused its safe answer in an effect keyed on an inline arrow, so every keystroke on the page re-ran it — and since the dialog does not trap focus, a keyboard user who Tabbed back to the Name field had focus pulled onto "Keep editing" mid-word.
  - **The dialog's accessibility contract is now asserted**, by role and by where focus lands, the way `DeleteSectionDialog`'s is. Before, its role, `aria-modal`, `aria-labelledby` and focus call could all be deleted with the suite staying green.
  - **Known gaps, left for T182 (AC-176) rather than invented here:** focus is not returned to whatever opened the dialog when it closes, and there is no focus trap — `DeleteSectionDialog` has both gaps too. T182's sweep says "every dialog", so it will find them.

If AC-075's width setting gains a live preview through `session.change`, both sides gain it, `isDirty` goes false, and Cancel discards silently.

Once leaving asks first, both go through the prompt. Update them rather than weakening them: the thing to keep proving is that the change does not survive, which is AC-069's failing state either way.

- [x] T084 (AC-078) Re-query every tile in a filter's scope and no tile outside it
  - Files: `packages/web/src/filters/apply.ts`, `packages/web/src/filters/apply.test.ts`

    - **What a value adds to a query** (`withFilters`, with Cube's operators from `docs/research/cube/data-apis.md`), always ANDed with the chart's own:
      - Picked values become `equals`.
      - A numeric range becomes `gte` and/or `lte`.
      - A date range goes into the chart's time dimension's `dateRange` when it has none. That is where Cube's pre-aggregation matching applies (research recommendation 12). A chart that does not use the field gets a time dimension of its own with no granularity.
      - A time dimension that already has a `dateRange` or `compareDateRange` gets an `inDateRange` filter instead, so both limits hold.
      - One end of a date range becomes `afterOrOnDate` or `beforeOrOnDate`.
      - A relative preset is sent as its words.
      - A filter with no field, or with no value that filters (two dates still to be chosen), is not in effect.
    - **Which charts are asked** (`filteredCharts`): every chart tile in scope of a filter in effect, with every filter that reaches it. A chart no filter reaches is not listed, and nothing is asked for it.
    - **Asking** (`FilteredCharts`): one query per reached chart, through the same `tileChartOf` every tile uses. It is keyed by the chart as the tile shows it (a drafted chart as edited) and by what reaches it, so a value set back is answered from the cache. It is cancelled on Cube when overtaken (AC-011). It is mounted only while a filter is in effect, so a dashboard without one asks the query client for nothing. It is outside the edit chrome, so opening the chart editor or the settings page does not re-ask.
    - **Drawn** before formats and colours, so a filtered tile is formatted like any other, and a member keeps its colour when a filter hides others (AC-039). While an answer is on its way, the tile keeps what it drew, dimmed and `aria-busy`, rather than a skeleton, which would unmount the chart and drop a reader's table view. A failed filtered query shows on its tile as any failed query does (AC-012).
    - **Filters set while editing reach the tiles too.** The session's document is mirrored up (`SessionMirror`) so that unsaved filters and scopes apply.

    - `apply.test.ts`, 12 tests: what is in effect (a field and a value; not two empty dates; not a value held for the filter's previous field); what each kind adds (equals beside the chart's own filters; gte/lte with either end open; a preset on a grouped time dimension's `dateRange`; two dates on a time dimension of its own; `inDateRange` where the chart already limits it; one end; the same query back when nothing applies); which charts a value reaches.

  - Files: `packages/web/src/filters/coverage.ts`, `packages/web/src/filters/coverage.test.ts`

  - **Built 2026-09-21.** Files as planned, plus: `filters/NotCovered.tsx`; `filters/coverageOnDashboard.test.tsx`; `filters/apply.ts` (`filteredCharts` is now `coverageOf` with every field reaching every chart); `filters/FilterBar.tsx` (exports `allMembers`); `dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`; `dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx`; `shell/styles.ts`; `e2e/filters.spec.ts`; **`infra/cube/model/views/order_summary.yml`**, the seed model's first view; and a new observation in `docs/research/cube/data-apis.md`.
    - **Reachable** comes from `/v1/meta`:
      - a member of the chart's own cube or view;
      - between two cubes, the same `connectedComponent`, which the docs describe as "at least one join path";
      - through a view, **its own name for a cube's member**, the view member whose `aliasMember` is that member.
    - **The alias was observed, not assumed.** Against the running Cube v1.7.42, `order_summary.status` carries `aliasMember: 'orders.status'`, and the view has no `connectedComponent` (recorded in the research). So a filter on orders' status applies to a chart on the view, as `order_summary.status`. A view that doesn't offer the field is not reachable, and neither is a cube asked for a view's member.
    - **Where the model can't say**, a filter is neither applied nor marked. That covers a model that hasn't arrived, a cube it doesn't have, and a cube with no join components. It is never sent to fail, and never marked on a guess.
    - **An unreachable tile** is never asked with the filter, so no error appears on it, and it says "Not filtered by ‹field›", naming the field as the filter bar does. The mark appears only while a filter holds a value, and goes when the filter is cleared.
    - **The seed view** (`order_summary`: orders' count and status) exists for the browser tests. Cube in production mode reads model files only at start (research §3), so the local Cube container was restarted to load it. The k8s deployment in namespace `wechart` doesn't have it until it is next deployed.

    - `coverage.test.ts` (10): own cube and joined cube, by the field itself; not across join components; a view by its own members and by its alias for a cube member; not a view without the field, nor a cube from a view's member; not a field the model has lost; unknown for a missing cube or view, before the model, or without join components; which charts are filtered, as which member, and which are marked; nothing marked while unknown or out of scope. The contract changed mid-task from yes/no to "the member to filter by" once the alias was observed. Those tests were red against the first version.
    - `coverageOnDashboard.test.tsx` (3): the unreachable tile is never asked, is marked, and shows no alert, while the reachable one is asked; no mark without a value or after Clear; nothing applied or marked while the model can't say.

A filter on orders' status filters the view's tile to one row through `order_summary.status`.

  - **Done 2026-09-21. AC-079 ticked** — `e2e/filters.spec.ts` "a tile a filter cannot reach is left unfiltered and marked, with no error", with `coverageOnDashboard.test.tsx`.
  - **Cold review: FAIL, and it was right.** I had treated a shared `connectedComponent` as proof two cubes can be queried together. The repo's own `markings-query-support.md` (Q4, and its corrections to `data-apis.md`) says it is necessary, not sufficient: the component ignores the joins' direction, and Cube's joins do not. The seed has such a pair. `customers` and `products` share component 1 but both join only *from* `orders`, and Cube answered a customers chart filtered on products' category with **HTTP 400 `Can't find join path to join 'customers', 'products'`** (observed, now in `data-apis.md` next to the corrected line). That tile showed an error, which is AC-079's first failing state. A cube with no join component was left silently unfiltered, the second failing state. Filters also did nothing at all until the model loaded. All fixed in a follow-up commit:
    - **The model now only rules out.** A different component, a view without the field, a field the model lacks, or a view's member on a cube means not reachable, and the tile is marked without asking. The field itself, or a view's alias for it, is reachable. **Everything else is asked of Cube**: two cubes in one component, a cube with no component, or the model not loaded yet.
    - **Cube's refusal decides.** When a filtered query comes back `Can't find join path to join 'a', 'b'`, the filters on the cubes it names (other than the chart's own) are left off. They are marked "Not filtered by …" and the rest are asked again. If none is left, the tile shows its own unfiltered chart. There is no error on the tile, and the other filters still apply. Refused filters are also left out of the empty state's list (AC-043).
    - **A bug found on the way:** the first version read the cube names with a pattern that matched the apostrophe in "Can't", so it named no cubes and dropped every filter. The one-filter test passed anyway, through the "none named, drop all" fallback. The two-filter test ("keeps the filters it can join") caught it.
    - **Tests, red first:**
      - `coverage.test.ts`: one component → Cube answers; a chart the model can't say about is filtered as asked.
      - `coverageOnDashboard.test.tsx`: the refused join leaves the tile unfiltered, marked, not busy, with no alert; a second, joinable filter still applies and is re-asked without the refused one; before the model loads, filters apply.
      - `e2e/filters.spec.ts`, on the real app: a customers chart with products' category is marked and draws every country with no alert; adding a country filter narrows it to one row, the mark still there.
    - **Changed, and recorded (low).** A chart shown by two tiles is now decided by its first tile, even for a filter whose scope takes only the second (`filteredCharts` used to look further). Both behaviours are wrong for such a chart. It can arise only once two tiles share a chart, which T068 must not allow (see its note).
    - **Low, noted.** The earlier view test's "no alert" check passes at once, before any error could arrive. The new test checks for no busy state before checking for alerts.
    - **Full runs:** typecheck and lint clean (the existing DataTable warning aside); 1,423 unit tests and 588 browser runs passed.

  - **Built 2026-09-21.** Files as planned, plus `router/routes.tsx`, `dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx`, `dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`, `e2e/filters.spec.ts`.
    - **The `filters` search parameter** is a list of `{id, member, value}`, one per filter holding a value. It is absent when none does. The router writes it as JSON and parses it back (`docs/research/router-search-history.md`, Q1). The route's `validateSearch` checks every entry and drops anything that is not a value: an unknown preset, a date that is not `yyyy-mm-dd`, non-string picks, a non-numeric range end, a missing id or field. A URL is typed and pasted by hand, so it is never trusted, and a bad entry never throws, which would replace the dashboard with an error.
    - **Read once, written on change.** The dashboard opens with the URL's values, then holds them itself and writes each change back with `replace`: a filter setting is not a place Back should step through. The URL is not the control's live source, because the router applies search changes inside a transition, and typing into a range would lose keystrokes to the lag. The change is reported from an effect, not from inside the state update, which would run twice under StrictMode. The route's callback is stable across renders (`useCallback`), so the effect does not report on every render.
  - **Tests, red first:** `url.test.ts` (5: round trip through JSON, including a value with quotes and commas; absent when none is set; says which filter and field; drops each kind of malformed entry and keeps the good one; nothing from a non-list). `e2e/filters.spec.ts` "the filters in effect are in the URL, and the URL opens with them": a checkbox and a preset, set on the real app, appear in the URL. A **new browser context** opening that URL shows the same box ticked, the other unticked, the same preset, and the tile filtered to one row. Clearing both takes the parameter out. The test also counts URL writes and checks none happen after the values settle. That is a guard only: making the callback unstable did not cause a repeat, because the router does not re-render for an unchanged search, so the check has never caught anything.

So: change a filter, go Forward onto the editor's older entry, close the editor, and the URL held the older filters while the dashboard showed the newer. The dashboard now writes its values whenever the URL comes to say otherwise (`sameValues`), which covers both its own changes and a Back or Forward. The prop is renamed `urlFilterValues` (what the URL holds now) from `initialFilterValues`. Test, red first (the URL read `[shipped]` against a dashboard showing shipped and completed): `e2e/filters.spec.ts` "the URL keeps the dashboard's filters through the chart editor, Back and Forward". It also covers the review's third point: filters stay in the URL across opening and closing the editor.

  - **The cap is four because there are four marking colours, not because four is written down.** direction.md's token table defines `mk1`–`mk4`, each validated to ΔE 15 against every series colour; a fifth marking would have no colour to be. So `MAX_MARKINGS` is derived from `MARKING_SLOTS` and the two cannot drift, and `addMarking` takes the first *free* slot — which means deleting a marking frees its colour and makes the fifth add possible again. There is a test for that.
  - **The three rules are invariants, not validations at the door.** Every function takes a valid set and returns one, so a dashboard is never briefly without a default: rule 43 says new chart tiles use the default, so a moment without one is a moment when a new tile has nothing to mark with. Deleting is where that is most easily lost, and it is not the operation anyone thinks of as being about defaults — mutation-verified by dropping the reassignment.
  - **`markingsFrom` repairs rather than throws.** Markings are persisted, so they arrive with no default, two defaults, or five; a dashboard whose markings are wrong is otherwise a perfectly good dashboard, and refusing to open it would lose more than the markings. `problemsWith` states the rules once, with a positive control asserting it finds nothing wrong with a valid set — a function that always complained would have satisfied every other case.
  - **It also resolved a scope concern T076 raised.** T076 had improvised `Marking` in the web package without a colour slot; that type is deleted and the real one imported, which immediately caught two fixtures missing `slot`.

What *is* mutation-verified: the conjunction (matching any one member instead of all fails five cases), items as alternatives, inclusive range bounds, and the rejection of an empty tuple — which, being a conjunction of nothing, would mark every row on the dashboard.

The 5 px threshold is rule 47's, and the boundary is asserted at exactly 5 (`>=`, not `>`), which an off-by-one mutation catches.

  - Files: `packages/charts/src/interaction/{dragOverlay,hitTest}.ts`, `packages/charts/src/interaction/hitTest.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/index.ts`
  - Failing test: `hitTest.test.ts`: a rectangle and a lasso each mark every item they touch via `convertToPixel` hit-testing, a drag of at least 5 px is never a click, and a press-and-release without movement is (AC-088).

  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-088 ticked. The geometry is pure and takes pixels; the conversion is `convertToPixel` at the call site, which `docs/research/echarts.md` records as documented and stable and names as what this must be built on, because ECharts' own brush does not cover our series mix.

Tested apart they can both fire or neither at the boundary, and both are AC-088's failing states — one marks twice, the other leaves the marking untouched while a rectangle is drawn on screen. Mutation-verified: changing the drag threshold from `>=` to `>` produces `{clicked: 0, dragged: 0}` at exactly 5 px, a drag that does nothing at all. `dragOverlay` imports `CLICK_SLOP_PX` from `itemClick` so there is one threshold rather than two that can drift.

  - **Ray casting for the lasso, because it is correct for concave shapes**, which is most of why anyone lassos rather than drags a rectangle. There is a C-shaped test whose gap must stay unmarked; a bounding-box implementation would be a rectangle with extra steps.
  - **Once a drag, always a drag**: dragging out and coming back ends as a drag, not a click, or the release would mark whatever happened to sit under it. A rectangle is defined by where it began and ended; a lasso keeps every point visited, so the shape is the path drawn rather than a box around it.

- [x] T099 (AC-093) Mark a continuous range by dragging along an axis, shown as a stripe with stable bounds
  - Files: `packages/core/src/markings/range.ts`, `packages/core/src/markings/range.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/interaction/axisRange.ts`, `packages/charts/src/interaction/axisRange.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/index.ts`, `packages/charts/src/index.ts`
  - Failing test: `range.test.ts`: an axis drag stores a bounded range, renders a coloured stripe on that axis, and the bounds are unchanged after a filter change (AC-093).

  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-093 ticked.
  - **Why the stability clause exists**, from `references-markings.md` quoting Spotfire: a marked range "does not take active filtering and data limitations into consideration". A range derived from the extent of the data on screen would rescale on every filter change, so the same stripe would cover a different span of values from one moment to the next and two readings of one dashboard would not be comparable. The bounds are two values on the axis and nothing else.
  - **The stripe is widened when it would otherwise vanish.** "The stripe does not appear" is the criterion's first failing state, and a range narrower than a pixel on a zoomed-out axis reaches it by arithmetic rather than by anyone forgetting to draw it. Mutation-verified. The widening goes away from the far edge so it cannot push the stripe off the axis.
  - **A range entirely off the axis draws nothing**, rather than clamping — a clamped stripe would sit at the edge advertising a marking that is not on this axis at all.
  - **A backwards drag is the same range.** Dragging right to left is the same intention, and a range that ended before it started would match nothing: a marking that silently does nothing, which is worse than one that refuses. Mutation-verified.
  - The pixel conversion stays at the call site (`convertToPixel` / `convertFromPixel`), as with the hit-testing geometry, so the part worth testing needs no chart instance — and so the stripe can be asserted to come from the stored bounds rather than from the current result.

Tabs load lazily, so a tile on an unopened tab does not exist yet — and the obvious design, telling each mounted tile when a marking changes, works for everything on screen and silently fails for everything that is not: the tab opens later and renders whatever it was last told, or nothing. - [x] T103 (AC-097) Draw partial marking as a baseline share for additive measures and a pattern otherwise
  - Files: `packages/charts/src/marking/partial.ts`, `packages/charts/src/marking/partial.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/index.ts`
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-097 ticked.
  - **The criterion where drawing the obvious thing is wrong.** Splitting a bar in proportion to the marked rows looks right for every measure and is only *true* when the measure sums across rows. Half the rows of an average do not make half the average; the distinct customers in a marked subset are not a fraction of the distinct customers overall, because they overlap. A sub-length there is a confident, readable, precisely drawn lie — so non-additive measures get a pattern and the exact numbers in the tooltip, which says what is known without implying what is not.
  - **`countDistinctApprox` is deliberately not additive, and the research says the opposite.** `docs/research/cube/data-modeling.md` calls it "additive and rollup-friendly", which is true of merging HyperLogLog sketches across rollups and false of what matters here. Two meanings of one word; taking the research's meaning would draw exactly the lie the criterion forbids. The test says so, so nobody "corrects" it later by citing the research.
  - **An unknown `aggType` is not additive.** A sub-length is a claim about the data, and one should not be made about a measure nothing is known about.
  - Mutation-verified twice: adding `avg` and `countDistinctApprox` to the additive set fails five tests, and dropping the zero-total guard fails the two that keep a measure summing to zero from being drawn as partly marked.

- [x] T104 (AC-098) Convey marking state beyond colour with tile badges and marking chips
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/markings/{MarkBadge,MarkChip}.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/markings/MarkBadge.test.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`
  - Failing test: `MarkBadge.test.tsx`: every tile using a non-empty marking shows a badge naming it and the marked-item count, and every non-empty marking shows a chip naming it and summarising its values (AC-098).
  - Depends on: T102
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-098 ticked.
  - **This criterion is what makes the marking palette legal**, not a courtesy. direction.md's colour audit records dark-mode `mk1`↔`mk4` at ΔE 6.8 under colour-blindness simulation — inside the 6–8 floor band, which is allowed "only with secondary encoding, which chips and badges provide by always naming the marking". Remove the name and the count and the colours themselves stop being defensible.
  - **The swatch is `aria-hidden` and the text carries the meaning.** Announced as well as drawn, a screen reader would read a colour and learn nothing. Mutation-verified both ways: removing the text fails two tests, exposing the swatch fails one.
  - **The chip summarises values rather than counting them.** "Marking (2)" names the marking and says nothing about what is in it — the same failure as colour alone, where you can see that something is marked but not what. Values are written `member = a, b` only when every marked item names the same single member; a tuple across two dimensions cannot be written that way without misstating it, so those are counted instead. An honest count beats a readable untruth.
  - **Neither appears when nothing is marked.** Rule 50 is about tiles using a *non-empty* marking, and "0 marked" on every tile of an untouched dashboard is noise that would teach people to ignore the badge — the one thing it cannot afford.
  - **The `/jobs` contrast flake reached five sightings** during this task and then passed on a clean full-suite run. Still uncaptured. See T101's note: the next step is to capture axe's violation, not to harden the wait.
  - **2026-09-20, sighting six, and a hypothesis worth more than another hunt.** Seven further full-suite runs failed to reproduce it (two of those logs are void — I was editing test files while they ran, so their failures are my own mutations). Two things are now eliminated by reading rather than by repetition: the shell stylesheet cannot be late, because `installStyles()` runs before `createRoot().render()` in `main.tsx`, so the banner cannot exist before the tokens are in `<head>`; and the spec in isolation passes 216 runs at 6 workers.
  - **The remaining hypothesis is that it is not a flake at all.** Under `pnpm -r test` the API is being hammered by the api package's own tests, so `/jobs` may render a **loading, empty or error state that the happy path never reaches** — and the audit then sees those colours for the only time. "Unstyled" elements (`.query-log__error`, `[data-icon]`) were eliminated as *rules*, but unstyled means *inherited*, and inherited secondary ink on a tinted status background is exactly how a contrast failure of this shape arises. **Next step: render `/jobs` in its loading, empty and error states deliberately and run the contrast audit on each.** That converts an unreproducible flake into a deterministic test, and if it finds nothing, the API-load theory is dead too.
  - **A sixth sighting, and a second page (2026-09-21, T197):** the same AC-005 audit failed on `/explore` (Chrome, light theme) under the full suite's load, then passed 270 of 270 runs of the contrast spec on its own. A failure on a second page fits the load-dependent-state hypothesis above better than anything specific to `/jobs`; the next step stands, for both pages.
