  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-099 ticked, **after a research step and four user decisions** — the criterion was not executable as written.
  - **What the research found before any code was written.** AC-099 names a threshold but no unit, no colour space and no algorithm; "OKLab" appeared nowhere in the repo. `validate_palette.js`, cited twice by direction.md, **is not in this repository**, so the figures it quotes (19.6, 9.1, 8.4, 6.8, 9.0) come from a tool we do not have and cannot be reproduced — our numbers are a new measurement, and the test says so. And the thresholds 15 and 8 have **no published basis**: no just-noticeable-difference exists for any OKLab ΔE, and CIELAB's familiar ≈2.3 does not transfer because the lightness axes differ by a factor of 100.
  - **User decisions, 2026-09-20:** ΔEOK ×100; Viénot, Brettel & Mollon 1999 at full dichromacy; raw tokens against the eight categorical slots; thresholds recorded in direction.md as product decisions. direction.md has been amended accordingly, including that `other`, the 0.28 fade composite and marking↔marking are all deliberately outside the criterion.
  - **The mutation that justified the whole research step.** Applying the CVD matrix to gamma-encoded sRGB rather than linear — which the research calls "the most widely replicated bug in the ecosystem" — does not throw and does not look wrong. It produces a **false failure**: `mk4` against the red slot reads ΔE 4.5 under protan instead of passing, so the palette would have been "fixed" by changing a colour that was fine. There is now a test pinning the order.
  - **Checked against other people's numbers, not its own.** Fixtures come from culori's test suite (hex→OKLab), Ottosson's published table, and structural invariants readable straight off the Viénot matrices — red and green collapse, blue is fixed, the neutral axis is fixed — which cannot inherit an implementation bug the way a recomputed expected value can.
  - **The dark categorical ramp existed only in direction.md.** AC-099 audits both themes, so it is now in `tokens.ts` beside the marking tokens, and `DEFAULT_PALETTE` in charts re-exports the light ramp instead of keeping a second copy — otherwise the audit could pass on a palette the renderer does not use. That needed `@wechart/ui` as a charts dependency; `ui` has none, so the graph stays acyclic.
  - Failing test: `markingPalette.test.ts`: the validator reports every marking colour at ΔE ≥ 15 from each series colour under normal vision and ΔE ≥ 8 under protan and deutan simulation, in both themes (AC-099).
  - Depends on: T002, T102
- [x] T106 (AC-100) Mark from the keyboard on a focused tile
  - Files: `packages/charts/src/interaction/keyboardMarking.ts`, `packages/charts/src/interaction/keyboardMarking.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/index.ts`
  - Failing test: `keyboardMarking.test.ts`: arrow keys move between items without changing the marking, Space marks the focused item, Ctrl/⌘+Space toggles it and Shift+arrows extend the marking (AC-100).
  - Depends on: T102
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-100 ticked. **All of it is ours**: `docs/research/echarts.md` records that ECharts generates no roles for data items, no focus order and no keyboard interaction, that its documented instance API has no focus or keyboard method, and that its own legend buttons are an open accessibility bug ([#21022]).
  - **Arrows move focus without marking, and that is a performance contract as well as the APG rule.** direction.md gives the reason: "each marking change re-queries other tiles". Arrowing along fifty categories with marking-follows-focus would fire fifty rounds of queries across every tile using the marking — so "select as you move" would make the keyboard path unusable in a way the mouse path never is. Mutation-verified.
  - **Tab is not handled**, and there is a test for it. A tile that swallowed Tab would be a keyboard trap, which is the one accessibility failure that makes a page unusable rather than merely awkward. The keys the tile *does* act on report that they were handled, so the page does not also scroll.
  - **Focus clamps at the ends rather than wrapping.** Wrapping from the last item to the first reads as a jump to anyone not watching the chart, and the live region would announce a category they did not expect.
  - **Shift-extend runs from the anchor, not the previous position**, so a second Shift-arrow grows or shrinks the same range; measured from the last step it could never shrink.
- [x] T107 (AC-101) Announce marking changes in a polite live region
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/markings/LiveRegion.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/markings/LiveRegion.test.tsx`
  - Failing test: `LiveRegion.test.tsx`: each marking change announces the marking's name, the number of marked items and the source tile (AC-101).
  - Depends on: T101
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-101 ticked.
  - **Why the criterion singles out the count.** Marking is otherwise an entirely visual event — items fade, an outline appears — so this is the only channel carrying it to someone not watching the chart. "Marking changed" *is* an announcement and says nothing anyone can act on. Mutation-verified: dropping the count fails four tests.
  - **The region renders even when empty.** Assistive technology watches the node, and a live region added at the same moment as its content is commonly not announced at all — so returning `null` for an empty message would make the *first* announcement of a session silently vanish. Mutation-verified, and it is the kind of bug that never reproduces for whoever wrote it.
  - **Clearing says "cleared", not "0 items marked".** The zero is arithmetically right and reads as a failure; clearing is something the user did on purpose, so the announcement confirms the act. The marking is still named, because four can be live at once and "cleared" alone leaves them guessing which one went.
  - **Polite, not assertive**: marking is something the user has just done, and interrupting someone to report their own action is what makes people switch live regions off. `aria-atomic` so "3 items" becoming "4 items" reads as a sentence rather than as the word that changed.
- [x] T108 (AC-102) Refuse items past the 1,000-item limit, exempting "Mark all"
  - Files: `packages/core/src/markings/limit.ts`, `packages/core/src/markings/limit.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/index.ts`
  - Failing test: `limit.test.ts`: adding past 1,000 items is refused with an explanation, while "Mark all" succeeds because it stores the tile's filter rather than a list (AC-102).
  - Depends on: T092
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-102 ticked.
  - **Two documents disagree about the number, and the code says which it followed.** `docs/spec.md` fixes it at 1,000; `docs/design/direction.md` still lists "The item cap. Spotfire's in-db default is 1,000" under **"Still open for the user"**. The spec is the contract, so 1,000 is implemented and the constant records the provenance. **If the user settles on another figure, the constant and AC-102 move together.**
  - **Five ACs cite "rule N of §7a" and §7a has no numbered rules.** AC-102 cites rule 29 of §7a; the global rule 29 is about "Why this chart?" traces. The same broken citation appears at rules 7, 10, 18 and 30 of §7a. Each AC's own text is complete, so nothing is blocked — but the references point nowhere.
  - **Items past the cap are dropped, not the whole gesture.** A lasso over 1,050 points is a reasonable thing to do and mostly succeeds; throwing all 1,050 away would punish someone for the size of their data and leave them with nothing marked. Mutation-verified.
  - **Re-adding something already marked is not growth**, or the cap would refuse items while leaving room unused.
  - **"Mark all"'s exemption is structural and the tests say so.** It takes a predicate and returns it, so there is no enumeration that could reach the cap and no mutation that makes it refuse without changing its signature — routing it through the limit leaves every test green, because adding nothing to anything refuses nothing. The cases hold the *shape* of the exemption, which is what a future version could break by enumerating the tile's rows instead.
- [x] T109 (AC-103) Label a tile as not linked when it cannot resolve a marking's members
  - Files: `packages/core/src/markings/applicability.ts`, `packages/core/src/markings/applicability.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/index.ts`
  - Failing test: `applicability.test.ts`: when a tile's cube or view cannot resolve a predicate member, the tile renders unmarked with a "not linked" label and no query error; join-path applicability is checked with `/v1/dry-run` (AC-103).
  - Depends on: T101
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-103 ticked.
  - **Both failing states are about what the user is told, and they are the two ways of telling them badly.** A query error blames them for something they did not do — the marking is fine, the tile is fine, they simply do not meet. Silence is worse: a tile sitting unbrushed among brushed ones looks like a tile with *nothing marked in it*, which is a different and wrong answer. So the check runs before a query is sent, not after one fails.
  - **The label names the missing members.** "Not linked" alone says the dashboard is not doing what was expected and nothing about why; the member name turns it into something fixable, usually a missing join. Every missing member is listed, because two missing joins are a different conversation from one.
  - **A caveat recorded from the research, and it is a security one.** `docs/research/cube/access-control.md` says `/v1/dry-run` returns "the normalized queries from *before* RLS and `queryRewrite`", so a member a security context would hide still looks resolvable. Fine for *linking*, which asks whether a join path exists — but **this must never be read as an authorisation check**, and the module says so.
  - **Mutation testing found dead code.** An early return for the empty-marking case turned out to change nothing: an empty marking names no members, so the general path already reports it linked. Removed, with the reasoning kept on the line that actually does the work and the behaviour still held by a test.

- [x] T111 (AC-105) Canonicalise the query so an identical marking produces identical query JSON
  - Files: `packages/core/src/markings/query.ts`, `packages/core/src/markings/query.test.ts`, `packages/web/src/filters/FilteredCharts.tsx`, `e2e/markings.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `e2e/markings.spec.ts`: two markings covering the same set, built in different orders, send byte-identical query JSON (AC-105).
  - Depends on: T110
  - Done 2026-09-23 with T113. AC-105 ticked.
  - **It landed in `markings/query.ts`, not the planned `query/canonical.ts`.** Canonicalising a whole query after the fact would have meant a second traversal that knows every shape a query can take; the only part that is order-dependent is the marking, so the conversion emits it sorted and nothing downstream has to care.
  - **Sorted by each filter's own JSON**, which needs no rule per shape: two filters that mean the same thing serialise the same, so the key is a total order over exactly the equivalences that matter. The comparator returns a real `0` on ties — a sort given an inconsistent comparator may order equal elements differently on different inputs, which is precisely the instability this criterion is about.
  - **The cache key carries the limit** (`filteredQueryKey`), so the same marks marked again are the same React Query key and the answer comes back without a request at all.
  - **Not canonical *across* markings:** the branches follow the tile's `limitedBy` order. That is stable per tile, so AC-105 holds, but reordering a tile's limit list would change its cache key once — recorded rather than fixed, because the order is also the order the user reads in the control.

## F11. Details tiles and marked-rows actions

- [x] T113 (AC-107) Limit a tile by markings, combined as AND or OR, skipping empty markings
  - Files: `packages/core/src/markings/limit-by.ts`, `packages/core/src/markings/limit-by.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/index.ts`
  - Failing test: `limit-by.test.ts`: two limiting markings with one empty return the rows of the non-empty one, and OR returns the union where AND returns the intersection (AC-107).
  - Depends on: T092
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-107 ticked.
  - **The skip-empty rule is ours, and direction.md flags it as such**: "Proposed rule, not from Spotfire's docs." It matters because the naive reading is catastrophic under AND — a details tile limited by two markings, where the user has marked in only one, would intersect against an empty set and show nothing. The tile goes blank exactly when someone starts working, and the remedy ("also mark something in the other master tile") is not a thing anyone would guess. Mutation-verified.
  - **Nothing marked anywhere returns every row, not none.** What the tile then *shows* is a per-tile setting — All data, an empty visualisation, or a message, with the message the default — so the decision belongs to whoever renders it. Returning nothing here would make "All data" impossible to implement.
  - Rows come back by identity and in their original order: the tile's own sort is the tile's business, and identity is what lets a renderer tell whether a row actually changed rather than repainting everything on each marking change.
  - **Connected 2026-09-23 — and the limit went into the query, not into the rows.** `limitRowsBy` filters rows that Cube has already aggregated, so a limited tile using it would draw the marked rows with every total computed over *everything*: a "total" that is not the total of what is shown. `packages/core/src/markings/query.ts` converts the predicate to a Cube filter instead (single-member items collapse to one `equals`, multi-member tuples become an OR of ANDs, ranges become `gte`/`lte`), which `FilteredCharts` appends to `query.filters` — where Cube ANDs the entries, which is AC-082 with no second mechanism. Nested `{or}`/`{and}` are supported per `docs/research/cube/data-apis.md`; that research also warns that dimension and measure filters may not share a logical operator, so a range over a measure is left for the caller to place. Evidence: `e2e/markings.spec.ts` "a limited tile shows only what is marked" and "an empty marking limiting a tile is skipped, not intersected". AC-107 re-ticked.
  - **A limited tile is asked even when no filter reaches it**, and its limit is never dropped to rescue a refused query — a dashboard filter that cannot be joined is one the user did not aim at this tile, but a limit they did, and a tile that quietly dropped it would show unmarked rows in a tile whose whole purpose is to show marked ones.
  - **Three encoding rules the cold review caught, each a silent wrong answer.** A marked *time bucket* was emitted as `equals` on the raw member with the bucket's start instant — so marking a month bar asked for rows at exactly midnight on the 1st, and the details tile went quietly empty; `markings-query-support.md` §1.2 says a bucket is `inDateRange` over `["…T00:00:00.000","…T23:59:59.999"]`, which is what it now sends. A marked *NULL* became `equals ['']`, which is `col = ''` — matching empty strings and never NULLs (§1.5 says `notSet`, and §1.6 puts it in an `or` beside the other values of that member, which blocks the single-`equals` collapse). And `predicate.ts` had already written the instruction — "The task that translates a predicate into a Cube filter needs that answer and should research it" — which the first version did not follow. All four cases are now tested.
  - **One rule recorded rather than fixed:** a `BoundedRange` over a *measure* cannot share a logical operator with a dimension filter (`data-apis.md`:226), and `rangeFilter` cannot tell a measure from a dimension — only the model can. Nothing produces such a range yet, because range mode is AC-093 and unbuilt; the constraint is written at the function and in the research doc's open questions, for AC-093 to supply the member kinds.
  - **The filter is canonical, which is what earns AC-105.** Values within an `equals`, members within an item and branches within a marking are sorted by their own JSON, so the same *set* of marks is the same query however it was clicked. Unsorted, clicking A then B and B then A were two cache entries for one question, and the second went to the database for an answer already in memory. Evidence: `e2e/markings.spec.ts` "the same set of marks asks Cube the same question, whatever order it was marked in", which compares the bodies the page actually sent.
  - **The `/jobs` contrast flake, sixth sighting, with two hypotheses eliminated.** Stressing `a11y-tokens.spec.ts` alone — 216 runs at 6 workers — passes every time, so it is contention with the *rest* of the suite rather than a timing problem inside that spec. And the query log's error text, the obvious content that only appears once other specs have run, carries no colour rule at all (`.query-log__error` and `[data-icon]` are unstyled), so it is not that either. Still uncaptured; next attempt should snapshot axe's violation from a full-suite run rather than re-testing the spec in isolation.
- [x] T114 (AC-108) Prevent a tile from being limited by the marking it marks with, and say why
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/settings/TileLimits.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/settings/TileLimits.test.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`
  - Failing test: `TileLimits.test.tsx`: the tile's own marking is not selectable as a limit and the reason is shown where the option appears (AC-108).
  - Depends on: T113
  - Done 2026-09-20. AC-108 ticked.
  - **Why the rule exists, recorded because the control looks arbitrary without it.** A tile limited by its own marking feeds back on itself: marking two bars restricts the tile to those two bars, the others vanish, and with them any way to change the marking. The tile locks itself, and the only escape is a settings page the user has no reason to connect to what just happened.
  - **"Prevent" is stronger than "do not offer", so it is enforced twice** — the control disables the option, and `allowedLimits` drops it from any stored list. A document can arrive hand-edited or from a build predating the rule, and a UI that merely hid the option would apply it anyway, locking the tile with no control on screen to unlock it. Both guards are independently mutation-verified.
  - **The reason sits beside the option**, as the criterion requires ("where that option is offered"). A disabled control with no explanation is the most frustrating kind: the user cannot tell whether it is broken, forbidden, or waiting on something they have not done. Options that are *not* disabled carry no explanation, or people learn to skip the one that matters.
  - `allowedLimits` returns its input by identity when nothing had to be removed, so a document that was already valid is not rewritten — which would make the edit session think it had changed.
  - **Connected 2026-09-23, and one real bug came out of it.** Both guards read `marksWith` as stored, and a tile that names no marking marks with the dashboard's *default* — which is most tiles. So on a real dashboard nothing was barred: the e2e found the own-marking checkbox enabled on the master tile. `TileLimits` now resolves the default the way `TileMarking` does, and `DashboardView` resolves it before calling `allowedLimits`. AC-108 re-ticked on `e2e/markings.spec.ts` "a tile cannot be limited by the marking it marks with, and is told why".
  - **The limit checkboxes are named "Limit by &lt;marking&gt;".** Beside the tile's "Marking" picker, a checkbox whose whole accessible name is the marking's name is a second control carrying the first one's name — neither a screen reader nor a test can tell them apart. The visible text is still the marking's name, so the accessible name contains it (WCAG 2.5.3).
  - **Labels are tied by `id`, not by wrapping.** A `<label>` around a `<select>` takes its options into the accessible name, so "When nothing is marked" was really "When nothing is markedAll dataEmpty visualization…" — which no exact query can reach, and which a screen reader reads out in full.
- [x] T115 (AC-109) Show the configured nothing-marked state when every limiting marking is empty
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/tiles/NothingMarked.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/tiles/NothingMarked.test.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`
  - Failing test: `NothingMarked.test.tsx`: each of all data, empty visualization and message renders exactly as set on the tile (AC-109).
  - Depends on: T113
  - **Component landed 2026-09-20. AC-109 deliberately NOT ticked, and the task left open.** The cold review found that the criterion's subject is the *tile* — "show **the tile's** nothing-marked state … as set on the tile", failing when "**the tile** shows a state other than the one configured" — and no tile can be in that state yet. `Tile` in `model/document.ts` has no nothing-marked field, and nothing in the app imports this component. Every test here passes `state` as a prop, so the suite demonstrates the component, not the criterion. Ticking it would have recorded a behaviour no user can reach. See the open question logged under "Marking subsystem is unwired" below.
  - **The message is stored on the tile, not composed at render.** Rule 58 and §7a say *pre-filled* with the master tiles' names (rule 33 itself lists only the three options), and pre-filled text is text the user may then edit. The first version of this component built the sentence from a `masters` prop on every render, which would have silently discarded that edit — the user would have typed a new message, saved, and watched it revert with no error to explain it. `defaultNothingMarkedMessage(masters)` now supplies the pre-fill and nothing calls it at render; T116 calls it once, when a tile is first limited. Mutation-verified.
  - **`allLimitsEmpty`, not `nothingMarkedApplies`.** The first name invited the one composition that silently disables the feature: core's `limitingMarkings` returns the markings that *actually restrict* something — the non-empty ones — so `allLimitsEmpty(limitingMarkings(xs))` is false for every input, and the nothing-marked state would simply never appear with no error to trace. Named for what it asserts rather than for the state it detects, so the mistake reads as wrong at the call site, and pinned by a test that composes the two on purpose.
  - **Copy fix the first tests stepped around.** With no master names the message read "Nothing is marked. Mark something **there** to fill this tile." — "there" with no antecedent. The branch is reachable: a tile keeps its `limitedBy` when the master tile is deleted, and a marking no tile marks with is legal. The old assertion (`not.toContain('in .')`) passed happily on it, because a test that can only see a *malformed* sentence cannot see an *unreadable* one. Now asserts the exact string, plus a rule that "there" never appears without names before it.
  - **No `role="status"` on the message.** This paragraph mounts at the same moment as its text, and the project's own `MarkingAnnouncer` already records what that costs: "a region added at the same moment as its content is commonly not announced at all". A role that does nothing is worse than none, because it reads as covered. AC-101's announcer is the thing that speaks.
  - **This is the decision T113 deliberately refused to make.** `limitRowsBy` returns *every* row when nothing is marked anywhere, and that looked like a bug until this task: had it returned no rows there, "All data" would have been impossible to implement without a second code path. The empty-marking case is not a filter result, it is a question about intent, and the tile answers it.
  - **Message is the default** (rule 58, user decision 2026-09-19), and it earns that. A details tile silently showing everything reads as unfiltered data; one silently showing nothing reads as broken. Only the message distinguishes "waiting for you" from "wrong", and the tile the user has to go and click may be on another page tab, so the sentence names it.
  - **The state set on the tile wins over the message it holds**, so a tile switched to "Empty visualization" does not leak the text it was given while it was on "Message". Verified by rendering all three states from identical input.
  - **"All data" renders nothing at all** rather than a transparent wrapper. The criterion's failing state is showing a state other than the one configured, and a wrapper that has to be styled to disappear is one stylesheet change away from being visible.
  - Not the same thing as a marking whose items are all *filtered out* — direction.md keeps those apart because the remedies differ: one wants a mark, the other wants a filter changed. AC-111 (T117) covers that case and must take precedence over this one.
  - Both states fill the tile (`height: 100%`), so the tile keeps its size in the grid instead of collapsing and shoving its neighbours around every time a mark is cleared.
  - **Connected 2026-09-23, and AC-109 ticked then.** `Tile` gained `nothingMarked` and `nothingMarkedMessage`; `DashboardView` computes a `LimitedState` per chart and `TileBody` renders it ahead of loading, missing and broken, because a tile waiting on a mark has nothing to load. `'all'` never reaches the component: the dashboard leaves the state undefined for it, so "All data" is literally the tile drawing normally rather than a wrapper that has to be styled away. Evidence: `e2e/markings.spec.ts` "the nothing-marked state is the one set on the tile, not one state for all", which walks the three options through the tile's own Properties control — the criterion's failing state is "a state other than the one **configured**", so a test that passes the state as a prop could never have shown it.

Cross-filter mode has no markings anywhere (AC-120), so a marking limit has nothing to mean there.

The tile marked whole draws every item marked; another tile matches its rows against those filters; a tile limited by the marking gets them as a filter group, and an unfiltered whole limits nothing. A Ctrl-click on a wholly marked tile turns it back into its items first; Invert of a whole tile marks none of it.   - Create details tile (T118) names every tile that marks with the marking (AC-110), and makes no change when there is nothing to make.

## F12. Cross-filtering and cross-highlighting

- [x] T123 (AC-117) Apply the configured action for each source and target pair
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/crossfilter/apply.ts`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/crossfilter/apply.test.ts`
  - Failing test: `apply.test.ts`: a pair set to Filter filters the target, one set to Highlight highlights it, and one set to None leaves it unchanged (AC-117).
  - Depends on: T076, T084
  - **Built 2026-09-23.**
    - **What Highlight draws — the user's decision, 2026-09-22.** Asked how a highlighted target should look, the user chose Power BI's: the tile keeps its own values, faded, and the selection's own values are drawn over them from the baseline, from a second query. Not a share of the tile's total: a measure over a subset is not a fraction of the measure over everything, which is why it is Cube's answer rather than arithmetic here. The research is `docs/research/cross-highlight.md` (written for this task; it also corrects `references.md`, which said every target offers Filter / Highlight / None — Power BI offers Highlight only where the target can show one).
    - **Where each drawing applies.** `share` on a plain bar or horizontal bar, where the selection has a slot of its own: `barGap: '-100%'` is a property of the whole grid, so on a grouped or stacked chart the overlay would land on every series at once (that research, gotcha 4). `fade` — the matching items keep their strength, the rest go back — on grouped, stacked and combo bars, pie, donut, treemap and heatmap, and only where the tile groups by the clicked member, since otherwise no item on it is the selection. Everything else (lines, area, scatter, KPI, table, pivot, trellis) is **filtered** instead, as Power BI filters lines, scatter and maps. A pair asking for a highlight a tile cannot draw filters it rather than leaving it alone: the pair asked for the selection to reach it.
    - **A cross-filter is written as a filter** (`DashboardView` `crossFilters`), scoped to the tiles the matrix says it filters, so it takes the path every dashboard filter takes: one query per tile, its reach checked against the model, and a tile whose cube cannot join to the field left unfiltered and told so — the user's second decision, and the same note AC-079 already shows.
    - **Clicking.** `mousedown` and `mouseup` paired with the existing 5 px slop (`itemClicks`), never ECharts' `click`, which `docs/research/echarts.md` finds is dropped after 4 px of movement with nothing dispatched at all. What the click stands for follows the prototype: the category on a bar or pie, the series on a grouped, stacked or multi-line chart, the panel in a trellis. One selection per tile, and the same value again takes it back, as Power BI and Superset both do.
    - **Off where it should be:** in Markings mode, while editing (a click selects the tile there), and in a previewed security context. Selections are held in the view, not in the document: clicking a mark is reading the dashboard, not changing it.
    - **Tests:**
      - `e2e/crossfilter.spec.ts` clicks a real bar — the mark, as the criterion says — on a two-tile dashboard, and checks the other tile filtered, highlighted (its own rows kept, the share drawn), or untouched, plus clicking the same bar again taking it back.
      - `crossfilter/apply.test.ts` (the pair decision, the default, two sources at once, and which drawing each type gets), `crossfilter/clicked.test.ts` (what a click stands for), `charts/src/crossfilter/highlight.test.ts` (both drawings).
    - AC-117 ticked on those. **AC-118 stays unticked:** the default applies here and is tested, but the criterion also asks the settings page to show it, which is T124's matrix.
    - **Cold review (one round), verdict FAIL. Fixed:**
      - **A click on a combo or multi-measure chart made a filter no row matches.** Where such a chart has no colour split its series are its measures, and the name under the pointer was paired with the x member: `orders.status = 'orders.count'`, which emptied every target it reached. Such a click now selects nothing.
      - **The share overlay mis-drew any bar tile with more than one series.** The gate was the chart type, but a bar chart draws one series per measure; `barGap: '-100%'` belongs to the whole grid, so the overlay would have landed on every series. It now gates on the series count, and such a tile fades or is filtered instead.
      - **A heatmap lost its cells under a fade.** Its data items are `[column, row, value]` triples, and spreading an array into an object dropped the value ECharts reads. Arrays are carried as the item's `value`, and a cell's column is matched by index.
      - **A highlight Cube could not join to the tile drew nothing, and said nothing.** It now leaves the same "Not filtered by …" note a filter leaves — the user's decision — and is asked by the name a view takes the member by.
      - **Axis, legend and title clicks cleared the selection.** The handlers are bound to the marks alone.
      - **A chart placed on two tiles could cross-filter itself** through its other tile.
      - **Coverage added:** the unreachable target (filtered and highlighted), and cross-filtering being off while editing, both on the real surface.
    - **Open, from the same review:** a highlighted tile's overlay is not re-asked by "Refresh now" (the base answer is); selections survive a page-tab switch and a mode switch as inert state, which T125 should settle with the chip; and clicking an empty plot area does not clear, which is AC-119's.
    - **Left for the tasks that own them:** the chip naming the member, value and source tile, and Escape (AC-119, T125); the settings matrix (AC-118, T124); no marking affordances in cross-filter mode (AC-120, T126).
- [x] T124 (AC-118) Apply and display the dashboard's default pair action
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/settings/CrossFilterMatrix.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/crossfilter/defaults.test.ts`

Tiles are named by the chart each draws.

Stored as "whatever the default was when the page was opened", AC-118's failing state would be reachable by changing the default later.

It now also finds a tile's `chartId` at any depth (`jsonb_path_exists` with `strict $.**`, checked against PostgreSQL 18's docs and the local database).

Tests that used a library draft only as an example of an unsaveable one now use another dashboard's chart, which is still refused.

      - **The notice's hide rule is tested.** `e2e/save-to-folder.spec.ts` opens a local chart's editor, and one being saved to a folder, and finds no notice.

      - **The count** is the other dashboards plus this one (`dashboardId`), so a chart this dashboard shows only in the session still counts the one other dashboard that does.
      - **Three stale comments** about the hold.
    - **Noted, not fixed:**
      - The count query walks every dashboard's body on each editor open, library page view and delete. That is fine at phase-1 size. A kept list of referenced chart ids, indexed, would scale.
      - `silent` makes a path error read as "no dashboards use it".

      - The ≤1 case's clock icon differs from the prototype's plain note.

## F15. Model editor (Cube YAML)

- [x] T137 (AC-131) List model files and the objects each defines
  - Files: `packages/web/src/workspaces/model/FileList.tsx`, `packages/api/src/routes/model.ts`, `packages/web/src/workspaces/model/FileList.test.tsx`
  - Failing test: `FileList.test.tsx`: every cube, view, measure, dimension, segment, hierarchy, join and pre-aggregation a file defines is listed under it (AC-131).
  - Depends on: T010
  - **Built 2026-09-21, on the store the user chose:** model files in wechart's metadata database, which Cube will read through `repositoryFactory` once publishing lands (T143; `research/cube/spike-model-store-and-quick-calcs.md`).
    - **API:**
      - A `model_files` table (migration `1758240004000`) and repo (`repos/model-files.ts`). It lists files in code-point order, so the order doesn't hang on the database's collation.
      - `GET /api/model/files` returns every file's path and text; the route is in `server.ts` with the others, not a separate `routes/model.ts`.
    - **Seeding:** at start the store takes in the files under `WECHART_MODEL_DIR`, which defaults to the repository's `infra/cube/model`, the directory docker-compose mounts into Cube (`model/read-dir.ts`).
      - A file is taken in only where its path isn't stored yet, so a restart never writes over a file changed in wechart.
      - Only the kinds Cube's own `FileRepository` reads (`.js`, `.yml`, `.yaml`, `.jinja`, `.py`) are taken, named as it names them, relative to the directory (`cubes/orders.yml`), so Cube can later be served the same names.
      - Until T143 Cube still reads the disk; nothing can be edited yet, so the two cannot differ.
    - **Web:**
      - `workspaces/model/objects.ts` reads what a file defines with `yaml` 2.9.1 (now a dependency, per `research/yaml.md`) under `CUBE_PARSE`, Cube's own YAML reading: merges and timestamps as js-yaml resolves them.
      - A file that won't parse says so with its line. A `.js` or `.py` file says it is code, whose objects Cube makes when it runs (AC-140 is T146's).
      - `FileList` shows each file as a region named by its path. Under it, each cube or view is a group, with its members listed by kind: measures, dimensions, segments, hierarchies, joins and pre-aggregations.
      - It is mounted as the Model workspace's content.
  - **Tests:**
    - `repos/model-files.test.ts` (3): a seed takes in a new file; it never writes over a stored one; path order.
    - `model.test.ts` (2): the route serves every file, and none without a store.
    - `model/read-dir.test.ts` (2): Cube's file kinds, its naming and hidden files; a missing directory reads as nothing rather than failing the service.
    - `server.test.ts`: `WECHART_MODEL_DIR` and its default.
    - `objects.test.ts` (4): every member kind AC-131 names; several cubes and a view in one file; an unreadable file's line; code files.
    - `FileList.test.tsx` (2): every kind, listed under its cube, in the rendered component; an unreadable file's problem, shown on it.
    - `e2e/model.spec.ts` (1 in each browser): on the real service, `/model` lists the seeded files. `cubes/orders.yml` shows cube `orders` with its measures, dimensions and joins; `customers.yml` and `products.yml` are there; `views/order_summary.yml` shows view `order_summary`.

    - **A view's members were never listed**, against AC-131's Fails-when.
      - A view now lists its own member lists.
      - It also lists what it includes from its cubes, by path, under Includes. For example `orders.count`, and `orders.customers.*` for `"*"`.
      - Tests: `objects.test.ts` and `FileList.test.tsx`, and the e2e checks the seeded `order_summary`'s includes.
    - **Jinja templates were read as plain YAML.** A `{% for %}` showed a false parse error, and a `{{ name }}` dropped its cube.
      - A `.jinja` file, or one matching Cube's Jinja test (`{%`, `%}`, `{{`, `}}`, plus `{#`), is now held as a template. It isn't parsed, and it says why.
      - The file page does the same, so a template no longer opens in the editor. That closes what T141's review left.
    - **One file whose values can't be read** (an alias to no anchor) threw from `toJS()` and took the whole listing down. That file now says so, and the rest still reads.
    - **The notes for non-YAML files now say what Cube does:**
      - JavaScript: Cube runs it.
      - Python: a root `globals.py` supplies the Jinja templates' functions and variables, and any other `.py` isn't used.
      - Jinja: rendered before its cubes and views are read.
    - **Dot-files are read, as Cube reads them.** Cube's `FileRepository` has no hidden-file filter (research/cube/data-modeling.md, checked in the v1.7.42 source), so a `cubes/.draft.yml` is compiled. wechart used to drop it.
    - **A path that isn't a file** (a directory named `old.yml`) is walked, not read.
    - **A missing model directory still reads as nothing.** Any other failure to read it, such as a file in its place, now fails the start instead of showing an empty model.
    - **An empty model** says "There are no model files." instead of showing a blank page. The `.model-files__note` class now has its rule.
  - **Left from the review:**
    - **Seeding never refreshes a stored path.** Until T143 Cube reads the disk while the listing reads the store, so a file changed on disk after the first seed makes the two differ. The claim above that they "cannot differ" was wrong.
      - This is left alone because the store is meant to be the truth once T142 and T143 publish from it, and a restart must not write over what was published.
      - T143 serves Cube from the store, which closes the gap.
    - **JavaScript models' objects aren't listed.** A static `cube(...)` file has objects only once Cube runs it, and AC-131 has no exception for that. This is put to the user. The seed model has no JavaScript files.
    - **The layout differs from direction.md §7b's user decision:** a file tree beside the form and the YAML. What was built is a listing page and a separate file page. That design gap is recorded for the Model workspace's layout work.

The tabs are APG, and each shows its count.
      - Edits hand back the next object. Writing them to the YAML is T139's.

  - Files: `packages/web/src/workspaces/model/YamlEditor.tsx`, `packages/web/src/workspaces/model/sync.ts`, `packages/web/src/workspaces/model/sync.test.ts`

  - **Built 2026-09-21.**
    - **The editor is CodeMirror 6, not Monaco.** The planned text predates `research/code-editor.md`, which recommends CodeMirror and rejects Monaco on theming (no CSS variables, so the tokens can't drive its contrast) and on trapping Tab.
      - Pinned per that research: `@codemirror/state` 6.7.5, `view` 6.43.12, `language` 6.12.4, `lint` 6.9.7, `lang-yaml` 6.1.3, and `commands` 6.11.1, which the research had not pinned and now records.
      - `YamlEditor` is a controlled editor, built once. A `value` from elsewhere is dispatched in as a change marked with an annotation, so the cursor maps through it and it isn't handed back. It has syntax highlighting and line numbers, and is named for screen readers by the file.
    - **The file's text is the one truth (`sync.ts`):**
      - A form edit is written into it through the parsed document: `withObject` walks what changed and sets or deletes only that. The file's comments, and every key the edit did not touch, stay exactly as written. A comment written on a key the form removes goes with it.
      - The form reads from the last text that parsed (`modelOf`).
    - **Timing (`ModelFilePage`):**
      - A form edit sets the text in the same update, so the YAML view shows it in that render.
      - A YAML edit reaches the form after direction.md §7b's 300 ms pause, inside AC-133's 500, and only if the text parses. The form freezing on a parse error, with the problem shown, is T141.
    - **Layout:** direction.md §7b, the form as the main working area and the YAML on the right.
    - Labels write "SQL" for `sql`, and the form's controls are the inspector's 28 px.

      - a key added keeps the comments and the other keys as written;
      - one member's key changed changes that line and nothing else (the whole file compared);

      - the model from a parsing text;
      - no model, and the problem's line, from one that doesn't parse.

      - the form's edit is still in the YAML after the YAML's.

    - `styles.ts` (`.model-editor__warnbar`);
    - `e2e/model.spec.ts`.
    - **The check runs on each edit, not on the file.** Here the form and the YAML are both on screen, and a form edit is written into the file in place (T139), which keeps every comment and anchor it doesn't touch.
      - So there is no "switch" to warn about, and most edits lose nothing.
      - Each form edit is checked before it's applied. `lossOf` compares the file before and after it, collecting each node's `commentBefore` and `comment`, its `anchor`, and each alias's `source` (research/yaml.md, "Comments and anchors").
      - What `after` no longer has is named. Comments are counted line by line, so a block `yaml` writes back split or merged isn't a false loss. A comment is named by its first line.
    - **This departs from the prototype and direction.md §7b.** The prototype makes the whole form read-only for a file with comments, until "Edit in form anyway", because its form rewrote the file. Here nothing is held for an edit that loses nothing.
    - **When an edit would lose something**, it is held. The form is read-only meanwhile, and the prototype's warnbar names what would go:
      - It reads, for example: "This edit would drop the comment “Cube hides primary keys … This one …”. The YAML keeps them while you edit there."
      - "Apply anyway" writes the edit.
      - "Keep the file" leaves the file as it was.
      - Typing in the YAML drops the held edit, since it was made against the text before.
      - The bar scrolls into view and puts the focus on "Keep the file", because locking the form drops the focus from the field that was being edited.
      - **AC-134 wins over AC-133 here:** such an edit reaches the YAML when it's applied, not in the same render.
    - **An edit that can't be written at all** is refused with `yaml`'s reason. An example is removing an anchor that an alias still uses, where writing the file throws. It isn't applied, and the refusal clears on the next edit or on typing in the YAML.
  - **Tests, red first:**
    - `lossCheck.test.ts` (9):
      - an edit that keeps everything loses nothing;
      - a comment on a removed key;
      - an alias written over, and a comment written after it;
      - an anchor on a removed key;
      - a member added to an anchored list loses nothing;
      - a comment inside a removed member;
      - a comment the file has twice and keeps once;
      - lines kept however they're grouped.
    - `LossWarning.test.tsx` (4): each thing named; a many-line comment by its first line; focus and scroll; Apply and Keep.
    - `e2e/model.spec.ts` (1 more in each browser), on the real `orders.yml`: clearing the `id` dimension's Public, whose key carries the comment about Cube hiding primary keys:
      - The warning names that comment. The file still has it, the field still reads true and is locked, and the focus is on "Keep the file".
      - "Keep the file" leaves the file as it was and frees the form.
      - "Apply anyway" removes the key and its comment.
  - **Cold review (one round), verdict FAIL. Fixed:**
    - A comment written after an alias went with it unnamed. `yaml` puts it on the alias node, which the check skipped. Test added.
    - No test showed an anchor's loss being named, and one test's title claimed it did. The title is corrected and an anchor test added.
    - The warning could be off-screen, with no focus. It now scrolls into view and takes the focus.
    - The refusal message could go stale. It now clears.
    - Whole-block comparison could warn falsely. Comments are now counted by line.
    - A CSS comment sat over the wrong rule. Moved back.
  - **Left from the review:**
    - Explicit tags aren't listed as losses. A form edit keeps a value's type, so none is lost in practice.
    - The warnbar's colours show only after an edit, so the rendered contrast audit never meets them. It's text-primary on a 16 % warning tint of the surface, in both themes.
    - **For the user:** a form edit to the cube that owns an anchor, such as `orders.dimensions: &shared`, is written in place. So every alias of it (`returns`) changes too, as editing the YAML there would, and no warning says so.

    - **The problems (`problemsOf`):**
      - They come from `yaml`'s own errors, parsed with Cube's options (`CUBE_PARSE`).
      - Each has the file's path, its line and column, the message, and its place in the text.
      - The message is its first line, less the "at line N, column M:" that `prettyErrors` adds. The panel gives the place beside it.

      - **Problems panel** (`model.js`'s `.problems` and `.prob`), beneath the YAML:
        - a header, "Problems · N errors";
        - then a row per problem: the error glyph, the message, and `path:line`.
        - With no problems it reads "No problems in <path>".
        - Picking a problem puts the editor's cursor at it and scrolls there (direction.md §7b, "Each problem links to a line").
      - **Editor marks.** The editor marks each problem from the same list, with `setDiagnostics` and `lintGutter` (research/code-editor.md, "Diagnostics"), so the text and the marks cannot drift.
      - **The read-only veil** (`model.js`'s `.readonly-veil`), over the forms: "The YAML doesn't parse (line N). The form is read-only until the code is fixed."
    - **The form held read-only (`CubeForm`'s `readOnly`):**
      - Each panel is held in a disabled `<fieldset>`. Every control in the form is native, so that locks them all.
      - The tabs and the members' `<details>` summaries are not controls, so the form can still be browsed.
      - The fields take the subtle ground and secondary ink, not the buttons' half opacity, because the form is read while the YAML is mended.
    - **When the problems are found:**
      - They are found again after every 300 ms pause, not only when the text differs from the last that parsed. Otherwise text put back as it was would keep its old problems.
      - A file that doesn't parse when it is opened now opens too, with its problems listed, where it had shown only an alert.
    - **The lock while the form catches up.** The form is also read-only while it is behind the typed text, for the 300 ms pause.
      - A form edit in that window would be written from the text before the typing, and so over it.
      - This lock isn't shown as locked: it is brief, and it would flash with every burst of typing.
    - **Research:** `code-editor.md` now sources `scrollIntoView` and `focus()` for going to a problem. It also records the `cm-lintRange-error` class, as observed in the browser tests.
  - **Tests, red first:**

      - Title is disabled, and so is every control of an opened measure.
      - The editor marks one range.
      - Picking the problem from another line, then deleting three characters, mends line 3. With the jump turned off, this check failed as it should.
      - After the mend, the panel reads "No problems", the veil is gone, Title is editable, and SQL table is `public.orders` again.

      - `problemsOf` now reads the values too, and places the throw at the first alias that resolves to nothing. Its messages are "Unresolved alias …", and for a merge source, "Merge sources must be maps or map aliases".

- [x] T142 (AC-136) Validate against Cube before publishing and refuse what Cube rejects
  - Files: `packages/api/src/routes/model.ts`, `packages/api/src/model/validate.ts`, `packages/api/src/model/validate.test.ts`
  - Failing test: `validate.test.ts`: an invalid model is not published and Cube's error is attributed to the file it came from (AC-136).
  - Depends on: T141
  - **Built 2026-09-21, on the store the user chose in the spike:** Cube reads the model from wechart through `repositoryFactory` and picks up a publish through `schemaVersion`, with no restart.
    - **Files as built:**
      - `infra/cube/cube.js`, with `infra/cube/package.json` so the repo loads it as CommonJS, as Cube does;
      - `packages/api/src/model/{publish,validate}.ts`, with their tests and `cube-config.test.ts`;
      - `config.ts`, `cube/client.ts`, `server.ts`, `repos/model-files.ts`, `seed.ts`;
      - on the web side, `workspaces/model/{publish.ts,ModelFilePage.tsx,ProblemsPanel.tsx}`;
      - `docker-compose.yml` and `playwright.config.ts`.
      - There is no `routes/model.ts`: the routes are registered from `model/publish.ts`.
    - **`cube.js`: where Cube reads the model.**
      - It reads from the store named in the request's signed security context (`wechartStore`), and only if that store is on `WECHART_MODEL_STORES`. Otherwise it reads the mounted directory, as Cube did before.
      - A `wechartDraft` compiles a draft under its own app id.
      - The store is asked for its version on every request. A version kept even for a second hid a publish (T143).
      - Idle compiled models expire after ten minutes (`maxCompilerCacheKeepAlive`, reset on use), so drafts don't pile up in Cube's memory.
      - Observed through a throwaway Cube (research spike §1a).
    - **One Cube, several wechart instances.** The compose Cube trusts the dev and browser-test API (:4100) and the demo (:4200). Each instance's tokens name its own store, so each reads its own model.
    - **API routes for Cube** (`/api/cube-model/{files,version,drafts/:id}`), behind a shared key (`WECHART_MODEL_KEY`, compared timing-safely):
      - With no key, no one is served.
      - A store address without a key fails the start.
      - The version is worked out from the store on each ask, since another instance may have published.
    - **Publish (`POST /api/model/publish`):**
      - Only YAML files, by plain paths. Nothing with Jinja markers, and no stored template written over.
      - The whole model with the changes goes to Cube as a draft, through `/v1/meta` with the draft named.
      - It is published only if Cube read the draft and compiled it.
      - Cube's refusal (a 500, draft read) comes back as 422 with its errors attributed per file.
      - Cube failing without reading the draft, or answering from another model, is a 502, not a refusal of the file.
      - On success the files are written and the metadata cache let go of.
    - **Attribution (`validate.ts`):**
      - A compile-errors group names its file.
      - An undefined name goes on the changed file that holds it.
      - A YAML error goes on the changed file whose line its snippet shows.
      - Otherwise, each changed file: the live model compiled, so the error is in the change.
    - **The page:** "Save & reload Cube", the prototype's label, in the editor head.
      - It is enabled when the text has changed, parses, and has been read by the form, with nothing held.
      - On success: "Published. Cube is reading the new model.", and the listing and every field list are refetched.
      - On refusal: "Cube refused the model, so nothing was published.", with Cube's reasons in the Problems panel as "Cube: …" on their files.
    - **Browser-test isolation.** The browsers run at once and each publish writes a whole file, so a test that publishes successfully does so on a cube file of its own, created through the API (`e2e/model-files.ts`).
      - `WECHART_RESET_DATA` also clears the model, which is seeded again from the directory.
  - **Tests, red first:**
    - `validate.test.ts` (5): the three kinds of error, as observed; several to a file; the fallback.
    - `publish.test.ts` (12):
      - served to Cube only with the key, and to no one without one;
      - the version;
      - a draft compiled, then published, with the context named and the cache let go of;
      - a refusal on its file, with nothing published and the draft gone;
      - Cube unreachable is a 502;
      - no store is a 409;
      - paths;
      - Jinja refused before Cube is asked;
      - no template written over;
      - answered unread is a 502;
      - failed unread is a 502.
    - `cube-config.test.ts` (5): trusted store and key; a draft's own app id, with the version always asked; an untrusted or absent store reads the directory and asks nothing; draft names; compiled-model expiry.
    - `server.test.ts` (3 more): config; the store in every token; added from config.
    - `ProblemsPanel.test.tsx` (1 more): Cube's problems.
    - `e2e/model.spec.ts` (2 in each browser), on the real Cube:
      - A measure naming a member that doesn't exist is refused. "Cube: no_such_member is not defined" shows on `cubes/orders.yml`, and neither the stored file nor Cube's metadata has it.
      - A description on this browser's own cube is published, stored, and served by Cube.
  - **Cold review (one round), verdict FAIL. Fixed:**
    - **Security (H1):** a publish could carry Jinja, which Cube renders with its environment. `{{ env_var('CUBEJS_API_SECRET') }}` was confirmed to come back with the secret, which is AC-007's failing state.
      - Only YAML without Jinja markers is published now, and no template is written over. This matches the Non-goal that dynamic models are read-only.
    - **Validation without Cube reading the draft (H2)** now publishes nothing.
    - **An infrastructure failure shown as Cube refusing the file (M1)** is now a 502. A store address without a key fails the start.
    - **Compiled drafts piling up in Cube (M3):** idle ones expire.
    - **`cube.js` untested (M4):** it now has tests of its own.
    - **Loose paths (L3):** only plain segments are accepted.
  - **Left from the review:**
    - **`scheduledRefreshContexts`** isn't defined. Refresh runs against the directory's model. Nothing needs it until pre-aggregations (F16), which must define it.
    - **Two publishes to different files at once** are each compiled against the live model without the other. The user has ruled that concurrent changes may overwrite.
    - **Cube's problems stay listed** until the next save, labelled "Cube:" as from it.
- [x] T143 (AC-137) Make published members available to the chart builder without restarting Cube
  - Files: `packages/api/src/cube/meta-cache.ts`, `e2e/publish-model.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `e2e/publish-model.spec.ts`: after publishing, the new member appears in the chart builder's field list once Cube reports the new schema version, with no restart (AC-137).
  - Depends on: T142, T024
  - **Built 2026-09-21.** T142 carries the mechanism: Cube asks wechart's store for the model's version on every request, and recompiles when it changes. There is no restart. T143 is what a publish must reach, all the way to the field list.
    - **Cube's side.** `cube.js` no longer keeps a store's version for a second; its comment credits T143, though the change landed in T142's commit.
      - The e2e showed a publish missed by a request inside that second. wechart then cached Cube's old model for its minute.
      - The version is now asked every time.
    - **wechart's cache (`meta-cache.ts`).** `invalidate()` now also lets go of a fetch already in flight, through a generation count. A fetch begun before a publish still answers the callers that waited on it, but it is neither handed to later callers nor kept.
      - This was found the same way: another browser's page asked for the model just before a publish, and its fetch landed after.
      - The cache is let go of after the files are written, not before.
    - **The browser.** The chart builder's field list reads the model afresh each time it opens (`refetchOnMount: 'always'`, research/data-layer.md).
      - So a builder opened after a publish in any tab has the new members. The app's five-minute `staleTime` had kept another tab's copy.
      - The tab that published also refetches its metadata (T142).
  - **Tests, red first:**
    - `meta-cache.test.ts` (1 more): a fetch in flight on invalidate is neither served after it nor kept. It failed before the generation count.
    - `publish.test.ts` (1 more): the cache is let go of after the write.
    - `e2e/publish-model.spec.ts` (1 in each browser), on the real Cube:
      - Setup: a cube file of the browser's own, whose model wechart has cached, is confirmed without the member. A second tab opens the builder on that cube.
      - "Save & reload Cube" publishes a measure `published` through the Model workspace.
      - The builder, opened again, lists it.
      - The second tab, reopening the builder within the app with its model already cached, lists it too. It failed before the field list refetched on opening.
      - wechart's `/api/meta` has it.
  - **Cold review (one round), verdict FAIL. Fixed:**
    - Another tab kept its old field list for up to five minutes.
    - The e2e didn't fill wechart's cache before publishing, and didn't cover a tab with the model already cached.
    - The order of write and invalidate was untested.
  - **Left from the review:**
    - **A builder left open and never reopened** doesn't update itself, since metadata isn't pushed. Reopening it, or any new builder, shows the new members.
    - **Several API instances on one store:** only the publishing one lets go of its cache. The others serve their cached model for up to AC-009's 60 seconds. The compose setup runs one API per store.
    - **Cost:** the version Cube asks for on each request is worked out from every stored file. A stored revision bumped on each write would be one lookup, and would matter for AC-174 if the model grows.

- [x] T207 (AC-189) Refuse to publish a rollup that names members the model doesn't have, and name them on its row
  - Files: `packages/api/src/model/rollupReferences.ts`, `packages/api/src/model/rollupReferences.test.ts`, `packages/api/src/model/publish.ts`, `packages/web/src/workspaces/model/ModelFilePage.tsx`, `packages/web/src/workspaces/model/form/CubeForm.tsx`, `packages/web/src/workspaces/model/preagg/RollupList.tsx`, `e2e/preaggregations.spec.ts`

  - Depends on: T149
  - Added 2026-09-22 at the user's decision ("Yes, refuse to publish"), which answers the question T149's record left open.
  - **Done 2026-09-22.** Files as built:
    - API: `model/rollupReferences.ts` (`rollupProblemsOf`) and its test; `model/publish.ts` (`unknownInRollups`, run after Cube compiles the draft); `model/validate.ts` (`CompileError.preAggregation`); `publish.test.ts`; `yaml@2.9.1` added to `package.json`, already researched in `research/yaml.md`.
    - Web: `preagg/RollupList.tsx` (`stillUnknown`, and `RollupFacts` takes `unknown`) and its test; `form/CubeForm.tsx` (`rollupProblems`, and an icon on the Pre-aggregations tab); `ModelFilePage.tsx`; `ProblemsPanel.tsx`; `shell/styles.ts` (`.rollup-unknown`).
    - e2e: `e2e/preaggregations.spec.ts` (two tests).
    - Research: `research/cube/rollup-references.md` (new: Cube's source, on how a rollup's names resolve), and `research/cube/pre-aggregations.md`, "Observed locally" (probes on a throwaway Cube v1.7.42).
    - **Why the model files, not `/v1/meta`.** Cube's `/v1/meta` leaves hidden members out, even with `?extended=true`, and a rollup may name them: a `public: false` dimension and the primary key both matched. So the check reads the draft's YAML the way Cube's js-yaml does, with `<<` merges resolved. Names are resolved as Cube resolved them in the probes: bare, `CUBE.`, `TABLE.` and the cube's own name are its own cube's; `other.member` is another cube's; a join path (`CUBE.customers.country`) is the member of the cube it ends at; and a cube has what it `extends`. Each name is held to its list's kind: a dimension under `measures` or `segments`, a measure under `dimensions`, and a string dimension as `time_dimension` all failed in Cube. Only an index column may carry a granularity. What wechart can't read gives no refusal, because a false refusal would stop a model Cube serves: a cube defined in code or a template, one extending such a cube, a view, a template-built name, or an index column's alias form.
    - **Which problems refuse.** Every unknown name in a changed file. In a file the change leaves alone, only names the change newly makes unknown, compared name by name against the live model. So a rollup already broken in an untouched file doesn't hold up an unrelated change, or one that mends some of its names.
    - **What the user sees.** The outcome line reads "A pre-aggregation names members the model doesn't have, so nothing was published." (wechart's refusal, not Cube's). The Problems panel lists the rollup at its line, without Cube's "Cube:" prefix, and links to the file when the rollup is in another one. The cube's Pre-aggregations tab carries the error icon while a rollup there still writes a refused name. That rollup's row reads "⚠ Not in the model, so not published: `CUBE.no_such_member` (dimensions)", and a name drops off as soon as the rollup stops writing it. The refusal is kept in session storage for the tab, because each model file opens as a page of its own, so a rollup in another file shows it when that file is opened. It is cleared by the next successful publish.
    - AC-189 ticked, in all three browsers, on the real Cube:
      - `e2e/preaggregations.spec.ts`, "a rollup naming a member the model doesn't have…": `CUBE.no_such_member` typed into a rollup's dimensions is refused; the stored file doesn't contain it; and the rollup's row shows it with its icon.
      - "taking away a member another file's rollup names…": removing a dimension that another file's rollup names is refused, and that rollup's row shows the name once its file is opened from the Problems panel. Without the kept refusal this test fails.
    - **Cold review (one round), verdict FAIL. Fixed:**
      - YAML `<<` merges were read as plain keys, which gave false refusals. Now read as Cube reads them, and tested with a case that fails without `merge`.
      - The live-versus-draft comparison was keyed on a rollup's whole list of names. It is now name by name.
      - A refusal about another file's rollup never reached that rollup's row. It is now kept for the tab, and linked from the Problems panel.
      - The Problems panel said "Cube:" for wechart's refusal.
      - The kind checks had no evidence behind them. They were probed, and Cube refuses each.
      - The tab's icon outlived the fix.
      - This Files list.
    - **Suite waits.** Cube compiles each publish's draft in 2.7–7.4 s while the suite runs in parallel, so publish-outcome waits now allow 20 s (`e2e/model-files.ts`, `PUBLISHED`, committed under T142).
- [x] T150 (AC-144) Say whether a chart's query matches a pre-aggregation, and name the member that prevents it
  - Files: `packages/web/src/editor/AccelerationPanel.tsx`, `packages/api/src/cube/preaggMatch.ts`, `packages/api/src/cube/preaggMatch.test.ts`
  - Failing test: `preaggMatch.test.ts`: a matching query reports the rollup Cube actually uses, and a non-matching one names the member that blocks the match (AC-144).
  - Depends on: T014, T148
  - **Done 2026-09-21.** Files as built:
    - API: `cube/preaggMatch.ts` (`rollupsFrom`, `missOf`) and its test; `cube/client.ts` (`sql`, `dryRun`, `metaExtended`); `server.ts` (`POST /api/acceleration`), tested in `server.test.ts`.
    - Web: `editor/AccelerationPanel.tsx` and its test; `editor/ResultsDrawer.tsx` (an "Acceleration" tab, asked only while shown).
    - e2e: `e2e/preaggregations.spec.ts`.
  - **Whether it matches is Cube's word.** `/v1/sql` plans the query without running it and names the pre-aggregations it would use, by `preAggregationId`. The panel reports that and nothing else. So it can't report a match Cube doesn't use, which is the first half of the Fails-when.
  - **Why it misses** is worked out from what Cube matches on: the dry run's transformed query, against the rollups Cube compiled (`/v1/meta?extended=true`). It follows the matcher's additive rules (`research/cube/pre-aggregations.md` §2):
    - every leaf measure, dimension and filtered member must be in the rollup;
    - each time dimension must be the rollup's, at a granularity Cube's own `granularityHierarchies` says the rollup's can make;
    - a measure that doesn't add up needs a rollup grouped exactly as the query.

    The rollup reported is the one with the fewest members missing, and the member named is the first that stops it. A cube with no rollups says so, naming the chart's first measure. If every member is in the nearest rollup and Cube still didn't use it, the panel says so, naming the time dimension. That points at the date range or the time zone, which the rules above don't cover. So a miss always names a member. The shapes of all three answers were observed on the local Cube, and are recorded under "Observed locally".
  - AC-144 ticked, on the real Cube in all three browsers (`e2e/preaggregations.spec.ts`):
    - The seed cube's count by status reads "Served from the pre-aggregation monthly_orders.by_status_month.", and `/api/load` confirms Cube serves that chart from that rollup.
    - Adding `ordered_at`, which every chart asks by day, reads a miss: the nearest rollup, and `monthly_orders.ordered_at` "is grouped by day, and the rollup keeps it by month". `/api/load` confirms Cube answers it from the database.
    - A chart on `orders` reads "orders has no pre-aggregations".
  - **Cold review (one round, 2026-09-21), verdict FAIL.** A chart touching a joined cube had its reason blamed on a member the rollup holds. Fixed, with each of Cube's shapes checked on the local Cube first (`research/cube/pre-aggregations.md`, "How the dry run spells what it owns"):
    - **Joined members.** The dry run spells a joined cube's member by its join path (`orders.customers.country`), and the model spells it `customers.country`. Members are now compared by their own cube and name (the last two segments), and hierarchies are looked up the same way. Rollups are looked for on every cube the query involves, the joined ones included, as Cube searches them.
    - **Filters.** The extra pass over the query's raw filters is gone. A filtered dimension is already among the dry run's owned dimensions, by its cube name. A filtered measure is not, so it is no longer blamed. A blocking dimension that is filtered on still says so.
    - **Segments.** A rollup's `segmentReferences` count among what it can serve, since a query's segments are among its owned dimensions.
    - **Date ranges.** A rollup that allows non-strict date ranges (Cube's default) is matched on the time dimensions as asked (`ownedTimeDimensionsAsIs`), and a strict one on the granularity a range needs. A date range with no grouping is no longer read as "grouped by day". A range that isn't whole periods, for a strict rollup, now says so.
    - **Measures that don't add up** need the same time dimension at the same granularity as well as the same dimensions.
    - **Other fixes.** A plan Cube gives as a list, one per query it runs, is read whole. A Cube refusal on any of the three calls is passed on as Cube's. A dry run that owns nothing still names the query's first member.
    - Tests: the two fixtures that didn't match what Cube emits now do. Added: joined members (blamed and not), a rollup on a joined cube, a measure filter, segments, a date range without grouping, the non-additive granularity case, the list plan, and Cube's refusal.
    - Left as found: a rollup Cube plans to use but hasn't built yet reads as a match, while the chart itself shows Cube's "not built yet" error.

Focus now follows the column, and at the top or bottom it goes to the direction still open. The move is announced politely ("count moved to position 1 of 3.").
      - **Reordering moves the YAML items, not their values.** `sync.ts` now sees a list whose items are the same in another order, and moves the item nodes. A comment or an anchor goes with its column rather than staying at its place (`research/yaml.md`, a sequence's `items`).

After "Save & reload Cube" reports Published, the stored file has the columns in that order and `type: aggregate`.

    - e2e: `e2e/refresh.spec.ts` on the seed dashboard against the real Cube in all three browsers, and the seed dashboard added to `e2e/a11y-tokens.spec.ts`.
    - **Past which cache.** wechart's own answer is dropped and put back with the new one, and Cube is asked with `cache: 'no-cache'`, which "Skips refresh key checks. Always returns fresh data" (`research/cube/refresh-jobs-caching.md`). The first build sent `must-revalidate`, and the e2e caught it: Cube returned its cached result with the same `lastRefreshTime`, because that value revalidates only when the refresh key has changed. The research doc and `data-layer.md` now quote all four values.
    - **What the user sees.** In view mode each chart tile has a ↻ "Refresh <chart title>" button beside the pencil. The head shows "Data as of h:mm:ss" as the prototype's `.badge`, where its tiles carry their data's age. It is not drawn over the chart, where it covered axis labels. Edit mode has no refresh, because an edit has its own drafts.
    - AC-148 ticked: `e2e/refresh.spec.ts` shows the `datetime` moving on after Refresh, and that every `/api/load` body carries `cache: 'no-cache'`.
    - **Review fixes (2026-09-21):**
      - **A tile a filter reaches** draws its filtered answer, so Refresh asks that again. `FilteredCharts` exposes a handle whose `refresh` runs `fetchQuery` under the tile's own filtered key with `no-cache`, and the tile's time is the filtered answer's own. Before the fix, it re-asked the unfiltered chart, left the filtered answer on screen, and moved the time forward anyway. Tested in `dashboard/refreshTile.test.tsx` and in a second case in `e2e/refresh.spec.ts`, against the real Cube.
      - **Races with the dashboard's own load.** ↻ is disabled while the charts, or this chart's filtered answer, are being asked. A refresh overtaken by a newer load or a save drops its answer: `chartsGeneration` in `DashboardPage` moves on at each load and each save that writes drafts. Tested in `dashboard/refreshPage.test.tsx`.
      - **A refresh reads the chart as it is now.** Its document replaces the one held, so the tile's name and its filters follow a change saved elsewhere (`refreshPage.test.tsx`).
      - **A save with drafts keeps the other tiles' times** (`drawnWithDrafts`, tested in `chartDrafts.test.ts`).
      - **Data not from today names its day** (`DataAsOf.test.tsx`).
      - **Left as found:** a refresh can't be cancelled, and it holds no abort signal. The dashboard's own load has the same gap.
    - **On a tile served from a pre-aggregation**, Refresh reads the rollup again, and the time stays at the rollup's build time. `no-cache` skips Cube's result cache, not a pre-aggregation (observed locally; `research/cube/pre-aggregations.md`, "Observed locally"). That time is true, but it doesn't move.
    - **Follow-up, 2026-09-22, at the user's decision** ("Say it's the rollup's"): Refresh stays as it is, and AC-148 is reworded so that such a tile says whose data it is. Files: `tiles/RollupRefreshNote.tsx` (new), `dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`, `shell/styles.ts` (`.tile-note`), and tests in `dashboard/refreshTile.test.tsx` and `e2e/acceleration.spec.ts`.
      - **What the user sees.** Once refreshed, a tile whose answer came from a pre-aggregation shows a note in its flow, above the chart. The note has the accelerated icon and reads "Served from pre-aggregation `<id>`: its data is as of its last build, <time>. Rebuild it in Jobs.", with Jobs linking to `/jobs`, whose Pre-aggregations tab rebuilds it. A × dismisses it. The note is shown in view mode only, like Refresh, because its link would leave an edit. It follows the answer the tile draws, so it hides when the database answers.
      - AC-148 re-ticked. `e2e/acceleration.spec.ts` ("refreshing a tile a pre-aggregation serves…") runs against the real Cube in all three browsers: the `monthly_orders` tile's note names `monthly_orders.by_status_month` and links to Jobs, and the `orders` tile the database answered shows none. `refreshTile.test.tsx` covers the filtered path. `e2e/refresh.spec.ts` still covers the moving time.
      - **Cold review (one round), verdict PASS. Fixed:** the Fails-when line could not fail, because the badge already said a rollup served the tile; it now names the note. The "nothing before a refresh" check now waits for the rollup's answer to be drawn. The note is hidden in edit mode.

      - A build is asked for the security context wechart's own requests carry, so it builds the model Cube answers wechart from, in UTC.
      - Cube returns one token per partition, and each is followed every 1.5 s until it reports `done`, `missing_partition`, `failure: …`, `not_found` or `pre_agg_not_found`, then no further.
      - The status route passes on each token's state and table, but not the selector, which echoes the security context.
      - A name that isn't `cube.name` is refused before Cube is asked. Cube's own refusal of an unknown pre-aggregation is passed on as Cube's.
    - **The state is shown as text here.** Its icon comes with T158 (AC-152).
    - **Cold review (one round, 2026-09-22), verdict FAIL. Fixed:**
      - **A look at the Queries tab threw away every build being followed.** That is AC-149's failing state, since a job's end then never shows. Both tabs' panels now stay mounted, the one not shown hidden, and each tab's `aria-controls` names a panel that exists. A test switches tabs mid-build and sees it through to `done`.
      - **Polling stops once every token asked about has finished,** not once every answer has. An answer that left a token out had counted as finished. Whether a job has finished now comes from the API, whose `isFinished` is the one tested rule; the browser's copy is gone. An answer naming no token or no state is left out, not made up.
      - **A build that starts no job** (no tokens) says so and asks nothing more, where it had polled a 400 every 1.5 s.
      - **The list is read again each time the tab mounts**, so a published rollup shows. Only rollups and original-SQL pre-aggregations are offered: a lambda or a join is made of rollups listed in their own right, and its build isn't researched.
      - **One start at a time.** Each start's answer, a refusal included, is the one shown, and builds are numbered rather than timed.
      - **A draft's token has no `jobs` scope.** `contextToApiScopes` leaves it at the defaults, so Cube as well as wechart keeps drafts unbuilt. Checked on Cube: a draft token's jobs call gets 403 "API scope is missing: jobs", and a plain one gets 200.
      - **A note under Builds:** Cube 1.7.42 reports `done` once the partition's table exists, which a failed import can leave (research gotcha 3).
      - **The accessibility sweep** covers the query log again (`/jobs?tab=queries` opens it). The e2e's job locator is the list item, not any `job-*` test id.
      - Left as found: a cube marked `public: false` is missing from the extended metadata outside dev mode, so its rollups can't be started here; the refresh worker still builds them.
    - AC-149 ticked. `e2e/jobs.spec.ts`, against the real Cube in all three browsers, builds the seed rollup from the Jobs page. The job's token appears, its state is followed to `done`, and it names its Cube Store table.
