- [x] T162 (AC-156) Show the refresh worker's state and the Cube deployment's health
  - Files: `packages/web/src/workspaces/jobs/Health.tsx`, `packages/api/src/cube/health.ts`, `packages/api/src/cube/health.test.ts`
  - Failing test: `health.test.ts`: an unhealthy reply from Cube's health endpoint marks the deployment down, and the refresh worker's state is shown (AC-156).
  - Depends on: T158
  - **Done 2026-09-22.** Files as built:
    - API: `cube/health.ts` (`healthOf`, `originOf`) and its test; `config.ts` (`CUBE_WORKER_URL`); `server.ts` (`GET /api/cube-health`, separate from `/api/health`, which is this API's own).
    - Web: `workspaces/jobs/Health.tsx` and its test; `StateBadge.tsx` (a general `Status`, which the job badge now uses); `JobsPage.tsx` (the header); `shell/styles.ts`.
    - Stack: `docker-compose.yml` publishes the refresh worker's port as 54001, and `playwright.config.ts` gives the e2e API `CUBE_WORKER_URL`.
    - e2e: `e2e/jobs.spec.ts`.
    - **From Cube's own probes.** `/readyz` and `/livez` need no auth, sit outside the API's base path, and answer `HEALTH` or `DOWN` (`research/cube/refresh-jobs-caching.md` §F).
      - The API instance is probed at its origin. The refresh worker, observed to answer its own probes, is probed at `CUBE_WORKER_URL`.
      - Each reads healthy only if both its probes say `HEALTH`. A `DOWN`, a non-200, an error, or no answer within 3 s reads down.
      - Without `CUBE_WORKER_URL` the worker reads "not watched", never "running".
    - **In the Jobs header**, as the prototype's "Refresh worker running" badge: "Cube healthy" or "Cube down", and "Refresh worker running", "down" or "not watched". Each is an icon and words, asked again every 10 s while the page is open.
      - A health that can't be read reads "unknown", not the last answer. The query kept that answer after a later ask failed, and it would have gone on saying healthy.
    - AC-156 ticked:
      - `e2e/jobs.spec.ts`, in all three browsers: on the real stack the header reads "Cube healthy" (ok icon) and "Refresh worker running", and the API's own answer agrees. With the API answering down, the header reads "Cube down" (failed icon) and "Refresh worker down".
      - `cube/health.test.ts`: Cube not ready, not live, unreachable, or 200 without `HEALTH` all read down.
      - `Health.test.tsx`: the header moves from healthy to down while open.
    - **Cold review (one round, 2026-09-22), verdict PASS. Fixed:**
      - **The probe's time limit is tested**, against a real server that takes the request and never answers: down, within the limit. Also against real servers answering `DOWN`, a 503 that still says `HEALTH` (down), and `HEALTH` (healthy). The limit can be set in a test.
      - **The e2e leaves the page open** and waits for the header's own poll to read down, rather than reloading.
      - **An answer the page doesn't know** (a new state, or none) reads "unknown", rather than breaking the page.
      - **`CUBE_WORKER_URL` must be an http(s) URL**, or the API refuses to start. Otherwise every probe fails, and the worker reads down forever.
      - **The worker's port is published on 127.0.0.1 only**, since it serves Cube's whole REST API, not just its probes.
      - The research's UNVERIFIED note on the worker's probes now points to the observation.
      - Left as found: behind a proxy that adds a path prefix, the probes at Cube's origin would 404 and read down (safe, never a false healthy). "Refresh worker running" means its process and connections are up, not that its builds succeed.

      - the object;

      - members: "all", "all except x", or the list;
      - rows: "all rows", or the filters in words through `and` and `or`, and "all rows (none declared)" when there's no `row_level`, which is Cube's default;
      - each condition's `if`;
      - the masked members;
      - the file it's declared in.

      Cubes and views with no policy are named as open to every security context. A file whose policies can't be read (code, a Jinja template, or YAML that doesn't parse) is named, so nothing is left out silently.

Cube compiled it through the publish check. It is a view of its own, since a cube or view with policies denies every group none of them names, and nothing else in the sample should change.

    - Core: `export/csv.ts` and its test (`csvOf`, `tableColumnsOf`); `index.ts`. `papaparse` 5.7.0 does the quoting, as research recommends. research/supporting-utilities.md §3 now records what its 5.7.0 source does.
    - Web:
      - `dashboard/TileViewMenu.tsx` and its test: the prototype's view-mode ⋯ › Download CSV.
      - `dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`.
      - `tiles/DataTable.tsx`, which now takes its columns from `tableColumnsOf`.
      - `dashboard/DashboardView.test.tsx`: a test renamed.
    - e2e: `e2e/export.spec.ts`.
    - **What "what the tile shows" is taken to mean:** the tile's table — what a table tile draws, and every chart's Table view (AC-178). That is every row the tile holds (already filtered, folded to its top N and sorted), a column for every key the rows carry in their order, and each value as the table writes it.
      - Not a chart's marks: a pie drops null values, a KPI shows one row, and a pivot or trellis draws only what its channels place. Its table shows every row, and the CSV follows the table.
    - A byte-order mark is prepended for spreadsheets.
    - The formula guard applies only to values Cube gave as text. A number, and the formatter's text for one, is left as written.
    - Download is held back while a newer answer (filter or refresh) is on its way.
    - Demonstrated by `e2e/export.spec.ts` on Chrome, Firefox and Safari: a filtered table tile of three dimensions, the third on no chart channel, parsed and compared to its table cell by cell.
    - Started ahead of T120, which is deferred with markings. **AC-171 is not ticked:** its marked-rows trigger and "marking limits" need T120 and T190. Tick it when those land, with an export test on a marked-rows view.
- [x] T178 (AC-172) State the exported row count and that a result was truncated
  - Files: `packages/core/src/export/csv.ts`, `packages/core/src/export/truncation.test.ts`
  - Failing test: `truncation.test.ts`: an export past Cube's row limit reports how many rows were written and that it is truncated (AC-172).
  - Depends on: T177
  - **Done 2026-09-22.** Files as built:
    - Core: `export/csv.ts` (`exportNoteOf`, `rowsText`), `export/truncation.test.ts`, `index.ts`.
    - Web: `dashboard/TileViewMenu.tsx` and its test; `dashboard/DashboardView.tsx` (`countRows`); `shell/styles.ts`, which also styles T177's disabled Download.
    - e2e: `e2e/export.spec.ts`.
    - Research: `research/cube/data-apis.md`.
    - The download is immediate. Then the tile's own query is asked again with `total: true`. Cube's rows for it and its full count come from that one answer, so they cannot disagree.
      - More in the count than returned: truncated, with both numbers.
      - All returned: "Nothing was cut short", with the query's own count, so rows folded into "Other" are not read as missing.
      - A count that fails, is missing, or is smaller than the rows: "Could not tell". It never claims nothing was cut.
    - Each download lets go of the one before, and an earlier count arriving late is not said of the last.
    - **The premise, checked against a real Cube:** a one-off compose Cube with `CUBEJS_DB_QUERY_DEFAULT_LIMIT=2` returned 2 of 3 rows with `total: 3`. So the count sees past the default limit a tile's query is capped by (research, "Observed locally").
    - AC-172 ticked on two tests:
      - `e2e/export.spec.ts`, "a download says how many rows it holds, and whether Cube cut them short": the real page and Cube. Past the limit, Cube's real answer has its `total` raised at the network, since the sample data is far under 10,000 rows.
      - `TileViewMenu.test.tsx`: each case, and the stale count.
    - When the marked-rows export (T120) lands, it must carry the same note.

## F21. Performance, accessibility and design conformance

- [x] T180 (AC-174) Meet the 3-second p95 for opening a 10-chart dashboard
  - Files: `e2e/perf/dashboard-open.spec.ts`, `packages/api/src/repos/query-log.ts`
  - Failing test: `e2e/perf/dashboard-open.spec.ts`: over a repeated run against live warehouse data, the p95 time to render all ten tiles, taken from wechart's own query timings, is under 3 s (AC-174).
  - Depends on: T015, T023
  - **Done 2026-09-22.** Files as built:
    - `e2e/perf/dashboard-open.spec.ts`.
    - `repos/query-log.ts` and its test: `byRequestIds`.
    - `server.ts` and its test: `POST /api/jobs/queries/find`.
    - `research/echarts.md`: a chart is drawn when `setOption` returns, read from the 6.1.0 source.
    - **Method:**
      - Ten charts on `orders` (bar ×2, line ×2, pie, KPI ×3, table ×2), the dashboard opened 20 times per browser, with no retry.
      - Two p95s, each held under 3 s:
        - wechart's own query timings, from its query log, found by the request ids the page sent (a polled query counts as one sample);
        - the whole open, from navigating to every tile drawn.
      - Every query is asserted live: none served by a pre-aggregation.
    - **Measured 2026-09-22 on an Apple M4 Pro, docker-desktop compose stack (Cube 1.7.42), this spec run on its own:**

      | Browser | Opens p95 | Queries p95 |
      |---|---|---|
      | Chrome | 851 ms | 428 ms |
      | Firefox | 1,019 ms | 288 ms |
      | Safari | 850 ms | 293 ms |

      Edge runs in CI only.
    - **Conditions:**
      - The sample warehouse has 240 order rows.
      - `orders` has no pre-aggregations, so every query reads Postgres through Cube.
      - Cube's result cache is not bypassed. Under its default 10-second refresh key most opens are served from it, and the browsers share it.
    - **Not demonstrated:**
      - Production-sized warehouse volumes (M3).
      - A cold-cache p95. The user ruled on 2026-09-22 that AC-174 is measured as users see it, Cube's cache included, so no cold reading is needed.
    - The budget held on the first run; no red phase was seen.
    - AC-174 ticked under these conditions.

  - Failing test: `e2e/perf/marking-update.spec.ts`: the p95 time to update every affected tile after a marking change is under 3 s (AC-175). The client-side concurrency bound is set here against Cube's queue concurrency.

- [ ] T182 (AC-176) Make every action keyboard-reachable with a focus indicator distinct from the marking outline
  - Files: `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts` (the ring), `packages/web/src/shell/useFocusTrap.ts`, the dashboard, filter, settings, security, search and Explore surfaces it names below, `e2e/keyboard-coverage.spec.ts`. The planned `packages/ui/src/focus.css.ts` and `YamlEditor.tsx` hint were not needed; see below.
  - Failing test: `e2e/keyboard-coverage.spec.ts`: navigation, authoring, marking, filtering and every dialog are reachable from the keyboard, focus is visible on every control and is visually distinct from the marking outline, and Tab leaves the code editor, which is no keyboard trap (AC-176).
  - Depends on: T063, T106, T139
  - **Partly built 2026-09-22. Left unchecked, and AC-176 unticked, because marking is deferred** (T190, T120, T181): marking from the keyboard, and a focus ring distinct from the marking outline, come with markings. The prototype's ring is the accent, the same as the marking colours' neighbourhood; an audit measured accent against `mk4` at ΔE 7.4 (light) and against `mk2` under protanopia at 4.9 (dark). Built here, from that audit of what exists now:
    - **Properties from the tile.** Enter on a focused edit-mode tile selects it, then opens and focuses the sidebar's Properties panel, as direction.md's keyboard section and the prototype have it (`grid/keyboard.ts` `properties`, `EditSidebar` `openSidebarPanel`). Tabbing towards the sidebar selects every tile on the way, so a middle tile's Properties were out of the keyboard's reach. The tile's accessible name lists the key.
    - **Focus kept after a removal.** After Delete, the ⋯ menu's Delete, and either answer to the section question, focus goes to the next item in the container, else the previous one, else the enclosing section (`focusAfterDeleting`). The section question's Cancel goes back to the section. Move to keeps focus on the tile where it landed, or on its neighbour if it went to a page that isn't showing. A filter's Clear leaves focus on its summary; Remove moves it to the next filter, or the one before.
    - **Dialogs.** `shell/useFocusTrap` keeps Tab inside the four modal dialogs: the section question, discard settings, generated SQL and search. The library chart's delete confirmation opens on "Keep the chart", Escape keeps it, and focus returns to Delete chart. The settings page opens with focus on its section's heading, gives focus back to the gear on the way out, and "Keep editing" returns it to whatever asked.
    - **The code editor is no trap.** CodeMirror leaves Tab alone unless it's bound to indent, and ours isn't (`research/code-editor.md`), so Tab moves on and no Escape-then-Tab hint is needed. The planned hint is dropped, and the e2e shows Tab leaving the editor with the file unchanged.
    - **A tile's table** has a named, focusable scrolling region (`role=region`, "{title}, table"), so its rows can be reached and scrolled in WebKit too.
    - **Rings.** Inside a box that clips or scrolls, the ring is drawn inset (`outline-offset: -2px`): the model's YAML/Join toggle, the edit sidebar's tabs and panel, the settings nav, a tile's ⋯ menu, the Problems panel and a filter's options. The undo toast's ring is drawn in the toast's ink; the accent there was 2.89:1 light and 2.15:1 dark, under WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1. The planned `packages/ui/src/focus.css.ts` isn't needed: the ring is the app sheet's (`shell/styles.ts`).
    - **Reasons in words, not tooltips.** A tile a filter can't reach says why beneath "Not filtered by…" (`aria-describedby`). A disabled Download CSV says why beneath it; a disabled button takes no focus, so its tooltip was out of the keyboard's reach. The Accelerated badge keeps its rollup name in the tooltip, the prototype's form chosen at T014. The same name is reachable from the keyboard in the chart editor's Acceleration panel.
    - **Tests:**
      - `e2e/keyboard-coverage.spec.ts` covers:
        - Enter → Properties;
        - Delete and Move to keeping focus;
        - the section question's trap and Cancel;
        - the library delete confirmation;
        - filter Clear and Remove;
        - the settings page's focus in and out;
        - the table region;
        - the code editor's Tab;
        - rings measured whole against every clipping box around them;
        - the toast ring's contrast in both themes.
      - Unit tests: `shell/useFocusTrap.test.tsx` (including a modal stacked over another), `EditSidebar.test.tsx` (including the panel already open), `DeleteSectionDialog.test.tsx`, `SettingsPage.test.tsx` (focus in and out, and the discard question's trap), `GeneratedSql.test.tsx` and `SearchPalette.test.tsx` (their traps), `keyboard.test.ts` (Enter), `DataTable.test.tsx` (region), `coverageOnDashboard.test.tsx` and `TileViewMenu.test.tsx` (reasons). Each trap test was checked to fail with the trap switched off.
      - The e2e suite passes in all three browsers (957). The ten-chart perf test failed in Firefox and Safari under the full parallel run and passed alone.
    - **Cold review (one round), verdict FAIL. Fixed:**
      - **The section question pulled focus back onto "Keep the items" whenever the dashboard re-rendered,** for example when a chart answered or the toast timed out. Its effect was keyed on the owner's fresh `onCancel`. It now reads the latest Cancel from a ref, as the SQL dialog does, and is tested with a re-render.
      - **The table region's ring was cut off, and so were its last rows and scrollbar.** The chart and the table were drawn at the tile's whole height below its head, and ran 66 px past the tile's clipped bottom. The tile's body now takes what the tile has left (a flex column, with a zero basis outranking the pixel height). The chart follows its box. The e2e measures both the chart and the region inside the tile.
      - **Stacked modals:** a trap takes no Tab from another modal opened over it, such as search opened over a dialog.
      - **"Keep the items"** puts focus on the first kept item, where the section was.
      - **Enter with Properties already open** moved no focus. React skips a render for the same panel, so the sidebar now counts each ask.
      - **Coverage added:** the ⋯ menu's Delete, the "keep" answer, and the trap in each dialog.
    - **Still open, beyond marking:**
      - `role=tab` and `role=tree` without arrow keys in tab groups, page tabs, the results drawer and the folder tree;
      - the grip is a dead tab stop;
      - ⌘Z, F6 and `/` from direction.md are absent;
      - the 8 s undo toast can time out under focus, and its Undo and Dismiss leave focus on the page;
      - deleting a tab by its × leaves focus on the page;
      - Escape with search open over a dialog closes both;
      - removing the only filter leaves focus on the page;
      - after a view-mode settings save the gear may still be disabled, and then takes no focus.

- [x] T206 (AC-188) Write time values by their granularity in axes, labels, tooltips and tables
  - Files: `packages/core/src/format.ts`, `packages/core/src/format.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/axis.ts`, `packages/charts/src/tooltip.ts`, `packages/web/src/tiles/DataTable.tsx`, `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `format.test.ts`: each granularity writes its value as AC-188 lists; `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`: the seeded "Orders over time by status" tile's axis shows no raw timestamp (AC-188).
  - Depends on: T195
  - Added 2026-09-21. The screenshots taken for AC-187 showed the seeded time chart's axis as `2026-01-02T00:00:00.000`, and no criterion covered it. The formats in AC-188 are a default the user can change; the model's own format for a member wins.
  - **Also seen, not a formatting matter:** the same seeded chart is a *bar* chart of daily counts over 240 days and three statuses. Its bars are 0 or 1 and less than a pixel wide, so it reads as empty. The seed chose `bar` for its tests; the recommender would not pick it for this shape.
  - **Built 2026-09-21.** The planned `format.ts` was in `packages/core/src/`, as listed.
    - **Research, observed first:** each predefined granularity was asked of the running Cube, and what comes back was recorded in `research/cube/data-apis.md` ("The values that come back"). The bucket's start arrives as `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.SSS` with no offset, in the query's time zone. It arrives twice, under `member` and under `member.<granularity>`. Weeks start on Monday. From the installed ECharts source, `research/echarts.md` now records that a category axis's and a legend's formatter are called with the raw name.
    - **Core:**
      - `TimeGranularity` and `MemberGranularities` are new types.
      - `formatterFor` and `formatValue` take the granularities and write a time member by its granularity: "2026", "Q1 2026", "Jan 2026", "Week of Jan 5, 2026", "Jan 2, 2026", and "Jan 2, 2026, 3:04 PM" for an hour or a minute (with seconds for a second).
      - The value is read as written and formatted in UTC, so a reader in another zone sees the bucket Cube named. The time of day is written out by hand, because Intl's en-US time puts a narrow no-break space before "PM" in newer ICU and a plain space in older.
      - `granularityOf` also recognises the `member.<granularity>` column.
      - `RenderSpec.granularities` is filled by `compile` from the chart's time dimensions through `granularitiesOf`. It shares `QUERY_GRANULARITY` (day) with `toCubeQuery`, so the rows' bucket and their labels cannot disagree.
      - The model's own strftime format for a time member is T201's to honour. Until then the granularity's form stands in, never the raw timestamp.
    - **Charts** (`charts/src/time.ts`, `timeWriter`): the rows keep their raw values, and the time is written only where a name is shown:
      - a category axis and its crosshair label, including a horizontal bar's y axis and both heatmap axes;
      - the tooltip heading, and a series named by a time split or a trellis panel;
      - the legend (`legend.formatter`);
      - trellis panel titles and category axes.
      - A category that isn't time is left exactly as it came, so an id is never grouped (T194).
    - **Web:** DataTable cells; pivot row and column headers (time members only); and the results drawer. The drawer's result rows go by the chart's granularity (`granularitiesOf` from the editor), and its ungrouped underlying rows are written with their time of day.
  - **Tests, red first:**
    - `core/format.test.ts` (8): each granularity; hour, minute and second; the time zone; a week starting in the prior year; `formatValue`; gaps and non-times; the `member.<granularity>` column; a member with no granularity.
    - `core/compile.test.ts` (1): the granularity matches the query's.
    - `charts/time-places.test.ts` (20): the axis on 11 chart types, the crosshair, four tooltip cases, the legend by colour and by pie slice, trellis panel titles, and a non-time category left alone.
    - `DataTable.test.tsx`, `PivotTable.test.tsx` and `ResultsDrawer.test.tsx` (1, 1, 2).
    - `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts` (1 in each browser): the seeded "Orders over time by status" tile. Its SVG axis text has no raw timestamp and includes "Jan 2, 2026"-style days; in its table view, the `orders.ordered_at` column is all days and no cell is a timestamp.

## F22. Wiring the marking subsystem (deferred)

- [ ] T190 (AC-087, AC-088, AC-089, AC-090, AC-091, AC-092, AC-093, AC-094, AC-095, AC-096, AC-097, AC-098, AC-100, AC-101, AC-102, AC-103, AC-107, AC-108, AC-109) Connect the marking subsystem to the rendering path
  - Files: `packages/web/src/tiles/ChartTile.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/model/document.ts`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/settings/TileLimits.tsx`, `e2e/markings.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `e2e/markings.spec.ts`: clicking a bar in a running dashboard marks it, a second tile limited by that marking re-queries, and the badge names the marking and the count — each of the listed criteria observed on a tile, the way its `Fails when:` is written.
  - Depends on: T086–T119
  - **Deferred 2026-09-20 at the user's direction; un-deferred by the user 2026-09-23** ("Markings (41 ACs)"), and being built in stages, each ticking only what it shows on a rendered tile.
  - **Stage 1, 2026-09-23: marking by clicking, drawn, said and cleared.**
    - `RenderSpec` gained `marking` (the predicate and the marking's colour), and `charts/src/marking/option.ts` applies `markedItemStyle` per drawn item by finding the row behind it — the same reading of an item the click uses, so what a click marks is what is drawn marked. Applied in `optionFor` beside the cross-filter highlight, so no chart type can forget a marking it is showing.
    - `DashboardView` holds `MarkingState` for the dashboard and reads it at render. Not pushed to tiles: a tab nobody has opened yet draws the same marking as the tiles already on screen the moment it appears, which is what AC-095 will ask.
    - A tile marks with its own `marksWith`, or the dashboard's default where it has none; `''` is "(None)". The picker is T091.
    - Clicking reuses the cross-filter click path, with the modifier now carried: plain replaces, Ctrl or ⌘ toggles the one item (AC-087). A release that no item took is a click on empty plot area, which unmarks that tile's marking — observed on the wrapper's `mouseup`, because `pointerup` fires *before* ECharts dispatches and every click on a mark would otherwise look like a click on nothing.
    - The badge on the tile and the chip on the bar name the marking and its count in words (AC-098); the change is announced politely with its count and the tile it came from (AC-101).
    - `useThemeName` was added beside `useTheme`: a marking's colour is a value ECharts paints with, and a surface rendered without a provider should still draw rather than fail over a colour it could default.
    - **Tests:** `charts/src/marking/option.test.ts` (own colour kept, marking-coloured outline, unmarked faded, by series where a dimension splits them, nothing when the marking is empty) and `e2e/markings.spec.ts` on a real two-tile dashboard: a click marks and fades the rest, the outline appears, the badge and chip say so, the announcement carries the count, a plain click replaces while Ctrl-click adds and removes, and a click on empty plot area unmarks.
    - **Ticked here: AC-087, AC-096, AC-098, AC-101.** AC-094 waits for Escape on a focused tile (its other half, with the keyboard work); AC-095 for a second tile using the same marking across page tabs.
  - **Stage 2, 2026-09-23: the tile's Marking setting (T091), and across page tabs.**
    - The marking is read at render from one place, so a tile on a page tab that was never open while the marking changed draws it the moment that tab is opened — which is AC-095's failing state, and the reason the state is held for the dashboard rather than pushed to tiles.
    - **Ticked here: AC-085, AC-095**, both on `e2e/markings.spec.ts`: a marking made on one page showing on a tile of a page opened afterwards, and the Marking setting on a real tile including "(None)", which then marks nothing.
  - **Stage 3, 2026-09-23: the legend, and a table's rows.**
    - **A legend entry marks its whole category** rather than hiding the series (AC-090), and Ctrl there *adds* a category where Ctrl on an item toggles one — the criteria differ, and Spotfire behaves the same way round.
    - **Read from the DOM, not from ECharts' legend events.** `selectedMode: false` is what stops a legend click hiding a series, and with it no legend mouse event arrived; a marking gesture that sometimes does nothing is worse than none. A release no mark took is matched against what the legend lists — the spec's own series names — and is otherwise a click on empty plot area.
    - **A table's rows mark, and Shift extends from the anchor** (AC-092). `DataTable` has had the behaviour since T098 and no caller ever passed it a marking; it does now. Rows are markable only where the cube declares a primary key, which `primaryKeyOf` reads from `/v1/meta` — a column that happens to be unique in one answer is not an identity. The lookup is done where the rest of the model is read, in `DashboardPage`, rather than querying again inside the dashboard.
    - **Tests:** `markings/primaryKey.test.ts` (the key, a cube without one, a hidden key, and no model yet) and `e2e/markings.spec.ts` — a legend click marking a category without changing how many marks are drawn, Ctrl adding a second, a row click marking one row and Shift-click marking the range.
    - **Ticked here: AC-090, AC-092.**
  - **Stage 4, 2026-09-23: trellis panel headers (AC-091).**
    - A panel's header marks every item in that panel: the facet value alone, which is what makes "every item" true whatever else varies inside it. The titles already carried `triggerEvent` for this, from T195.
    - **The panel is taken by its index, not by the words shown.** A panel of time is titled by its granularity ("Jan 2026"), so the title is not the value; the index is matched against the facet's own values in the order the panels were drawn.
    - **Tests:** `e2e/markings.spec.ts` — a header click marking the panel's category and fading the other panel's bars. The fixture names the facet in the chart's `encoding`, because two dimensions alone compile to an axis and a colour split, never to panels.
    - **Ticked here: AC-091.**
  - **Stage 5, 2026-09-23: the keyboard (AC-100), which completes AC-094.**
    - A tile is a tab stop in view mode as well, and answers the marking's keys there: arrows move between its items, Space marks the focused one, Ctrl and Space toggle it, Shift and an arrow extend from the anchor, and Escape unmarks the tile's marking. The same tab stop as edit mode, a different vocabulary, because arranging and reading are different things to be doing — and the tile's accessible name says which keys it answers.
    - **Arrows do not mark.** Every marking change re-queries each tile using it, so "select as you move" would fire a round of queries per keystroke; the move is announced instead ("shipped, 2 of 3. Space marks."), since nothing on screen says where the keyboard is.
    - An extend goes through `addItemsWithinLimit`, so a long Shift-extend stops at the 1,000-item cap and says so rather than refusing the gesture.
    - **Tests:** `e2e/markings.spec.ts` drives a focused tile with the keyboard alone — arrow announcing without marking, Space marking, Shift extending, Ctrl+Space toggling back, Escape unmarking.
    - **Ticked here: AC-100, and AC-094**, whose empty-plot-area half landed in stage 1.
  - **Stage 6, 2026-09-23: a tile that cannot reach the marking (AC-103).**
    - Such a tile is left unmarked and says so, rather than drawing every item faded — which would read as "none of it is in here", a different and wrong answer — and rather than sending a query Cube would refuse.
    - **This needed the model to be decisive, and it was not.** `reachFrom` only ruled a pair out when their join components differed, and a cube with no joins reports no component at all, so the answer was "cannot say" and nothing was labelled. Verified against the local Cube v1.7.42: `orders`, `products` and `customers` report `connectedComponent: 1`; `monthly_orders`, which declares no joins, reports none. `markings-query-support.md` §4.5 had this as a source reading marked UNVERIFIED; it is now verified there, with the probe.
    - **Absence counts only where the model reports components at all.** A model that says nothing about them — a different Cube version, or one read before the joins compiled — is telling us nothing, and reading that as "every cube is isolated" would stop every filter reaching every tile. That case stays "cannot say", and has its own test.
    - The same rule now spares dashboard filters a doomed query too (AC-079): a tile that cannot be reached is marked as such before anything is sent.
    - **Tests:** `filters/coverage.test.ts` (a join-less cube ruled out, its own members still its own, a joined pair unaffected, and silence everywhere still "cannot say") and `e2e/markings.spec.ts` — a tile on `monthly_orders` labelled not linked, unmarked, nothing faded, and no error on it.
    - **Ticked here: AC-103.**
  - **Why this task exists.** T086–T119 each built and tested one module, and every one of their Files lists names only that module and its test — no renderer, no e2e spec. The F5–F9 dashboard tasks by contrast each list `DashboardView.tsx` and `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`. So the plan never contained the step that makes marking reachable, and 18 criteria were ticked on unit tests of code nothing imports. Unticked 2026-09-20; see the status note in `docs/spec.md` under F10.
  - **The lesson worth keeping, independent of markings.** A criterion whose `Fails when:` names an observation on a tile is not demonstrated by a test that renders a component with hand-passed props. When planning any future feature, the task that introduces a UI module must also name the renderer that mounts it, or the feature is finished everywhere except where the user is.
  - Known interface hazard for whoever does this: `allLimitsEmpty(limitingMarkings(xs))` is false for every input, because `limitingMarkings` keeps only the non-empty markings. Pinned by a test in `NothingMarked.test.tsx`.

## Spec F22. Following the design prototype's structure

Planned 2026-09-24 from the critiques in `docs/design/critiques/2026-09-24-*.md` (R9: the user chose to do this before more features, then resume the handoff order). Every task here is surface work, so every one names the renderer and an e2e spec — the lesson of T190 above. The prototype is served for comparison with `python3 -m http.server 5500` in `docs/design/prototype/`.

Order: the reopened criteria first (they are ticked-and-false, the worst state to leave), then Explore and the shell, then dashboards.

- [x] T208 (AC-042) Offer Retry and details on a dashboard tile's error state, and never report a failed query as a missing chart
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/chartSpec.ts`, `packages/web/src/query/loadTile.ts`, `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`: a tile whose query fails shows its title, Retry and a details disclosure; Retry asks again and draws the chart (AC-042).
  - Depends on: none
  - Done 2026-09-24. AC-042 re-ticked on four e2e tests in `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`, all passing in Chrome, Firefox and WebKit: the chart-shaped skeleton while queries are held; a 500 from Cube giving the title, the message, Show query and a Retry that draws the chart; a dropped network during a refresh giving a retryable error rather than "no longer available", and Retry recovering it; and a 404 giving the missing-chart state with no Retry.
  - **Two causes, both found by reading what the critique saw.** `TileBody` rendered `<TileError error={error} />` and never passed `onRetry` — the component test passed the prop, so it was green while no dashboard ever showed the button. And `specFor` caught *every* failure to fetch the chart document as "This chart is no longer available", so a dropped network read as a deleted chart. It now separates a 404 (missing) from a network failure or other HTTP status (a failed load).
  - **The cold review found four more, all fixed.** The title vanished in the error state, which the task itself required — the tile now keeps its heading. A deleted chart received a Retry that could only fail — `CHART_MISSING` is now one shared value and the tile renders the missing state for it, with no Retry, as it does for `CHART_UNTYPED`. A network failure at `/api/load` was a bare `TypeError` with nothing behind "details" — `loadTile` now wraps it in a `TileLoadError` carrying the query, leaving a cancellation a cancellation. And Retry showed in edit mode, where Refresh is hidden because a drafted tile's re-read would drop the draft.
  - **Recorded, not fixed:** a 200 response whose body will not parse is reported as "could not reach the server". A wrong message, not a wrong state; left for the next time `getJson` is touched.
- [x] T209 (AC-053) Show the dashboard's tree in the Outline tab, a row selecting and scrolling to its element
  - Files: `packages/web/src/dashboard/edit/Outline.tsx`, `packages/web/src/dashboard/DashboardView.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`, `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`: the Outline lists page tabs, then their items with sections and tab groups nested, and clicking a row selects that element (AC-053).
  - Depends on: none
  - Done 2026-09-24. AC-053 re-ticked on `e2e/dashboard.spec.ts` "the Outline shows the dashboard as a tree, and a row selects its element", in Chrome, Firefox and WebKit. It names every element of the seeded dashboard in order, by chart title or text; checks a section's chart is inside the section's item; and selects an element further down (and brings it into view), one on the page tab that is not open, and one in a tab group's tab that is not showing.
  - **The cold review found three real problems, all fixed.** A row in a tab group's hidden tab selected an element with no box on the canvas — the tab group kept its open tab to itself — so it now follows a selection into the tab that holds it, once per new selection so a hand switch still works. The first version used ARIA tree roles without the tree's keyboard pattern, which promises arrow keys and expansion to exactly the people who rely on them; it is now nested lists of buttons, whose depth is announced and which promise nothing extra. And the test checked names by regex with `.first()`, not scrolling, and not the hidden tab; it checks all three now.
  - The prototype's kind icons stay, decorative; each row's name leads with the kind in words ("Chart: …"), which is what a screen reader hears in the icon's place.
- [x] T210 (AC-005, AC-211) Draw charts in the dark theme's palette and tokens, and bring chart text and the open palette into the contrast audit
  - Files: `packages/charts/src/themed.ts`, `packages/charts/src/themed.test.ts`, `packages/charts/src/index.ts`, `packages/web/src/tiles/ChartCanvas.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`, `e2e/a11y-tokens.spec.ts`, `e2e/package.json`
  - Failing test: `e2e/a11y-tokens.spec.ts`: in the dark theme, every SVG `<text>` in a chart and every result in the open palette meets its contrast threshold, and a chart's series use the dark palette (AC-005, AC-211).
  - Depends on: none
  - Done 2026-09-24. AC-005 and AC-211 ticked on `e2e/a11y-tokens.spec.ts`, all three browsers: every chart's SVG text on the seeded dashboard meets 4.5:1 against the tile it sits on in dark (composited for alpha and opacity, and only once every chart has drawn); a bar's slot-*n* light colour becomes exactly slot *n* of the dark ramp on switching the theme, without a reload; a pie, a heatmap and a trellis in dark draw no light categorical colour and no text under AA; the open palette's results pass axe in both themes; Security's dashboard links pass in dark.
  - **Why the audit missed it:** axe's colour-contrast rule does not measure text inside an SVG, and the palette was audited closed. Chart text is now measured directly, by `CHART_TEXT_CONTRAST`.
  - **`themed(option, theme)`** is applied last, in `ChartCanvas` — the one place every ECharts surface goes through — so no renderer has to know the theme. Categorical colours swap slot for slot, so a member keeps its place in the order (AC-039); "Other" takes direction.md's dark neutral; text, lines and the tooltip take the tokens. The light theme takes its own tokens too, rather than ECharts' defaults.
  - **The sequential ramp is reversed in dark, not recoloured.** direction.md publishes one ramp, light to dark, whose high end sank into the dark tile at about 1.4:1. Its two published ends the other way round keep the ramp's rule — magnitude reads as contrast against the surface — with no colour invented. A decision taken here, recorded so it can be overturned; a dark ramp of its own would be direction.md's to publish.
  - **The cold review found five real problems, all fixed.** The global link rule `a:visited` outranked a Primary link tile's own colour and would have drawn its text in accent on its accent fill — now `:where()`, with no specificity. Gridlines a user turns on kept a hard-coded light colour, and "Other" its light grey. Tooltips were ECharts' white box. And the recolouring swapped *any* string equal to a palette hex, so a data value that was a colour would have been renamed on the axis and in the name a click marks by — it now touches only colour keys. The e2e also got stronger: the exact dark slot, alpha and opacity, every chart rather than the first, and chart types beyond bars.
- [x] T211 (AC-190, AC-191) Put the search field and avatar in the top bar, and make the palette search fields and model files with arrow-key results
  - Files: `packages/web/src/shell/AppShell.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/SearchPalette.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/SearchPalette.test.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/globalSearch.ts`, `packages/web/src/shell/globalSearch.test.ts`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`, `packages/api/src/server.ts`, `packages/api/src/server.test.ts`, `e2e/search-palette.spec.ts`, `e2e/search.spec.ts`, `e2e/a11y-tokens.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `e2e/search-palette.spec.ts`: clicking the top-bar field opens the palette; a field name and a model file are found with their types; ArrowDown then Enter opens the second result; Escape returns focus (AC-190, AC-191).
  - Depends on: T210
  - Done 2026-09-24. AC-190 and AC-191 ticked on `e2e/search-palette.spec.ts`, all three browsers: the top-bar field names ⌘K or Ctrl K, opens the palette and takes focus back on Escape; the avatar is at the far right, "AD" for the API's single user, and present even when `/api/me` fails; a model file and a field are found with their types; ArrowDown, ArrowDown, ArrowUp move the active result, which stays in view; Enter opens it where it lives; focus returns after a second ⌘K and after Close.
  - **Scope is the user's (R9):** dashboards, charts, Cube fields and model files. `globalResults` is a pure function over the catalog's search answer, `/api/meta` and `/api/model/files`, which the page already caches; a field opens the model file that defines its cube, found by the Model workspace's own parser.
  - **A combobox over a listbox**, the WAI-ARIA search-with-results pattern: focus stays in the input, `aria-activedescendant` names the active option. The input's role changed from searchbox to combobox, so the tests that looked it up by role changed with it — one for one, no assertion loosened.
  - **`/api/me`** names phase 1's single user from `SINGLE_USER`, the same constant every new document's owner comes from, so the avatar cannot disagree with Explore's Owner column.
  - **The cold review found five real problems, all fixed.** Fields were capped at twenty in silence — AC-191's own failing state — so every match is listed and the list scrolls; the catalog half now asks for up to 1,001 and says when a search is broader. A second ⌘K while open recorded the palette's own input as the opener, so closing sent focus nowhere. The avatar was missing until `/api/me` answered, and for good if it failed. The active result was tracked by position while results arrived from three sources, so it could slide onto another result before Enter — it is kept by key now. And the active row could scroll out of sight.

AC-019 and AC-192 ticked on e2e in Chrome, Firefox and WebKit: `e2e/search.spec.ts` "lists every dashboard and library chart whose title contains the text, and no local chart" creates a dashboard, a library chart and a local chart sharing one word and asserts the box lists exactly the first two; `e2e/explore.spec.ts` covers the heading, a breadcrumb ancestor opening its folder, the New menu as the only way to create, and Rename in place.

AC-020's tests moved onto the menu and got stronger — each now finds the created item in its folder by id, where the chart test used to check only the URL. The old "refuses to create without a name" guarded a form this design removes; its successor asserts that a folder renamed to nothing becomes "Untitled folder".

The search stopped at 50 rows in silence — the palette's cap, harmless there — so `/api/search` now takes a bounded `limit` (1–5,000) and the box asks for 1,001, lists 1,000, and says so when a search is broader. The clearing test passed as long as the heading was not "Search"; it now starts in Finance and must end there. While the folders loaded, the heading borrowed the root's name and offered Rename for a folder shown as "All folders"; it now waits. A failed rename was silent; it now says so.

    - a policy applies when a group matches and its conditions hold;
    - `member_level: {}` and no `member_level` grant every member;
    - members are unioned, and full access wins over masking;
    - a view's rows are also filtered by its cubes' policies, and a cube whose policies none apply gives the view no rows.

- [x] T254 (AC-187) Draw a chart's text in Inter with tabular numerals, and its plot down to the tile's foot, as the prototype does
  - Files: `packages/charts/src/{themed,optionFor}.ts` (+ tests), `packages/charts/src/layout/grid.ts`, `packages/charts/src/series/{bar,combo}.ts`, `packages/web/src/tiles/ChartCanvas.tsx`, `packages/web/src/shell/styles.ts`, `docs/research/echarts-text-and-grid.md`, `docs/research/echarts-bar-density.md`, `e2e/design-conformance.spec.ts`, `e2e/{markings,crossfilter}.spec.ts`
  - Failing test: `e2e/design-conformance.spec.ts` "T254: …": every SVG text is Inter, with tabular numerals; the lowest label is no more than 12 px above the chart's foot (the prototype's `grid: { bottom: 8, containLabel: true }`) (recheck-dashboard N7, F9; mismatches 12, 13).
  - Depends on: `docs/research/echarts-text-and-grid.md` (ECharts 6.1's global `textStyle`, how its SVG writes text, and v6's grid label bounds)
  - Done 2026-09-24, all three browsers. The e2e failed first, and so did `themed.test.ts` "a chart's text" and `optionFor.test.ts` "a chart on axes".
    - **The chart's text is Inter.** It is set through the option's root `textStyle`, which reaches every text wechart draws; ECharts writes the family inline, so page CSS can't. The tooltip is the prototype's 12 px.
    - **Tabular numerals come from page CSS on the chart**, as ECharts has no option for them. Most of its text inherits them. The few written with the `font` shorthand get them through an `!important` rule, and the tooltip through its `extraCssText`.
    - **One limit, recorded in the research:** zrender measures text with a canvas, which has no tabular figures. The labels are laid out by proportional digit widths and drawn tabular, and Inter's digits differ little.
    - **The grid is the prototype's `{ left: 8, right: 28, top: 12, bottom: 8 }`**, in ECharts 6's supported terms: `outerBoundsMode: 'same'` with `outerBoundsContain: 'all'`. Axis titles stay inside the 8 px bottom too. `containLabel` is deprecated, and wechart's build doesn't register the legacy algorithm the prototype's full bundle ran. The legend's room and the Customize tab's placement still win.
    - **Bars and combos carry the grid themselves**, so a bar's estimated width is measured against the grid it is drawn on: `width − 36` along it, `height − 20` down a horizontal chart. `echarts-bar-density.md` says so.
    - **Found by the full run:**
      - The markings and cross-filter e2e helpers clicked a bar at Playwright's `boundingBox()` of its axis label.
      - In WebKit that box sits half the label's width to the right for middle-anchored SVG text. With the wider plot, the click fell in the gap between two bars.
      - Both helpers now measure with the element's own client rect, and wait for it to settle.
